# Changelog

## Unreleased

### opacity
- **Opacity of ink, pictures and areas (#358) and document format 24.** An *Opacity* slider (5 % to 100 %) in the pen options (the pen and its kinds, the shapes) and in the highlighter options (strokes and the area highlighter, with a value of its own): what you write next is that strong. It multiplies the pen's own look, so 100 % is exactly what every pen did before and the highlighter at 50 % is half of its usual translucency, still multiplied over the page. A favourite pen and a pen set keep it like the width, and the style eyedropper takes it from a stroke.
- **Change what is already there.** The selection bar has the same slider for the ink, the pictures and the area highlighters you selected: one step in the history (*Opacity changed*), undone in one go; groups come whole, a locked item or a text box is left alone, a mixed selection shows a dash until you move it, and the little button puts it back to 100 %. A selection of only a picture now has its bar too.
- **Everywhere you see the page.** The canvas, the stroke being written (also the wet ink of the pen, the highlighter and the marker, without a flash when it settles), the night look, PNG, SVG, PDF (one graphics state per strength, multiplied for the highlighter), the interactive HTML, the written-area and copy-as-image pictures, library cards, the page navigator and the hand-in PDF draw the same strength. A notebook that never uses it is saved exactly as before.
- **Document format v24.** One more optional number, `opacity` (0.05 to 1), on a stroke of any pen, an image and an area, written only below 1. The v23 to v24 migration is the identity plus the version (a stray key of a v23 file is dropped); lenient reading clamps a number out of range and drops anything else, counted; strict writing refuses both; repair keeps a valid one; large v23 files are still read page by page. Older versions of the plugin open a v24 notebook read-only. The JSON Schema is now `docs/schema/notebook-v24.schema.json`.
- **Also.** The *Opacity* action (all-actions sheet, ranked first with a selection of ink, pictures or areas and with the highlighter or a shape in hand, long-press explanations in English and Italian); *Join broken strokes* and the hook clean-up treat strokes of different opacity as different styles; merge keeps both copies of a stroke whose opacity differs.
- **Code.** `src/model/opacity.ts` (the limits and helpers), `src/ink/restyle.ts` (`planOpacity`, one `UpdateItems` step), `src/view/opacity-row.ts` (the shared slider row), `inkAlpha(tool, opacity)` in `src/ink/stroke-shape.ts` as the one description of a stroke's alpha; tests in `tests/model-v24.test.ts`; harness scenario `doc=opacity`.

### tidy
- **Tidy up (#238).** One step that straightens a page of handwriting: the words onto their baseline, the starts of the lines onto a common left margin, and oversized gaps between the lines closed. It works on the selection, or on the whole page when nothing is selected (*Tidy up page* in the page menu and the All actions sheet, *Tidy up* on the second row of the selection bar from tablet width, command *Tidy up the selection or the page*). A small sheet lets you switch each step on or off, shows how many words, lines and gaps would change and how much shorter the writing gets, and does nothing until *Tidy up*; *Cancel* leaves the page as it is. The choices are remembered on the device.
- **Rules.** The words are moved by the existing align-to-baseline engine; the lines are the ones it finds. Line starts within about two letter heights of each other become one margin (an indent stays an indent; on ruled paper the paper's margin line is used when the writing is next to it). A gap of two blank lines or more shrinks to one blank line, so paragraph breaks stay (switch *Keep paragraph breaks* off to close every gap to the usual line spacing). On ruled paper a line only moves by whole rules. Lines never end up on top of each other.
- **Safe.** Rigid moves of line-pen handwriting only (same points, pressure, width, id and layer). Shapes, the highlighter and the marker, pictures, text boxes, areas and every other element are never moved; neither are locked elements and the groups they belong to, a group that is not whole, ink a collapsed note keeps hidden, ink bound to a graph, and a line that has an underline, a drawing or an object in its way. A locked page refuses the step. One undo step ("Page tidied up" in the history panel); the page times are not touched. No format change.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested `src/ink/tidy.ts` (reuses `analyseBaseline`, which now also hands over the lines it found, `selectableItems`, `applyPlacement`); the sheet `src/view/tidy-modal.ts`; `tidy()` in `src/view/selection-actions.ts`; registry actions `selection-tidy` and `page-tidy` (help text, context ranking, history title in English and Italian).
- **Tests.** `tests/tidy.test.ts` (two paragraphs, crooked lines, big gaps, ruled paper, a locked box in the middle, groups, hidden ink, one undo, page times, registry); `tests/selection-arrange.test.ts` (one id added to the second row). Not tried with a real hand or on Android.

### smooth
- **Smooth strokes and Straighten strokes (#66).** Two actions in the All actions sheet (Selection) and the command palette work on the handwriting of the selection, each as ONE undo step with a notice that counts the strokes (history titles *Strokes smoothed* / *Strokes straightened*). *Smooth* takes the tremor out of the lines: a few Taubin passes (a smoothing step and a slightly larger step back, so curves do not shrink) with sharp corners held in place, no point moved farther than about a pen width, so the shape stays and only the wobble goes. *Straighten* turns a stroke that is almost a line (bend under 7% of its length, no loops, no doubling back) into a clean line between its two ends, each point keeping its share of the way; curves and short marks are left alone, and a second run does nothing.
- **Safe.** Strokes keep their id, layer, style, number of points and the pressure of every point; the first and last point never move. Shapes, the highlighter and the marker, invisible ink, ink bound to a graph, the ink a collapsed sticky note keeps hidden, text, pictures and area highlights are not touched; a group is reshaped only when wholly selected and without a locked element; a locked page refuses; the page times do not move. No format change; the stroke pipeline, the renderer and the golden render hashes are untouched.
- **Code.** Pure module `src/ink/smooth.ts` (no `obsidian` import), `SelectionTools.reshape` in `src/view/selection-actions.ts`, registry entries `selection-smooth` and `selection-straighten` with long-press help, a context-ranking rule and English and Italian texts. Tests: `tests/smooth.test.ts`, `tests/selection-arrange.test.ts`; harness scenario `doc=smooth`.

### redact
- **Redaction before export (#285).** The export dialog has a new row, *Cover parts of the page*, with a *Redact…* button. It opens a page preview where you drag boxes (pen or mouse; a finger too while *Draw with finger* is on, otherwise a finger scrolls) over names, numbers or anything else that must not leave the notebook. The picture is exactly what the file will contain. A box can be removed with its cross, the last one undone, a page or everything cleared. Boxes are kept per notebook on this device only (bounded local storage; never in the notebook, the vault or sync) and applied by a switch, *Apply the boxes*, that turns on when you draw.
- **Covered content is removed, not painted over.** Ink is cut at a box (the parts outside stay, and so does the thickness of the pen: nothing of a line within its own width of a box is kept); a text box, picture, equation, graph, structured box, sticky note, marker, checkbox or excerpt that touches a box is dropped whole, with its text and its source; an area highlighter is cut round the box; margin comments anchored on a box go with their replies; the page header or footer is left out when a box covers it; bookmark titles of a covered page are not written to Markdown. The page's tags and drawing states are not drawn by any export and are removed from the copy. The box itself is a solid black rectangle drawn over everything.
- **Backgrounds.** A PDF page or a picture behind a page with boxes becomes a flat image of its own with the boxes burned in before it is encoded (a copy: the cached page is not touched), in the PDF (the original PDF text layer was never in the exported file; the burned picture is the only one), SVG, HTML, PNG, print, Markdown, share and hand-in alike, and also in a *Background only* export where no ink layer would show the box. Trade-off: that page's background can no longer be selected or searched as text. A background that cannot be burned is left out, never drawn as it is.
- **Where it applies.** Export… (PDF, SVG, interactive HTML, PNG images, Print, page ranges, split into sheets, clean copy), *Hand in…*, *Share page…*, *Export as Markdown*, the written-area / selection picture and *Copy as image*, the quick page exports, and the pictures put into notes by *Insert selection into note*, *Make task*, *Look up later* and the figures list, the figures' *Copy image*, the Highlights digest and the colour-meaning digest (neither quotes a PDF word a box touches), the book index, the formula sheet, and the public API's `exportPdf`. In the dialog the switch decides; the other paths apply the notebook's boxes while the switch is on and say so in a notice (the pictures put into notes apply them without one); with boxes switched off they say so once. Boxes that cannot be read refuse every one of these paths with a notice: nothing is written without them. Dropped content is not loaded at all (a covered picture is never read).
- **Not covered, on purpose.** The text digest of PDF annotations (it quotes the PDF's own text), *Extract pages* / merge / split notebooks, the automatic PDF copy (a private copy of the whole notebook), the notebook file itself and its backups, the companion note, Zettel inbox cards, page embeds in notes (they show the live page), PDF text you copy yourself, regions taken into another notebook with *Copy as excerpt* (that notebook's exports do not read these boxes), and the file title (the notebook's name, also in the PDF title). The export dialog lists them under the switch. Margin comments away from a box are kept (use *Clean copy* to leave all of them out), and ink written over a dropped picture stays where it was.
- **Code.** Pure and Node-tested: `src/model/redact.ts` (boxes, limits, lenient reader), `src/ink/redact.ts` (`redactPage`, `cutStroke`, `burnPixelRects`); the boxes ride on the copy of the page (a symbol-keyed field that survives a shallow copy and is never serialised), so every writer, and every future one, draws the redacted page. View: `src/view/export-redact.ts` (`withRedaction`, applied by each writer on its own), `src/view/redact-dialog.ts`, `src/view/burn.ts`, `src/ui/redact-store.ts`. No format change, no change to the stroke pipeline.
- **Tests.** `tests/redact.test.ts`, `tests/redact-export.test.ts` (a SECRET marker in text, notes, equations, box titles, comments and a scanned background must not appear anywhere in the bytes of the PDF, SVG, HTML, hand-in PDF, area picture and Markdown), `tests/redact-store.test.ts`; harness `doc=redact` (real canvas: PNG, print/Markdown/share renderer, JPEG burn, area picture, the editor and the dialog row).

### zoomwidth
- **Pen width follows zoom (#350).** New setting *Writing > Pen width follows zoom* (off by default, so nothing changes). On: a new stroke looks as wide on screen as the width you chose at any zoom; the stored width is the chosen width divided by the zoom, clamped to 0.1 to 48 page units (96 for the highlighter). Pens, shapes and the stroke highlighter follow the rule; the area highlighter and the eraser do not. The live ink and the saved stroke have the same width. The pen options show the on-screen width with a "× zoom" hint.
- **No format change.** Strokes stay plain page-unit widths; the golden pen hashes are unchanged. Code: `src/ink/zoom-width.ts` (pure helper), the stroke start in `notebook-view.ts`.

### ctxpalette
- **Context palette in the colour picker (#394).** Under the recent colours the picker now has up to two short rows. *On this page* lists the colours already used on the page in view (strokes of every pen, the highlighter and shapes, shape fills, text, markers, boxes, equations, graphs, checkboxes and areas), most used first, near shades counted as one. On a page with a PDF or picture behind it, *From the slide* lists its dominant colours (the paper white is left out), found from a small downsampled copy of the page picture (median cut refined by k-means), computed in a timer after the picker is shown, kept per page and never started for a page whose picture is not drawn yet. A colour near a recent colour, or near one already in the page row, is not repeated (ΔE below 8); a colour that has a meaning (colours with meaning) shows its label on the swatch. Tapping a swatch sets the pen colour as the toolbar swatches do and does not add it to the recent colours. A row with nothing to offer is not shown. Long-press explains each row; English and Italian; light and dark. No format change, no change to the input pipeline.
- **Code.** Pure module `src/ink/context-palette.ts` (no `obsidian` import; `tests/context-palette.test.ts`), the rows in `src/view/colour-picker.ts`, the sampling and cache in `src/view/colour-tools.ts`, the page context in `src/view/notebook-view.ts`; harness `doc=ctxpalette` (PDF page) and `doc=ctxpalettew` (written page).

### gallery
- **Dynamic gallery block (#421).** A `notebook-gallery` code block in a Markdown note shows pages of your notebooks as a responsive grid of thumbnails with notebook name and page number; tap one to open the notebook at that page. Lines: `tag: #x`, `bookmarked` (notebook and global bookmarks), `course: Analysis` (timetable), `notebook: path`, `marked: question|homework|…` (marker kinds and the marked-pages kinds), `limit: 12`, `sort: recent|title`. It works in reading view and Live Preview, refreshes (debounced, after the pen is quiet) when the notebooks change, shows a quiet empty state and a readable line for a bad query, and never writes to the vault. The command "Insert gallery block" has a small builder (tag, course, marker, bookmarked, count, order). English and Italian. No format change.
- **Code.** Pure `src/integration/gallery-query.ts` (tested in `tests/gallery-query.test.ts`), `gallery-service.ts` (in-memory index, no writes), `gallery-render.ts`, `gallery-builder.ts`; thumbnails reuse `PageThumbs`, which gained `data-page-thumb-index`.

### related
- **Related handwritten pages (#422).** The command *Related handwritten pages* (also in All actions) opens a panel in the right sidebar. For the Markdown note in front it lists the handwritten pages that relate to it, best first: pages whose text links to the note (`[[Note]]`), pages that name its title or an alias, pages tagged like the note, pages that name one of its headings (or a bookmark titled like one), and, last, the first page of the notebooks of the note's course. Each row has the page thumbnail, the notebook and page, a reason pill (*Links here*, *Title*, *Alias*, *Tag*, *Heading*, *Same course*) with the matched text, and `+n` when the page has more reasons.
- **Use.** Tap a row to open the page; drag a row into the note to place its embed; the link button copies the link. The panel follows the note you work in (a short delay after you switch notes or edit its headings, tags or aliases) and a refresh button reads the notebooks again. Empty states say what to do. Long-press explains the buttons (English and Italian).
- **Read-only.** It reads the library index and the note's metadata only; it never writes to the vault. No format change. A new setting *Related pages: same course* (Integration) turns the course rows off.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested ranking in `src/library/related-pages.ts` (no `obsidian` import); the panel in `src/view/related-view.ts`; what it reads from Obsidian in `src/view/related-source.ts` (reuses the page switcher's index and thumbnails). Tests: `tests/related-pages.test.ts`; harness `doc=related`.

### Review fixes
- **Opacity is kept where ink is made for you.** *Snap to text lines* gives each snapped highlighter band the opacity the highlighter was drawn with, instead of full strength. The stroke a pen gesture leaves as ink after one Undo, and the built-in stamps (check, star, arrow), use the opacity of the pen in hand.
- **Opacity above 1 in a file.** A value above 1 is read as full strength and saved with no `opacity` key at all (still counted as repaired), so the app never writes `"opacity":1`.
- **Tidy up.** A line never ends on top of another line that moves, whatever their horizontal overlap (a short line under the end of a longer one used to count as unrelated below 15 %), also when lines are moved sideways to a common margin. Invisible ink and the ink a collapsed note keeps hidden are in the way like any other ink: a line is not moved onto them.
- **Smooth and Straighten.** Reshaped points are rounded to 0.01 pt like every other edit, so a reshaped stroke takes no more room in the file than before.
- **Pen width follows zoom.** The stored width is rounded to 0.01.
- **Related pages.** Opening the panel or its refresh button no longer writes the library index file. The notebooks it reads stay in memory and are saved by the next page switcher refresh.
- Redaction: a text box is measured for the widest characters it could hold (capitals, CJK, emoji), so a long line of wide characters under a box is dropped instead of slipping past its edge.
- Redaction: an excerpt whose source region is covered on another page is left out of every export, also from a page that has no box of its own.
- Redaction: a page shown in a drawing state, or exported as a clean copy, keeps its boxes (the copy no longer loses the page id).
- Redaction: boxes that cannot be read (damaged storage, storage that refuses) refuse the export with a notice instead of exporting without them; the boxes of this session are held in memory and still apply when the device cannot store them; a notebook whose switch is on is never forgotten to make room, and a full store says so.
- Redaction: the Highlights digest and the colour-meaning digest leave out every PDF word a box touches, and the colour-meaning digest reads the redacted pages.
- Redaction: the public API's `exportPdf` applies the notebook's boxes while their switch is on and fails with `export-failed` when they cannot be read.
- Redaction: with boxes switched off, an export outside the dialog says so once.
- Redaction: the book index, the formula sheet, the figures' *Copy image*, the pictures sent to a checklist task and *Export as Markdown* go through the same redaction as the other exports.
- Redaction: the reach of a stroke follows the real width of its pen (pressure, fountain and brush nibs), a checkbox whose label a box covers keeps its box but loses the label, and the header handed to a note's page picture is the redacted one.
- Redaction: the dialog preview drops covered excerpts like the export, *Done* saves the boxes even when nothing changed (so an unreadable state is set again), and the dialog lists what redaction does not cover.
- Redaction: the figures' *Copy* refuses any picture or excerpt that an export of its page would leave out, including an excerpt whose source region is covered on another page and a picture just beside a box.
- Redaction: the page header or footer is measured like a text box (capitals, CJK and wide symbols at their widest), so a box over the end of a long header leaves the whole header out.
- Redaction: redaction storage that cannot be read, or that was written in another format, is kept aside in a separate bounded copy and never overwritten silently; one notice says so, and every notebook's exports are refused until its boxes are drawn again and saved with *Done*, or until *Reset redaction storage* (in the Redact dialog, after a confirmation) clears the boxes of every notebook.
- Redaction: the messages about unreadable boxes say that they cannot be restored and that *Done* saves exactly the boxes shown; closing the Redact dialog without *Done* no longer saves an empty set.
- Redaction: an excerpt of a region that shows a covered excerpt is left out too, at any depth (a chain of excerpts that show each other ends).
- Redaction: every character has a true upper bound for its width; scripts and symbols without a checked width (and the very wide ones such as ⸺, ⸻ and ﷽) count as the widest, so a text box of them is never measured too small.
- Redaction: while a notebook has any box, excerpts of its own pages are left out of every export and refused by the figures' *Copy*: their saved picture is never refreshed, so it may still show content that was moved and covered elsewhere.
- Redaction: the picture of a selection applies every box that touches a selected item measured as the export measures it (text at its widest), not only the boxes near the estimated area, so a box over the real last line of a long CJK or capitals text is not missed.
- Redaction: the not-covered list names *Copy as excerpt* into another notebook (that notebook's exports do not read these boxes).
- Redaction: an excerpt's source region is tested with the same pen reach as ink (settled by the excerpt rule above, which leaves it out whenever its source page has a box).
- Redaction: a stored set that would lose a page or a box when this version reads it is unreadable for that notebook, kept aside and told, never read with fewer boxes.
- Redaction: every distinct unreadable value or entry gets its own bounded copy before anything is written over it (at most six copies; when none can be kept, nothing is overwritten); the notice says whether a copy was kept or nothing was left to keep.
- Redaction: closing the Redact dialog with Escape or × never saves a set that is net unchanged, and nothing at all while the stored boxes could not be read (a draw and an undo keep the notebook refused until *Done*).
- Redaction: the per-character width bounds cover the widest glyphs of the fonts the exports and the UI can use, with a margin (ASCII symbols 0.9 em, capitals and `@ % & m w` 1.1 em, Latin letters 1.25 em, fractions, letterlike symbols, sub- and superscripts, technical signs and ligatures at their own bounds).
- Redaction: when the very first save of boxes on a device fails (a full quota), the boxes are held for the session with one notice, and the other notebooks are not refused.
- Related pages: entries kept by the read-only panel are never written over a newer entry of the library index (the newer read wins; entries of notebooks deleted since are not written).
- Document format: an `opacity` of exactly 1 stays as it is in a file and counts as full strength (documented).
- **A stroke or element with no colour no longer makes a notebook unsavable.** The writer refuses a missing colour (or any `undefined`) with a message naming the item, before any text is built; if such an item is already in memory, the save gives it a colour on the same object (a checkbox, sticky note, area or equation takes the default colour of its kind, a stroke or text box black) and saves, instead of failing on every later attempt; nothing is replaced, so undo and redo keep working. A committed (frozen) item, or any other damage, is still refused and the file is left alone. Found by a harness fixture that built a checkbox with no colour.

## 0.25.0 — 2026-11-08

Seven features and a first part of the history: an area highlighter, a panel of the actions you took, align to baseline, lecture and study writing profiles, copy as image, a toolbar that fades while you write, a suggested paper for a new page and a page navigator view of marked pages only. The notebook format moves to version 23 (the area highlighter): the migration from version 22 is automatic, and 0.24 and older open a v23 notebook read-only and never overwrite it. Two independent review rounds are folded in (see Review fixes).

### Area highlighter and document format 23
- **Area highlighter (#99) and document format 23.** The highlighter's options have *Stroke | Area*. In *Area* a drag from corner to corner puts a translucent rectangle on the page, in the highlighter colour, above the page background (a scanned PDF or a picture) and below all your ink and typed text, so what it covers stays readable. It looks exactly like highlighter ink (the same translucency, multiplied over the page; screened at night) and is dimmed by the colour-meaning filter like highlighter ink. It can be selected, moved, resized (the corner handle scales it in proportion; a quarter turn turns it, any other turn only moves it round, so it stays upright), recoloured with the highlighter colours in the selection bar, grouped, locked, copied, deleted and undone; the whole-stroke and segment eraser remove a whole area when they touch it and the same stroke of the pen has erased no ink (rubbing out a letter written on top of an area keeps the area; the *Pen* eraser filter spares it, the *Highlighter* filter always takes it) and the area eraser removes the ones inside its outline. A drag under 2 pt adds nothing. The action *Area highlighter* is in the all-actions sheet, ranked first while the highlighter is in hand and on PDF pages, with a long-press explanation.
- **Everywhere you see the page.** The canvas, the live drag preview, PNG, SVG (and so the interactive HTML and the written-area export), PDF, library cards, the page navigator and the hand-in PDF draw areas with the same look and order.
- **Document format v23.** One more element type, `area` (`x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `color`, plus the usual `group`, `locked`, `lockAt`). The v22 to v23 migration is the identity plus the version; lenient reading drops and counts an area with an invalid rectangle or colour, strict writing refuses it; repair keeps a valid one; older plugins open a v23 notebook read-only. `docs/schema/notebook-v22.schema.json` is now `notebook-v23.schema.json`; `docs/format.md`, the architecture notes (why an element rather than a stroke flag) and `tests/format-schema.test.ts` are updated. The stroke pipeline and the golden render hashes are untouched.
- **Code.** `src/model/area.ts` (geometry), `src/ink/area-render.ts` (the one description of the look), `src/ink/area-erase.ts`; the drag reuses the shape tool's drag in `src/view/notebook-view.ts`; tests in `tests/model-v23.test.ts`.

### Action history panel
- **Action history panel (#84, panel only).** *Action history…* (all-actions sheet, notebook menu, command palette) lists the steps of the open notebook newest first: a readable title per step ("Stroke written", "Added: text box", "Selection moved or transformed", "Erased"…), its page, how long ago, and how many items it touched. The point the document is at is highlighted (*Now*); undone steps are listed dimmed above it, and *Start of the history* is the last row. Tapping a step goes back, or forward, to the point just after it: every step is an ordinary History Undo or Redo, so the guards apply to each one (a locked page, a change the writer would reject, read-only mode); when a step is refused the jump stops there and a notice says how far it got. Long-press help in English and Italian; ranked higher in the actions sheet once steps were undone.
- **Code.** `History` records the time of each step and exposes `entries()`, `depth()` and `stepsTo()` (no change of behaviour); the pure titles are in `src/model/history-describe.ts` (every command class, every label the code uses, a generic fallback for an unknown command), the jump in `src/model/history-jump.ts`, the list model in `src/view/history-panel-logic.ts`, the dialog on the modal shell in `src/view/history-panel.ts`. No document format change.
- **Not in this release: undo that survives reopening.** The history is still emptied when the notebook is closed. Commands hold element objects by identity and private apply state, so saving them safely needs a serialiser per command class, re-linking to the freshly read notebook and a verified dry run, and the history would have to be saved at idle saves because Android can end the app without a close event. Roadmap #84 stays open.

### Align to baseline
- **Align to baseline (#93).** Select handwriting and tap *Align to baseline* (second row of the selection bar on tablet widths, the All actions sheet everywhere, command *Align the words of the selection to a common baseline*): the words of each line are moved straight up or down onto one common baseline, in one undo step, with a notice that counts the words. On ruled paper the words go onto the ruled line instead. A line that is clearly written uphill or downhill keeps its slope and loses only the wobble.
- **How it reads the writing.** Words are found from the gaps between strokes (the dot of an i, a comma or a full stop go with their word), lines from the baselines, and the baseline of a word is where most letters sit, not the lowest ink: a g, p, y or q keeps its tail below the line, and the bar of a T or an f does not pull the baseline up. Everything is relative to the size of the hand, so large and small writing work alike.
- **Safe.** Strokes only translate in y (same x, pressure, width, style, id and layer; nothing is rotated, scaled or redrawn). Underlines, drawings, shapes, the highlighter and the marker, invisible ink, ink bound to a graph and the ink a collapsed sticky note keeps hidden are left where they are. A group moves as one unit, and a group that is not wholly selected or that holds a locked element stays put. A word that would have to move more than twice the height of the writing is left alone (it is another line). A move under a quarter of a point is not made, so a second run does nothing. The page times do not move (it is a move, not writing); a locked page refuses it.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested module `src/ink/baseline.ts` (no `obsidian` import; reuses `applyPlacement`); the action in `src/view/selection-actions.ts` (`alignBaseline`) and the registry (`selection-baseline`), with a long-press explanation, a context-ranking rule (higher on ruled paper) and English and Italian texts. No format change. On a phone-width bar (five places) the older actions keep their places and this one is in the sheet.
- **Tests.** `tests/baseline.test.ts` (synthetic words with descenders, punctuation, two lines, ruled paper, slopes, groups, locks, pinned ink, undo, page times, a 20-line page), and new cases in `tests/selection-arrange.test.ts`, `tests/context-rank.test.ts` and `tests/long-press-help.test.ts`.

### Lecture and study profiles
- **Writing profiles (#219).** A chip on the pen options (and the commands "Writing profile: Lecture / Study / next", also in All actions) switches the pens on the strip, the stabilization and the paper that new notebooks start on in one tap. *Lecture* has thin fast pens, light smoothing and ruled paper; *Study* has fountain pens, strong smoothing and grid paper. "Save current setup as a profile" keeps your own (up to 12; update or delete from the same menu). A pen set can be referenced by a profile; otherwise its pens become a normal pen set. The chip shows the profile only while the smoothing and paper still match it. A setting under Writing switches to Lecture when lecture mode starts (off by default). Switching changes tools and defaults only: no stroke, page or document is touched; no format change. English and Italian; long-press help and context ranking (Lecture first during a class). Not done: the optional toolbar density per profile.

### Copy as image
- **Copy as image (#277).** *Copy as image* puts the selection, or the written area of the page, on the system clipboard as a picture to paste into messaging, mail and other apps. It is the last button of the selection bar's second row (from tablet width; on a phone bar it is in the All actions sheet) and *Copy written area as image* is in the page menu and the sheet. The picture is the transparent area export drawn at 2x, with a pixel cap for very large areas, painted onto white by default (new setting *Background of copied pictures*: White or Transparent, for apps that show transparency as black).
- **Never silent.** Where the WebView has no image clipboard the PNG goes to the system share sheet; where that is missing too, a PNG is saved next to the notebook (recorded as written by the plugin). Every outcome shows a notice, and closing the share sheet is not an error. No format change.
- **Code.** Pure decisions (size cap, routes, fallback order) in `src/ink/clip-image.ts`; drawing and delivery in `src/view/copy-image.ts`, reusing the area export's plan and renderer; English and Italian; long-press help; harness `doc=clipimage`.

### Toolbar fades while writing
- **Toolbar fades while writing and returns on the first touch (#399).** *Settings > Appearance > Toolbar while writing > Fade while writing* (default: always shown, so nothing moves for anyone who did not ask): once the pen has been writing for the *Fade delay* (1 to 6 s, default 2), the toolbar, the contextual bar and the floating pills (recent symbols, back-to-page, referenced-on, welcome-back, lecture header) dim to 15% and come back that many seconds after the last stroke. A finger touch outside the page, a pen hovering over a bar or a press on a bar brings them back at once; the first finger touch on a dimmed bar only restores it and does not press the button (switch *First touch only brings the bars back* off to press at once). A long press on a dimmed bar restores it and shows the icon help. A selection, the lasso or an open dialog never lets the bars fade. Opacity transition only (no layout shift, the bars stay hit-testable), none under reduced motion; one class is toggled by the existing stroke start and end events, with one timer and no per-frame work. Pure state machine in `src/ui/tool-fade.ts` (`tests/tool-fade.test.ts`), DOM part in `src/view/tool-fade.ts`; English and Italian.

### Suggested paper for a new page
- **Suggested paper for a new page (#409).** The **+** strip highlights the paper the notebook and the course point to, with a short reason on the chip: *Like the last 5 pages* (a run of one paper), *Like most of the last 6 pages*, *Alternating, like the last pages* (ruled, grid, ruled, grid), *Usual for Analysis* (what was added while that class was in progress, kept on this device), *Usual in this notebook*. If the suggested paper is not among the usual chips it takes the place of the weakest one; the same-paper chip and the slide chip stay. It never adds anything by itself and never replaces your choice. New setting *Suggest a paper for new pages* (Pages), on by default.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested ranker `src/model/paper-suggest.ts` (no `obsidian` import): a list of candidates with a score and a structured reason, with an `extra` input so another feature (a profile paper, score `PROFILE_SCORE`) outranks history. The course habit is a bounded per-device value in local storage. No format change; notebooks are only read. English and Italian.

### Marked pages view
- **Marked pages only in the page navigator (#419).** A flag button in the panel header switches between all pages and *Marked pages only*: pages with a bookmark, a global bookmark, a marker (gap, question, homework, importance), ink in a colour with a meaning, an open checkbox or a page tag. Each page keeps its number and shows why it is marked; a menu picks which kinds count; works in thumbnails, the large layout and the compact list; an empty result says so quietly. The choice is remembered per notebook on this device (bounded local storage). Read-only: no format change; long-press help in English and Italian.

### Review fixes
- **Erasing writing no longer takes the area highlighter under it.** The whole-stroke and the segment eraser now remove an area highlighter only when the same stroke of the pen has erased no ink: rubbing out a letter you wrote on top of an area keeps the area, scrubbing an empty area still removes it, and the *Highlighter* eraser filter always takes it. An area the pen passed over before it reached ink comes back at once, so one Undo never holds both. The area eraser (by outline) is unchanged.
- **An area in a file older than format 23 is refused.** Like every earlier element type (excerpts before 10, sticky notes before 15, checkboxes before 17), an `area` in a file of an older format was not written by this plugin, so the file is refused and left untouched instead of being read as if it were new, both in the normal and in the staged loading of large files.
- **Hand-in pre-flight.** A template box that holds only an area highlighter is no longer reported as an empty box.
- **Going far back in the history panel no longer freezes the page.** A jump of hundreds of steps on a large notebook (every step checks the whole document, as an Undo does) now runs in short slices of about 60 ms with the panel showing *Going to that step: N of M*, takes no tap or edit until it ends, and ends with the same notice and the same stop at a refused step as before. A short jump is still taken in one go. The steps of one jump also stop repeating the check before each step (they reuse the answer the step before ended with), which roughly halves the time; every step still checks the document after it. New benchmark `tests/history-jump.perf.ts` (`npm run bench`): 500 steps take about 0.7 s on 2,000 strokes, 2.5 s on 10,000 and 9 s on 40,000 strokes, in slices of at most about 80 ms.
- **Copy as image never saves a file you did not ask for.** When the share sheet refuses because the tap is too old, one notice asks you to tap *Copy as image* again and nothing is written to the vault. Every outcome now shows exactly one notice (the saved copy used to show two success notices and two *working* notices).
- **Writing profiles.** A saved profile gets an id that is never reused, so a new profile can no longer show the pens of a deleted one. Deleting a profile deletes the pen set it made (it used to pile up against the limit of 24); if a notebook, a folder or another profile still uses that set it is kept and the notice says so. When the pen set list is full, switching to a profile says that the pens did not change (the smoothing and the paper did) instead of claiming a switch. Strokes, pages and notebooks are never touched.
- **A long history jump is a change like an Undo.** Every slice marks the notebook changed and the notebook counts as busy until the jump ends, so a file change that arrives meanwhile is kept against and the other version is backed up (it can no longer reload over the steps just taken), and closing the notebook mid-jump saves the steps already taken.
- **A jump stopped by a reload or by switching to another file leaves the new notebook alone.** It writes nothing into the replacement and says the jump was interrupted (not that a step was refused).
- **A failing slice no longer leaves the notebook locked.** Whatever happens inside a long jump, the edit lock is released and the steps taken are saved.
- **Copy as image cannot ask for a new tap forever.** The picture drawn for a share that asked for a new tap is kept for a minute and shared at once on the next tap; if the share still needs a tap with the picture ready, the saved copy is made instead, with one notice.
- **Deleting a profile keeps a pen set that another open notebook is showing** (the notice says so). The strip of the notebook you delete it from falls back to your favourite pens, as before.

## 0.24.0 — 2026-11-08

Eight features: filled shapes and dashed or dotted lines, joining of broken strokes, a context-ordered actions sheet, explanations on long press of an icon, tag suggestions, an adaptive page navigator, global bookmarks across notebooks and a hand-in dialog. The notebook format moves to version 22 (filled shapes and dashed or dotted lines): the migration from version 21 is automatic, and 0.23 and older open a v22 notebook read-only and never overwrite it. Two independent review rounds are folded in (see Review fixes).

### Filled shapes, dashed lines and document format 22
- **Filled shapes and dashed or dotted lines (#37) and document format 22.** The shape tool's options have a *Fill* (none, solid, or a translucent tint, in the colour of the line or a palette colour) for the rectangle and the ellipse and a *Line* style (solid, dashed, dotted) for every shape; the selection bar restyles both for the selected shapes as one undo step (freehand ink is never touched, groups and locks are respected). Fill and dashes are drawn on the page, in the live preview, in the PNG, SVG, PDF and HTML exports, the written-area export and the thumbnails; tapping inside a filled shape picks it with the eyedropper.
- **Document format v22.** Three optional stroke fields, only on shape strokes: `fill`, `fillTint`, `dash`. The v21 to v22 migration is the identity plus the version; a bad value is dropped and counted when reading and refused when writing; older readers open a v22 notebook read-only. `docs/schema/notebook-v21.schema.json` is now `notebook-v22.schema.json`; `docs/format.md` and `docs/architecture.md` describe the fields. Old documents render exactly as before.
- **Code.** Pure geometry in `src/ink/shape-style.ts` (dash runs by arc length, scaled by the line width; closed-shape test; fill polygon), the selection restyle in `src/ink/shape-restyle.ts`, one row builder for the options and the bar in `src/view/shape-style-row.ts`; English and Italian.

### Join broken strokes
- **Join broken strokes (#313).** When the pen briefly loses contact in the middle of a line, the line is saved as two strokes. Select the ink and tap *Join broken strokes* (second row of the selection bar, All actions sheet, command *Join broken strokes in the selection*): each pair that is one gesture split by a dropout becomes one stroke, in one undo step, with a notice that counts the joints. A chain of several fragments becomes one stroke.
- **Conservative on purpose.** Two strokes are joined only when they are neighbours in the page order, in the same style (tool, colour, width, nib, render version, slide) and the same group, both are real lines (a dot is never a fragment), the end of the first and the start of the second are a few points apart (a small multiple of the pen width and of the point spacing, and a small share of the shorter piece), the direction is continuous (no sharp turn: the corner of an L, a V and a dash pattern stay apart) and the pressure does not jump. Words, letters, the dot of an *i*, dashes and intentional pen lifts are never joined. Highlighter and marker strokes are not touched.
- **Safe.** The joined stroke keeps the id, style, group and nib of the first piece and the points of all pieces unchanged (still `[x, y, pressure]` in page coordinates); the page order and the page times do not move; a locked page refuses it; the ink a collapsed sticky note keeps hidden, and strokes of a group that would shrink to one member or that holds a locked element, are skipped. Nothing is joined while writing and *Clean up strokes* does not join (they stay two separate actions). No setting, no document format change.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested module `src/ink/stroke-join.ts` (no `obsidian` import); the action in `src/view/selection-actions.ts` (`joinBroken`) and the registry (`selection-join`); English and Italian.

### Actions ordered by context
- **Actions ordered by context (#424).** Obsidian does not let a plugin reorder its own command palette, so the plugin's *All actions* sheet does it: it opens with a short *For this page* section (up to five actions that fit what is in front of you: the selection, a PDF page, the lasso in hand, a lecture, the side pane, presentation mode), and the search ranks matching actions by designed importance, recent use and fit to the context. Library-level and diagnostic actions step back while a notebook is open. Commands that act on a notebook were already hidden from Obsidian's palette when no notebook view is active (`checkCallback`); that is unchanged. No format change. English and Italian.

### Explanation on long press of an icon
- **Explanation on long press of an icon (#429).** Hold a finger on a toolbar tool, the undo and redo buttons, the pen chip, a button of the selection or contextual bar, or a Home header icon: after about half a second a small bubble anchored to the icon says its name and, in one line, what it does (the tools also say that a second tap opens their options). The button is not pressed, and the bubble goes a moment after you lift, or at once when you tap elsewhere. A swipe, a quick tap, the mouse (it keeps its hover tooltip) and the pen (S Pen button rules and writing are untouched) never show it, and buttons with their own long press (the quick actions that pin, the page indicator quick tab) keep it. *Settings > Appearance > Explain icons on long press* turns it off. English and Italian; one shared helper (`src/ui/long-press-help.ts`) with pure timing and placement logic (`src/ui/long-press-logic.ts`).

### Tags suggested from context
- **Tags suggested from context (#411).** When you edit the tags of a notebook, or of a page, up to six suggestion chips appear: the course of the class linked to the notebook or its folder (timetable), tags that other notebooks in the same folder use, frequent words of the typed text and titles (English and Italian stop-words ignored), and what is on the page (questions, homework, definitions, theorems...). One tap adds the tag through the same path as before (notebook tags and page tags are still undo steps in the notebook). Computed on the device from what is already there: no AI, no network. New setting *Suggest tags* (Integration). The document format is unchanged.

### Adaptive page navigator
- **Adaptive page navigator (#414).** The page panel now fits the notebook. When most pages have a PDF or picture background (an annotated PDF, slides) it shows large thumbnails in a wider panel; when most pages are written it shows a compact list (page number, title, the time the page was written, tags, and a small thumbnail); a mix keeps the usual layout. The title is the bookmark's title, else the first line of the topmost typed text on the page. A button in the panel header cycles *Auto*, *Thumbnails* and *List*; the choice is remembered per notebook on this device only (bounded local storage, never in the notebook or the vault). Thumbnails stay cached per page and width and are drawn lazily; the list is built in chunks and offscreen rows are skipped by the browser, so a 500-page notebook opens at once. No format change. English and Italian. Tests: `tests/nav-adapt.test.ts`; harness `navadapt`.

### Global bookmarks
- **Global bookmarks (#265): pages from different notebooks in one list.** *Bookmark in the global list* (page sheet and command, a toggle) adds the page in view to one list that covers every notebook; the same action removes it. *Global bookmarks* (command, page sheet, and a *Bookmarks* chip on Home once the list has something) opens the list in a dialog: tap a row to open the notebook at that page, rename a row, move it up or down, or remove it. A page that was deleted, or a notebook that cannot be found, is shown dashed with a *Missing* badge and can still be removed. The list is not part of any notebook: **no format change**, nothing is written into notebook files.
- **Where it lives.** One file per device, `_meta/bookmarks-<device>.json` (the same sidecar pattern as quick tabs: a device writes only its own file, the latest change wins, a removal is kept as a tombstone for 90 days, entries and fields it does not know are kept, a file that cannot be read or is still empty from a sync is never overwritten). It names the notebook by path and by its own id and the page by page id, so it follows a notebook that is renamed or moved (a folder rename too) and finds again one that was moved where no rename was seen. The library check does not count it as holding any asset.

### Hand in…
- **Hand in… (#334).** A dialog (notebook menu, Export & share sheet, command palette) that exports chosen pages as one PDF with a generated cover page first: your name (remembered on this device only), the course (prefilled from the timetable class linked to the notebook), the assignment title and the date. A grid of page pictures with checkboxes chooses the pages (the page in view at first; All / None). Below it a live pre-flight lists blank pages among the chosen ones and what is still open: gaps, questions and homework not done, unticked checkboxes, notes that say TODO and empty template boxes. Each row jumps to its page; *Leave out the blank pages* unticks them; the main button turns into *Export anyway* while anything is open. The PDF is the ordinary export, saved next to the notebook as `<notebook> hand-in.pdf` (never over an existing file), recorded as plugin-written, and offered to the share sheet when the device has one. No format change.

### Review fixes
- **Long-press help never presses the button.** The click that ends a long press is swallowed by a one-shot guard armed when the bubble shows, not by a clock from that moment: a finger held for two seconds on Delete, Cut or Clear page no longer runs it when lifted, and a quick tap on the same button right after is never swallowed.
- **Join broken strokes.** Ink bound to a graph is never joined with ink bound to another graph or with free ink (the joined stroke would be clipped to, or leave, the graph frame).
- **Shape style.** Redo restyles the stroke as it is now, so a change made by background upkeep (slide relink, margin refresh) is not reverted; filling an outline whose ends are only close closes it exactly, so the fill survives scaling; the version comparison counts a fill, dash or nib change as a change.
- **Global bookmarks.** The file at a bookmark's path is trusted only when it has the same notebook id; looking at the list never moves a bookmark (a notebook that has not synced yet keeps its bookmarks), a bookmark follows a moved notebook only on a tap and only when one notebook has that id, and the notebooks are read once per dialog. A copy of a notebook (same id) is not mistaken for the original. Every unknown field of the file and of an entry survives a rewrite, including values this version only shows shortened or rounded. The same page bookmarked on two devices is one row and is removed everywhere.
- **Global bookmarks, round 2.** Following a moved notebook moves only the bookmarks of that notebook's id, never those of another notebook that now sits at the old path (no ping-pong of synced writes, no duplicates when toggling). A page counts as already bookmarked when the file at the bookmark's path is known to carry another notebook id. Moving or renaming a row that is the same page bookmarked on two devices acts on every twin, so the row never jumps back.
- **Long-press help, round 2.** A second finger, the palm or the pen landing while the first finger still holds the button no longer lets the first finger's lift press it.
- **Tag suggestions.** A suggested page tag never contains a comma, a semicolon or a hash, so one tap adds exactly one tag.
- **Page panel.** The thumbnail cache is bounded (least recently used first, 200 thumbnails), stale widths of a changed page are dropped and everything is released when the notebook closes; the per-notebook layout choices are stored as ordered pairs, so the oldest are forgotten correctly (the earlier object format is still read).
- **Explanations on long press** for the global bookmarks chip and actions, the page panel layout button, Hand in, Join broken strokes and the Fill and Line controls of the shape style row, in English and Italian.
- **Tests.** New cases in `tests/long-press-help.test.ts`, `tests/stroke-join.test.ts`, `tests/model-v22.test.ts`, `tests/version-diff.test.ts`, `tests/tag-suggest.test.ts`, `tests/nav-adapt.test.ts`, `tests/context-rank.test.ts` and the new `tests/bookmarks-review.test.ts`.

## 0.23.0 — 2026-11-08

Eight features: saved views, export of the written area or the selection, dimensional analysis of formulas, balancing of chemical reactions, an automatic formula sheet, a welcome-back card, notebooks ordered by the active term and clean-up of pen hooks and stray dots. The notebook format moves to version 21 (saved views): the migration from version 20 is automatic, and 0.22 and older open a v21 notebook read-only and never overwrite it. Two independent review rounds are folded in (see Review fixes).

### Saved views and document format 21
- **Saved views (#264) and document format 21.** *Save current view…* keeps what is on screen (the page, the part of it that is in view and the zoom) under a name; *Go to saved view…* lists them and one tap brings you back. Rename, update to the current view, move up or down and remove from the menu of a row; each change is one undo step. A view of a page that was deleted is shown as *Page missing* and can be removed (Undo of the page deletion makes it work again). The views are kept in the notebook, so they travel with the file; recalling puts the middle of the saved region in the middle of the screen at the same zoom relative to the page width, so it works on another screen size. Copies of pages and pages moved to another notebook take no views along; merging a conflicted copy adds the views it has that the notebook lacks. In the sheet, the notebook menu and the palette; English and Italian.
- **Document format v21.** One optional notebook-level key, `views` (`{ id, name, page, x, y, width, height, zoom }`, at most 100, page coordinates only). The v20 to v21 migration is the identity plus the version, the reader drops invalid views and counts them, the writer refuses malformed ones, large v20 files are still read page by page, repair keeps the views; a notebook saved as v21 opens read-only in 0.22. The schema is now `docs/schema/notebook-v21.schema.json` (`docs/format.md` and the format test follow). Tests: `tests/model-v21.test.ts`; harness `savedviews`.

### Export the written area or the selection
- **Export only the written area, or the selection, as a transparent picture (#280).** *Export written area…* (page sheet, notebook menu and command) crops the current page to everything written on it, with a small margin; the *Export selection as picture* action on the selection bar does the same for the selected items alone (a selected collapsed sticky note brings its ink). Both write a PNG with a transparent background at 1x, 2x or 3x, or a vector SVG (every pen, the highlighter and marker transparency, text boxes with their link labels), next to the notebook, never overwriting, and remember the file as written by the plugin. *Copy* puts the picture on the clipboard where the device allows it. The sheet, the PDF or picture background and the paper are never drawn. An empty page says so and writes nothing. The document format is unchanged.

### Dimensional analysis of formulas
- **Dimensional analysis of formulas (#293).** A quiet hint in the equation editor, under the preview, when the units of a formula do not agree: adding metres to seconds, an equation whose two sides differ (force against energy), a function or an exponent applied to something with a dimension. It reads units written with `\mathrm`, `\text` or `\SI` (SI base units, prefixes from yocto to quetta, the named derived units N, J, W, Pa, Hz, C, V, Ω, S, F, H, T, Wb, lm, lx, Bq, Gy, Sv, kat and common others such as min, h, L, t, eV, bar, atm, cal, Wh, ft, in, mi, lb), follows dimensions through + − × ÷ ^, roots, fractions, derivatives (`\frac{dx}{dt}`), integrals, sums and limits, and takes the dimension of a symbol from the value it is set equal to (`m = 2\,\mathrm{kg}`) or from a line `[v] = m/s`. A symbol it knows nothing about is never flagged. Only dimensions are compared, not scales (`3 m + 2 km` is fine). When the units agree it shows a muted tick line. Nothing is stored and the document format is unchanged.
- **Setting.** *Settings > Writing > Check units in equations* (on by default).
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested engine in `src/math/dimensions.ts` (no `obsidian` import; also reads plain text such as `5 m + 3 s`); the words in `src/view/dimension-hint.ts`; English and Italian.

### Balancing chemical reactions
- **Balancing of typed chemical reactions (#294).** Select a text box or an equation that holds a reaction and tap *Balance reaction* in the selection bar (or the command): a preview shows each line before and after, and *Balance* replaces the text with the smallest whole coefficients in one undo step. Formulas with parentheses and brackets, hydrates (`CuSO4·5H2O`), charges (`Fe^3+`, `SO4^2-`, `Fe³⁺`, `e^-`) and arrows (`->`, `→`, `⇌`, `=`) are read; the species keep the spelling they were typed in. The coefficients come from an exact rational nullspace of the element and charge matrix (no rounding). A reaction that cannot be balanced says why (an element on one side only, unknown element, unclear charge, no positive solution, a species that cannot take part) and one with several answers says so instead of picking one. Locked elements and locked pages are never changed. An equation becomes `\ce{...}`. No format change.

### Automatic formula sheet
- **Automatic formula sheet (#249).** *Formula sheet…* (page sheet, notebook menu, command *Build formula sheet*) collects every equation of the notebook in reading order and writes `<Notebook> — Formulas.md` next to it: a heading with a link per page and a `$$…$$` block per formula (`\tag{n}` for numbered ones). The note is owned by the plugin through `notebook-formulas` markers that record the notebook's path and document id; a rebuild replaces only that region (re-read inside `vault.process`), and a note without markers, with an unpaired marker or recording another notebook is never rewritten. No document format change; the notebook file is never written.

### Welcome-back card
- **Welcome-back card when you reopen a notebook (#420).** After a notebook has stayed closed on this device for a while (default 8 hours; *Settings > Study > Timetable > Welcome back card* and *Offer it after*, 1 to 168 hours), a quiet card at the top centre offers the way back to where you were (the notebook's stored reading position, named *slide N* when that page shows a PDF page) and the first open questions and gaps already marked in the notebook (question and gap markers that are not resolved; the rest is counted as plain text). Every row is a button that goes there. The card appears once per opening, goes away on the first stroke, on the close button or when a row is taken, steps aside while the *Referenced on* list is open, and stacks below the symbols strip, the *Back to slide* pill and the *Referenced on* chip. The only thing stored is the time the notebook was last open, in this device's local storage (bounded to 80 notebooks, corrupt data read as empty); the notebook file, the format and the settings file of other devices are untouched. Pure selection logic: `src/model/welcome-back.ts`, `tests/welcome-back.test.ts`; view: `src/view/welcome-back.ts`.

### Notebooks ordered by the active term
- **Notebooks ordered by the active term (#416).** The Library sort menu has *Active term* (*Semestre in corso*): notebooks linked to a course of the term that includes today come first, the one whose next class is soonest first (then course order in the timetable), then notebooks of the courses whose term is over or not yet started, then every other notebook, each group by last change. The link is the one the timetable already has (a class's notebook or course folder); nothing new is stored and the document format is unchanged. The option is hidden without a timetable and falls back to last-modified. It becomes the default only when a class has dates around today and you have never chosen a sort; picking any sort (or having one saved) keeps your choice.

### Clean up hooks and stray dots
- **Clean up hooks and stray dots (#314).** *Clean up strokes* in the contextual bar of an ink selection (and in the All actions sheet and the command palette) trims the small hook a pen leaves where it lands or lifts, and removes the stray dot a bouncing pen leaves touching the end of a stroke, as ONE undo step. A notice says how many hooks and dots went. Only the points at the very start or end of a stroke are cut; the stroke keeps its id, style, group and nib, and its place in the page order.
- **Conservative on purpose.** A hook is a tick of at most a few pen widths (6 pt at most) and a tenth of the stroke that turns back at 120 degrees or more against a straight run of line. Real corners (the foot of an L, a check mark, an arrow barb, a Z or a W), curves and circles are never cut. A dot is removed only when it is a single tiny mark in the same pen, colour, width and nib as the stroke written just before or after it and touches one of its ends: the dot of an "i" or "j" and a full stop sit apart from the stroke and stay. Grouped dots and hidden ink are left alone.
- **Respects the page.** Groups come whole, the ink a collapsed sticky note keeps hidden is never touched, a locked page refuses the step and says so, and the selection stays on the cleaned strokes (also across pages).
- **Setting *Trim hooks as you write* (Settings > Writing, off by default).** When the pen lifts, the stroke is committed as written and a hook at its start or end is cut as a step of its own, with stricter limits than the manual clean-up; one Undo gives the written stroke back. The live input and the rendering are not touched, and a snapped or recognised stroke is left as it is.
- **Code.** Pure `src/ink/hook-clean.ts` (`findHooks`, `trimHooks`, `isStrayDot`, `planCleanup`), tested with synthetic strokes in `tests/hook-clean.test.ts`. No document format change.

### Review fixes
- **Balance reaction keeps the rest of an equation.** Only the lines of an equation that are a reaction and get balanced are rewritten (as mhchem, written with the ce command); other maths, text, spacing, a reaction that is balanced already or cannot be balanced are kept character for character, and the preview shows the whole resulting source. When a step is refused the notice names the real reason; the "page is locked" message appears only when the page is locked.
- **Trim hooks as you write is undoable.** The stroke is committed as written and the trim is its own undo step (one Undo gives back the stroke you wrote, a second removes it; a late side-button press takes the whole contact back). The writing-time rule is stricter than the manual clean-up (only a near reversal of a very short hook), so the exit tail of a printed a, d or u stays.
- **Clean up strokes never edits hidden ink.** The ink a collapsed sticky note keeps hidden is left alone, and a hidden stroke is never taken for the neighbour of a dot.
- **Saved views.** A unique name (" 2", " 3"…) never leaves a double space, so a conflict merge of two views with a long name is not refused by the content guard. A view cannot be saved or updated for a page whose id is over 200 characters (the "could not be saved" notice appears instead of a guard error). The merge summary counts saved views that differ in the copy (the notebook's stay) and views that did not fit the list of 100, and says that the copy stays in place in that case. Repair keeps the view lines it can read when the `views` list itself is damaged.
- **Formula sheet.** A dollar sign after a double-backslash line break is escaped, so it cannot end the maths block of the note. A note of yours with the same name and no markers (or with one marker missing) is never touched and the sheet goes to the next free name (`Formulas (2)`) instead of failing; an empty note is filled.
- **Trim as you write, round 2.** The trim is worked out before the stroke is committed, so the renderer draws the trimmed stroke at once and the trim step holds that same object: no full redraw of the page after each pen-up. The first Undo after a trim now says "Hook put back. Undo again to remove the stroke." (English and Italian). A take-back of a stroke and its trim redraws and saves whenever a step was undone, even if the second step is refused. The view side lives in `src/view/commit-trim.ts` and is tested with a real history (`tests/commit-trim.test.ts`) and in the hookclean harness.
- **Balance reaction keeps `&`.** A row of an aligned environment with one `&` right before the arrow is balanced as `\ce{left} &\ce{arrow right}`, so the columns still line up; a row with any other `&` is left as written.
- **Merge message.** "Saved views differ in the copy" no longer names a backup (the copy is only moved when you ask): the copy's versions stay in the copy.
- **Tests.** `tests/review-w23-data.test.ts`, plus new cases in `tests/hook-clean.test.ts`, `tests/chem-balance.test.ts`, `tests/chem-tools.test.ts` and `tests/formula-sheet.test.ts`.

## 0.22.0 — 2026-11-08

Seven features: three new pens, optional cloud sync with Google Drive or OneDrive, two-way page references, colours with meaning, instant axes, custom covers and an automatic lecture header. The notebook format moves to version 20 (the new pen tools and the nib angle): the migration from version 19 is automatic, and 0.21 and older open a v20 notebook read-only and never overwrite it. Cloud sync is off by default and uses your own OAuth client; the plugin ships no client ID or secret.

### How the new features work together
- The cloud sync is joined to the Google Drive and OneDrive sign-in at load; Google's client secret lives under one device-local key and never in the token blob, `data.json` or the vault. The new pens: ghost guide lines and scribble to erase work with the fountain pen and the brush (not the marker), the colour-meaning filter dims the marker too, and an L or a cross drawn with the marker is not turned into axes. Floating pills that share a corner (slide-back, symbols strip, "Referenced on", colour-filter pill, lecture-header chip, a parked minimap) are stacked instead of overlapping. The pen options popover leaves a strip of page below it on a phone held sideways, so a tap on the page still closes it.

### New pens and document format 20 (#35)
- **New pens (#35): fountain pen, brush and marker.** The pen options have a *Pen type* row. The *fountain pen* is a nib: lines drawn along the nib angle are thin, lines across it thick (pressure still modulates the width, ends are crisp, nothing tapers). The *brush* answers to pressure (fine tip to full belly) and speed (thinner when it is fast), grows from a point and closes to a point. The *marker* is a flat chisel: constant for any pressure, a little narrower along the nib, 85% opaque and painted in stroke order (above the ink written before it, with that ink showing through), not multiplied like the highlighter. The fountain pen and the marker have a *Nib angle* (a slider with a little bar that leans like the nib); the angle is stored on the stroke. The same look in the canvas, the wet ink, thumbnails, SVG, PDF, PNG and the interactive HTML export; eraser, lasso and moves work on their outlines, and a rotated selection turns the nib with it. A hold-still shape made with a new pen becomes an ordinary shape.
- **Favourites, pen sets and the style eyedropper remember the kind** (and the nib angle). *Settings > Writing* has *Pen type* (the pen the pen tool starts with) and *Nib angle*.
- **Document format v20.** The tools `fountain`, `brush` and `marker` (always `render` 2) and the optional stroke field `nib` (whole degrees 0 to 179, absent = 45, only the fountain pen and the marker have one). The v19 to v20 migration refuses a v19 file that already has a new tool (no version wrote one) and drops a stray `nib` key; read leniently, an unknown tool name is a pen (counted) and a bad nib is dropped (counted). 0.21 and older open a v20 file read-only. `docs/schema/notebook-v20.schema.json` replaces the v19 schema; the geometry of the pen, highlighter and shape is frozen by golden tests (`tests/pens-golden.test.ts`).

### Cloud sync with Google Drive and OneDrive (#126)
- **Cloud sync with Google Drive or OneDrive (#126), the engine and the settings.** Off by default and opt-in per device, no telemetry. *Settings > Sync & safety* has a *Cloud sync* group: the service, your own OAuth *Client ID* (the same on every device; Google's *Client secret* stays in this device's local storage, never in `data.json`), the account (*Sign in*, *Change folder*, *Disconnect*), *Sync now* with the last result (*Stop* while running, *Review* when a run waits for a decision), what to sync (whole vault, selected folders, or include / exclude globs and file types), sync on open, on close, every N minutes, Wi-Fi only (where the network type is unknown, a phone or tablet holds the automatic runs and a desktop runs), a large-file rule (ask or skip above a size) and the activity log of the last 50 events. Command *Cloud sync now*; a status bar item on desktop. `.obsidian`, `.trash`, temporary files and the backup folder (#127) are never synced.
- **Safety rules.** Three-way sync against a per-device state in the plugin folder (`cloud-sync/state-<device>.json`, remote index and base with SHA-256): the first sync is a union and deletes nothing; a deletion is propagated only from a synced, unchanged state and always to the trash (Obsidian's on this side, the cloud's on the other); more than 20 deletions (or 3 and over 30 % of the tracked files) stop the run and ask (*Keep them* copies them back, *Delete them* goes ahead); a file changed on both sides keeps both, the cloud's as `Name (conflict YYYY-MM-DD HH.MM Device).ext`, which the #122 merge offer recognises; uploads are conditional on the cloud revision and the run re-plans when the cloud changed meanwhile; open notebooks are saved before upload and never replaced while they hold unsaved ink; downloads go through the usual external-change reload. A damaged state file is kept as `.bad.json` and the next run lists everything again without deleting. Transient errors retry with back-off (honouring retry-after); a sign-in problem stops and asks to sign in again; a missing cloud folder is an error, never "everything was deleted". No document format change.
- **Code.** Pure, Node-tested logic in `src/sync/` (`rules.ts`, `state.ts`, `planner.ts`, `conflict-name.ts`, `engine.ts`, `settings.ts`, `network.ts`, `sync-memory.ts`); Obsidian wiring in `src/sync/sync-service.ts` and `src/sync/vault-adapter.ts`; the sign-in contract in `src/sync/auth-ui.ts`, implemented over the providers by `src/sync/auth-adapter.ts` (wired in `src/sync/auth/obsidian-auth.ts` and plugged in at load with `cloudSync.setAuth`; Google's client secret has ONE device-local key and is kept out of the token blob, the client ID is the only OAuth value in the synced settings, and the *Account* row says that every device must use the same client ID because Google's `drive.file` scope only shows the “Notebook sync” folder this client created); UI in `src/view/cloud-sync-settings.ts` and `src/view/cloud-sync-modals.ts`. Tests: `tests/sync-planner.test.ts`, `tests/sync-rules.test.ts`, `tests/sync-state.test.ts`, `tests/sync-engine.test.ts`; harness `doc=synccore`.

- **Cloud connector: Google Drive and OneDrive providers and sign-in (#126, part 2).** Sign-in with the OAuth 2.0 device code flow (RFC 8628), the same on Android and desktop: a dialog shows the code and the address, with *Copy code*, *Open sign-in page*, a countdown and *Cancel*, and handles denied, expired, refused-client and network failures with *Try again*. The plugin ships no client ID or secret: you register your own app (step-by-step guide in `docs/cloud-sync.md`). Google uses `drive.file` (the only Drive scope the device flow allows besides the hidden app folder), so everything lives in a `Notebook sync` folder the plugin creates; OneDrive uses `Files.ReadWrite.AppFolder`, so everything lives in the plugin's app folder (`Apps/<app name>`) and nothing else of the drive is visible. Tokens stay on the device (Obsidian's local storage), refresh by themselves, are never logged; *Disconnect* revokes them where the provider allows (Google). Both providers list changes incrementally (Drive changes feed, OneDrive delta) with paths rebuilt from parent ids, upload large files in chunks, refuse to overwrite a remote file that changed (`If-Match` on OneDrive, a revision check just before the write on Drive), move removed files to the remote trash (never a permanent delete), and retry 429/5xx with backoff and `Retry-After`. HTTP only through `requestUrl`. No format change. `src/sync/auth/`, `src/sync/providers/`; tests `tests/sync-*.test.ts`; harness `doc=syncprov`.

### Colours with meaning (#101)
- **Colours with meaning (#101).** *Settings > Reading & PDF > Colour meanings* opens a legend editor: give each ink colour a name (red = definition, blue = example), reorder, recolour, add and remove; *Add the usual meanings* starts from red / blue / green. *Colour matching* (strict, normal, loose) says how close a stroke's colour must be to a legend colour; two legend colours that are too close are flagged. A notebook can have a legend of its own (its menu, *Colour meanings…*), kept in `_meta/meanings-<device>.json` (one file per device, like the quick tabs; read leniently, unknown fields kept, an unreadable file is never overwritten).
- **Filter by meaning.** The notebook menu has *Filter by meaning…*: choose a meaning and the ink of the others (and of no meaning) is drawn faint, with a pill at the bottom left to clear it. It is view-only (the renderer redraws its static layer once; nothing is saved and the document is not touched) and not kept when you switch notebook.
- **Digest by colour.** *Digest by colour…* writes `<Notebook> — Colours.md` next to the notebook: one section per meaning in legend order, a picture of each place the colour was used (close ink of one meaning is one place), the PDF text under it and a link to the page. Only the part between the `notebook-meanings` markers is rewritten; your text around it stays, a second run makes no new pictures, an unpaired marker is left alone and a note of yours with that name is added to only after you agree.
- No document format change. New: `src/model/colour-meaning.ts`, `src/library/meanings.ts`, `src/library/meanings-store.ts`, `src/view/colour-meaning.ts`, `src/view/meaning-modals.ts`, `src/view/meaning-digest.ts`; the renderer has a view-only `setInkDim`.

### Custom covers (#53)
- **Custom covers (#53).** *Change cover…* (library card menu and the notebook tab menu) opens one dialog with four sources: a generated *Pattern* (families, colour, new variation), an *Image* from the vault or the device, a *Page* of the notebook (stepper) and five designed *Templates* (Classic, Band, Frame, Minimal, Poster) that put the notebook name on generated art. Images and pages are moved and zoomed in the 3:4 frame (drag, wheel, pinch, slider, arrow keys). Nothing is written until *Use this cover*: the picture is baked small (540 x 720 JPEG), stored as a content-addressed asset in `_assets` and set as the existing `meta.coverArt` image cover, so there is **no format change** and no new sidecar. An open notebook takes it as one undo step; a closed one through the safe read-patch-write; a picture this action created is trashed again if the change fails. Large photos are reduced to 1600 px for editing (Android memory). Crop maths `src/model/cover-crop.ts`, templates `src/model/cover-template.ts`, painting `src/ui/cover-compose.ts`, dialog `src/library/cover-dialog.ts`. Tests: `tests/cover-crop.test.ts`, `tests/cover-template.test.ts`.

### Two-way page references (#179)
- **Two-way page references (#179).** A page that other pages of the notebook link to (a page link in a text box or a sticky note) shows a quiet chip, *Referenced on p. 3, p. 12*; tapping it lists those pages with a jump to each. Links name pages by id, so reordering pages keeps them; deleted pages, links to itself and links to other notebooks are ignored. The index is derived in small idle slices and cached, nothing is stored (format unchanged). The new setting *Show pages that link here* (Pages > Outline) turns it off. Tests: `tests/page-refs.test.ts`; harness `doc=pagerefs`.

### Instant axes (#206)
- **Instant axes (#206).** An L (up, then right) or a + of two crossing lines drawn with the pen is offered as Cartesian axes: a *Make axes* chip for nine seconds, or made by itself with the setting *L or + drawn as axes* (offer, make, leave as ink). The result is plain shape ink (one arrowed stroke per arm with evenly spaced ticks following the page grid or line spacing) and small number labels, all in one new group and one undo step that also takes away your lines. *Ticks on axes* and *Numbers on axes* settings. *Axes* in the insert sheet drops four-quadrant axes where the pen last wrote. No format change; the input pipeline is untouched (the gesture is read when a stroke ends, with the shape recogniser of hold-to-snap).


### Automatic lecture header (#210)
- **Automatic lecture header (#210).** When a class of the timetable is on for the notebook, its header (course, lecturer, room, date and start time) can be written on the page you are writing on: two small text boxes in a fresh group (course larger, the rest in a quiet line under it) at the top margin, as one undo step. It goes only where the page is empty: it moves down to the first free spot in the top of the page and never overlaps ink or objects; with no free spot nothing is written and the chip *Add lecture header* waits. It is offered or done when a new page is added during the class, and when lecture mode starts on a page that is not a lecture page of its own. New settings in *Study > Timetable*: *Lecture header* (Off / Ask / Automatic; Ask shows a quiet chip) and which fields to write (Lecturer, Room, Date, Start time). New command *Insert lecture header*: uses the class in progress or else the closest class of today, and refuses a locked page. Date and time follow the interface language (English, Italian). No document format change. Pure rules in `src/timetable/lecture-header.ts`, view side in `src/view/lecture-header.ts`. Tests: `tests/lecture-header.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/lecthead-check.ts` (`doc=lecthead`).

### Review fixes
- **Cloud sync waits for the vault to load (review W22 S1).** No run starts (not even *Sync now*, which says why) until Obsidian's layout is ready and its metadata cache has indexed the vault (30 s fallback), so a file not listed yet is never taken for a deletion.
- **Sync deletions here go to Obsidian's `.trash` (S2)**, never through the *Deleted files* setting, which can be *Permanently delete*.
- **Google Drive: no lost or doubled writes (S3, S4).** A version another device wrote just before ours is read from the file's history and kept as a conflicted copy; a trash that raced a write is undone. Files and folders are created with ids reserved by `files.generateIds`, so a retried request cannot make a twin; two cloud files with one name are no longer skipped: every device follows the same one and the other is kept as a conflicted copy before going to Drive's trash.
