- **Disconnect (S5)** asks first, stops a run in progress and waits for it before forgetting the sign-in and the sync state; it can also forget Google's client secret. Signing in to the other service disconnects the current one first.
- **OneDrive (S6, S13, S14).** The scope is now `Files.ReadWrite.AppFolder`: the plugin syncs with its app folder (`Apps/<app name>`) and sees nothing else of the drive. The revision is the file's content hash, so renames and other metadata changes no longer make conflicted copies. On older OneDrive for Business accounts that refuse the folder's change feed, the folder is listed file by file instead of reading the whole drive.
- **Safer automatic runs (S7, S16).** Runs at start-up and when leaving the app never delete (the status counts the deletions that wait); automatic runs stop and ask above 10 deletions. On a phone or tablet, an unknown network type holds the automatic runs when *Wi-Fi only* is on, and the new *Size cap on phones* (default 50 MB) keeps larger files from being transferred there.
- **Names (S9, S10).** Names that cannot exist on every device are skipped on both sides with one notice and never deleted because of it; names that differ only by case or Unicode form are never both written, and a case-only rename is followed. A conflicted copy outside the sync scope stays local (S8). The state is saved at most every 10 seconds during a run (S11). Drive's upload session must be on Google's API host, and error texts no longer carry addresses (S15). `docs/cloud-sync.md` explains that folders made on the Drive website are invisible to the plugin (S12). New `src/sync/names.ts`; tests `tests/sync-review-w22.test.ts`, `tests/sync-service-w22.test.ts`.
- **Kept files stay kept (review round 2).** A file you chose to keep (*Keep them*) that cannot be copied back yet (above the size cap on phones, or waiting for the large-file rule) is no longer tracked as synced, so a later run sees it as new on one side and never moves it to the trash.
- **Cloud sync, round 2.** Two cloud files with one name: every device follows the newest one. A local rename to a name that cannot be synced is not taken for a deletion in the cloud. On Google Drive, a version written by another device at the same moment that cannot be checked or is above the size cap is reported in the activity log (it stays in the file's version history), a twin above the cap is skipped with a note instead of downloaded, and a replace finds its file when two folders share one name. A OneDrive listing whose next page leaves Microsoft Graph, or that does not finish, fails the run instead of returning part of the folder.
- **Lecture header and deleted pages.** The pages of a notebook count as seen from the moment it opens, so a first change that deletes a page no longer makes the remaining pages look new.
- **Lecture header and undo.** In *Automatic* a page only counts as new when it was created by a forward edit: a page that comes back through Undo or Redo never gets a header, so the redo stack stays intact.
- **Unknown tool names.** A stroke whose tool this version does not know is still read as a pen and counted (with the usual backup); the strict write still refuses such a name. Documented in `docs/format.md`.
- **Style eyedropper.** Taking a ballpoint or shape stroke selects the ballpoint (and the default nib), so it no longer keeps writing with the fountain pen.
- **Marker memory.** The marker's wet canvas is created when a marker stroke starts and released a few seconds after the last one; other pens never touch it.
- **Colour meaning and the marker.** The "filter by meaning" dimming applies to marker ink like every other ink.
- **Axes in Automatic.** The short wait before an L becomes axes is dropped by the next pen contact, a scroll or zoom by you, or a page change, and never runs while the pen is on the screen.
- **Digest by colour.** The write reads the note again: if its markers vanished while the pictures were rendered, you are asked before the section is added to your note.
- **Colour meanings sidecar.** Unknown fields inside a legend entry, and entries this version cannot read, are written back unchanged until you edit that legend.
- **Cover picture.** Two "Use this cover" run one after the other, and a failed one never trashes a picture that another notebook or an open one already uses; the dialog's first read no longer leaves an unhandled error.
- **Floating pills.** On a phone the strip, the "Back to current page" pill and the "Referenced on" chip start below the "Lecture mode" chip; the minimap parked at a bottom corner, the colour-filter pill and the lecture-header chip stand above the contextual bar; the pill and the chip start to the right of a minimap at the bottom left. The new harness scenario `pillstack` puts all of them on screen at once.

## 0.21.0 — 2026-11-08

Eight features: a start and end time on every page, scheduled backups, a documented file format with a JSON Schema, an interactive HTML export, a back-of-book index, a reusable signature and date stamp, a way back to the current slide and a strip of recent symbols; plus the review fixes carried over from 0.20. The notebook format moves to version 19 (page start and end times): the migration from version 18 is automatic, and 0.20 and older open a v19 notebook read-only and never overwrite it.

### Start and end time on every page (#217) and document format 19
- **Start and end time on every page (#217), document format 19.** A page now remembers when the first ink or object was added to it and when the last one was. They show in the page header line (when the header is on), on the cards of the notebook map (tooltip, label and a quiet line under the thumbnail) and in *Page information* (*Page started*, *Page last written*). The new setting *Show when a page was written* (Pages > Header) hides them from the header and the map; the page information always shows them. A page written before this version has none until something is added to it. They are also the time base audio recorded with a page will use (M5).
- **Document format v19.** Pages get an optional `startAt` and `endAt` (ms since 1970, written together, end not before start). The v18 to v19 migration only drops these keys from a v18 file, so large v18 files still open page by page; a lone, fractional, out-of-range or inverted pair is dropped and counted when read and refused when written; 0.20 and older open a v19 notebook read-only and never overwrite it. The times are upkeep, not an edit: they are stamped by the history after a command that adds ink or an element succeeded, have no undo step of their own, and Undo and Redo never move them. A duplicated page, a template and a page made from one start without times; a page moved to another notebook or restored from the page trash keeps them; restoring an old version keeps the earliest start and latest end. A merge of conflicted copies takes the earliest start and the latest end and never stamps the moment of the merge. Repair keeps a valid pair and resets an invalid one. See `docs/architecture.md`, *Page start and end time, format v19*.

### Scheduled backups (#127)
- **Scheduled backups (#127).** *Settings > Sync & safety* has *Scheduled backups* (off by default, every day or every week), *Backup folder* (a vault folder, default `Notebook backups`; point your own sync tool at it to copy the zips elsewhere) and *Backups to keep* (default 5), and a *Back up now* row that shows the time and result of the last backup; the command palette has *Back up the notebooks now*. A backup is one `Notebook backup YYYYMMDD-HHmmss.zip` with every notebook file, the `_assets` and `_meta` folders and the templates, read as they are on disk after the open notebooks were saved; the trash, the `_backup` copies and the backup folder itself are left out. While Obsidian is open the schedule is checked a minute after start-up, every half hour and when the app returns to the foreground; a backup made by another device and synced here counts. It waits for the pen, reads in short slices with a progress notice, runs one at a time, writes under a temporary name, checks the size, renames, and only then trashes the older plugin-written backups beyond the number to keep (through Obsidian's trash, and only files that carry the plugin's exact backup name; the newest complete backup is never trashed to make room for partial ones). A file that cannot be read (a notebook still arriving from the sync client) is skipped and the backup is marked `partial`. A failed run leaves the older backups as they were and the next try waits an hour. The folder is not part of the library, the shared-files inbox or the companion notes. No format change; no Drive and no folder outside the vault (the plugin has no Node or Electron access); the zip is built in memory, so a vault above 64 MB on a phone or tablet (1 GB on a computer) is refused with a message. Own STORE-only zip writer and strict reader in `src/model/zip.ts` (no library, no licence to record), rules in `src/storage/backup-logic.ts`, one run in `src/storage/backup-run.ts`, Obsidian wiring in `src/storage/backup-service.ts`. Tests: `tests/zip.test.ts`, `tests/backup-logic.test.ts`, `tests/backup-run.test.ts`, `tests/backup-wiring.test.ts`.

### Documented format with a JSON Schema (#167)
- **Documented format with a JSON Schema (#167).** New `docs/schema/notebook-v19.schema.json` (JSON Schema draft 2020-12, format 19, with the page times `startAt` and `endAt`) describes the `.notebook` file exactly as the plugin writes it: document, meta, pages, strokes, all nine element types, comments, states, slides, groups and locks, every optional field, enum and limit. New `docs/format.md` is the guide for third-party tools: file layout, versioning and migration policy, how to read leniently, the `_assets` and `_meta` files, a minimal example. A test validates real serializer output (a document that uses every element type and optional field) against the schema with a small dependency-free validator, and checks that invalid documents are refused. No format change and no runtime change.

### Interactive HTML export (#164)
- **Interactive HTML export (#164).** *Export…* has a new format, *Interactive HTML*: one self-contained `.html` file (whole notebook, this page or some pages, ink and background choices and *Clean copy* as in the other formats) that opens in any browser without Obsidian. Each page is the inline SVG of the SVG export, with a small vanilla-JS viewer: page strip, wheel and pinch zoom, pan, fit width, keyboard navigation, light and dark by the system theme, links to pages of the notebook (`[[Name#p3]]`) that jump inside the file and web links that open normally. Pictures are embedded up to about 32 MB (a notice says how many were left out); a Content-Security-Policy forbids any network request; titles and text are escaped. The page strip fills its thumbnails only as they scroll into view (a 500-page file opens at once), and printing the file prints every page, one per sheet. Written next to the notebook, never over an existing file; built one page at a time. No format change. Pure builder in `src/ink/export-html.ts`, writer in `src/view/export.ts`. Tests: `tests/export-html.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/htmlexport-check.ts` (`doc=htmlexport`).

### Back-of-book index (#187)
- **Back-of-book index (#187).** *Add to index…* (selection bar and sheet) turns the lasso selection into an ordinary star marker labelled `#idx <term>`; *Back-of-book index…* (page sheet, notebook menu, command palette) lists the terms A-Z with a page chip each (a tap goes to the page), sub-terms written `Term / Subterm` nest, equal terms merge whatever the case or accents, and *Write to note* writes the list to `<Notebook> — Index.md` next to the notebook (only the generated region is rewritten; a note of yours without the markers is left untouched). Settings, Reading: *Index term prefix*. No format change. Pure logic in `src/model/book-index.ts`, dialogs in `src/view/book-index.ts`. Tests: `tests/book-index.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/bookindex-check.ts` (`doc=bookindex`).

### Reusable signature and date stamp (#154)
- **Reusable signature and date stamp (#154).** *Sign and date* (page sheet, Insert; command palette) opens a dialog with your saved signature. *Draw signature* opens a pad (finger, S Pen or mouse; undo, clear) and saves it as the personal stamp "My signature", so it syncs with the vault and shows in the stamp palette; no format change. *Place* puts the signature, scaled to a standard width, and today's date under it where the pen last touched (or the middle of the page in view) as one undo step; the items share one fresh group so they move together, a locked page refuses it. The date follows two new settings (Writing, Advanced): *Signature date format* (your language, ISO, custom) and *Custom signature date pattern*. Pure logic in `src/ink/signature.ts`, storage in `src/storage/signature.ts`, dialogs in `src/view/sign-dialog.ts`. Tests: `tests/signature.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/signstamp-check.ts` (`doc=signstamp`).

### Back to the current slide (#225)
- **Back to the current slide (#225).** While lecture mode is on (or when you write beside a linked slide), the notebook remembers the place you last wrote. Scroll half a screen or more away from it and, once the scroll stops, a small pill at the top of the view offers the way back: *Back to slide N* (the PDF page or linked slide) or *Back to current page*, with an arrow towards it. Tapping it returns like a jump from the notebook map (recorded in the back history) and puts the written line near the middle of the view; the pill can be closed and comes back after you return and leave again. The command *Back to the current slide or page* does the same (no default hotkey). New setting *Back to the current slide* (Study, Timetable; on). The place lives in memory only: no format change, nothing written. Pure logic in `src/model/slide-back.ts`, the pill in `src/view/slide-back.ts`. Tests: `tests/slide-back.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/slideback-check.ts` (`doc=slideback`).

### Recent symbols (#212)
- **Recent symbols (#212).** A strip at the top of the page area with the symbols you use most: typographic ones (∀ ∃ ∫ → ⇒ ≠ ≤ …) and signs you saved with *Save selection as symbol*. Tap one, then tap the page: a character becomes a text box, a saved sign is drawn about one line high in the current pen colour and width (one group, one undo step, fresh ids). Ordered by use with decay (a use counts half as much after two weeks); counts stay on the device. Symbols are small personal stamps, so there is no format change. Settings, Writing: *Recent symbols* and *Symbols shown*.

### Review fixes
- **Dropped files are never trashed after a recorded change.** Once the History has recorded a drop, the steps that follow (layout refresh, page navigation, scrolling to the page) can no longer make the edit report a failure, so the files the notebook already references are never moved to the trash; a failing step is logged.
- **Heading pictures: edited pictures are recognised better.** The checksum of a heading picture now covers every chunk except text and time (so a gamma, colour-profile or transparency edit counts as the user's edit, not only a change of the pixels). Pictures written by 0.20 (image-data checksum) are still recognised.
- **Heading pictures: no silent drop when the names run out.** Up to 24 names are tried; if all are taken, the previous picture stays linked in the companion note and one notice per session explains why.
- **Reference window keeps its page.** When the lending pane switches to another notebook or closes, the window reads the file and keeps its current page if it still exists, instead of jumping to page 1.
- **Heading pictures: narrower overwrite window.** A stale picture of ours is rewritten only if the file's size and time are still the ones probed, checked right before the write; otherwise the picture takes a new free name.
- **Backups never lose the backup just written.** The retention always keeps the zip a run has just made. A backup whose name is dated more than a day ahead (a device with a wrong clock) counts as the oldest instead of pushing the new one out, so *Backups to keep: 1* can no longer leave you with no current backup.
- **Backup folder cannot hide your notebooks.** The folder is refused (a notice, the previous folder stays) when it is the notebooks folder, holds it, sits inside it (`_assets` too), overlaps a templates folder or holds notebook files. A run with such a folder, or one whose plan has no notebook although the vault has some, now ends as failed instead of "ok" with nothing in it. Settings synced from another device fall back to the default folder.
- **Backups: old files are trashed only when they are ours.** Before an old backup goes to the trash, the plugin reads the zip comment it wrote; a file you copied there under a backup's name is left alone. A leftover temporary file is removed only when it carries the mark or this device wrote it.
- **Backups of a notebook that is being saved.** The size and time of a notebook are checked before and after it is read; if they changed (an autosave overlapped) it is read once more, and a file that keeps changing is skipped and the backup is marked partial, instead of storing a cut-off copy as complete.
- **Backups on phones and tablets.** The limit is now 64 MB (the zip needs about twice that in memory); a vault over the limit fails with "too large for one backup on this device" and nothing is read. Computers keep 1 GB.
- **Index note belongs to one notebook.** The generated region of a "Notebook — Index" note records the notebook it was written for. Another notebook whose name collapses to the same file name (for example `Bio  1` and `Bio 1`) writes to `… — Index (2).md` instead of replacing the first one's list.
- **Heading pictures stay intact on devices still on 0.20.** New pictures also carry the 0.20 checksum, so a device that has not updated no longer treats them as edited and piles up numbered copies. When all names are taken, the other names are not read again in the same session.
- **A recorded step is always saved.** The save is scheduled before the layout and viewport refresh that follow a change, so a refresh that fails can no longer leave a recorded step unsaved.
- **Page times (format v19), review round.** Undoing a merge no longer loses a stamp made after it: the merge's page-times step undoes only while the times are still what it set, and redo widens the current times, so the end stays at or after every ink on the page. A copy that differs from the notebook only by a page's start or end now counts as a change to merge (the dialog, the *Merge* button and recovery agree, and recovery keeps the widest span). Clock readings before 2015 or after 2100 (a device that booted without network time) are ignored when stamping. Editing the text of an element (text box, sticky note, marker, box title, equation, checkbox label) now moves the page's last-written time; moving or restyling does not, and ink moved in from another page, or made from an equation, no longer stamps the page it lands on. The stamp runs after the step is recorded and is guarded, so a clock error never costs an undo step. Comments in `page-times.ts` match the behaviour.
- **Index note keeps its notebook.** The index note now also records the notebook's document id, so renaming or moving the notebook and its folder no longer leaves the note behind and starts a numbered copy; a note whose recorded path is gone is taken back at the notebook's own index name. Older markers still work.
- **Backups.** An old backup larger than this device's cap is never read or trashed (a phone no longer loads a desktop backup to check it). The per-device `_meta` files get the same changed-while-read check as notebooks.
- **Backup folder setting.** The folder is checked and saved when you finish typing, not on every keystroke, so a refused name no longer leaves a half-typed folder behind. The retention text says plainly that a plugin backup in that folder is trashed when it is old.
- **Heading pictures.** When every picture name is taken, the old picture that stays linked is no longer remembered as current, so a name you free is used on the next write.

## 0.20.0 — 2026-11-05

Eight features: groups and locked elements, a quick share of a page or a selection, files dragged straight onto a page, ghost guide lines on blank paper, headings found from the size of the handwriting, a floating reference window, a planner generator and a visual version diff. The notebook format moves to version 18 (groups and locks): the migration from version 17 is automatic, and 0.19 and older open a v18 file read-only and never overwrite it.

### Groups and locked elements (#39) and document format 18
- **Groups and locked elements (#39), document format 18.** *Group* in the selection bar makes the selected strokes and elements one group: picking any member with the lasso, or tapping a grouped element or grouped ink, picks them all, so they move, resize, rotate, copy, duplicate, cut and delete together. *Ungroup* splits it. *Lock* fixes the selected elements in place (a background picture that must not move): the lasso, a tap and the area eraser skip a locked element, ink over it is unaffected. A tap with the lasso on a locked element shows a small *Locked · Unlock* chip; *Locked elements…* in the page menu lists them with *Unlock* and *Unlock all*. Each change is one undo step and a locked page still refuses them.
- **Document format v18.** Strokes and elements get an optional `group` id; elements an optional `locked` flag and `lockAt` time. A group is two members or more on one page; a smaller one is released when read (counted) and never written. The v17 to v18 migration only drops such keys from a v17 file (large v17 files still stage); 0.19 and older open a v18 file read-only and never overwrite it. Copies of a page or a selection and templates get fresh group ids and keep locks. Merging a conflicted copy unites the groups by id and the later lock decision wins (a lock tie keeps the lock).

### Visual version diff (#121)
- **Visual version diff (#121).** *Compare this notebook with a previous version* (command palette, and *Compare with a previous version…* in the page sheet) and *Compare two previous versions* open a read-only dialog that shows, page by page, what changed: a page list (added, removed, modified, moved; counts of ink and elements), a preview of the chosen page in three views (*Before*, *Overlay*, *After*) with added ink in green, removed ink ghosted in red and moved or changed items outlined in amber, and arrows between changed pages. Pages are matched by page id, strokes and elements by their ids and compared by geometry and style ("moved" when only the place changed). Only the versions compared are read and parsed; the work is capped for very large notebooks (the pages beyond the cap read *Not compared*) and each page is drawn on demand into a small cache of canvases at dialog size. *Restore this page from <date>* goes through the existing page-restore path: one undo step in the open notebook, a locked page is refused, and a confirmation comes first; a page deleted since is inserted again after its neighbour. PDF and picture backgrounds are not drawn in the preview. No format change. Pure logic in `src/model/version-diff.ts` and `src/model/diff-restore.ts`, drawing in `src/ink/diff-render.ts`, dialog in `src/view/version-diff-dialog.ts`. Tests: `tests/version-diff.test.ts`, `tests/diff-render.test.ts`, `tests/diff-restore.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/vdiff-check.ts` (`doc=vdiff`).

### Headings from handwriting size (#102)
- **Headings from the size of the handwriting (#102).** Lines written clearly larger than the page's usual letters are headings (1.7x: second level, 2.4x: first level); a line only 1.25x larger counts when a straight stroke is drawn under it (slanted lines and slanted underlines too). Colour is not used. They are listed in the outline (as before, now also finding underlined titles) and, new, in the notebook map: a *Titles* filter chip with the number of pages that have headings and up to two pictures of the ink on each card; tapping a heading, in the outline or the map, goes to the line, not only to the page. Pictures are drawn only for cards in view, from a bounded cache. No OCR, no format change. Thresholds are documented in `src/ink/headings.ts`.
- **Idle analysis.** The analysis is cached per page by a fingerprint of the page's content (`src/model/heading-index.ts`; rebuilt strokes with the same content are not analysed again) and runs in 8 ms slices at idle moments a few seconds after a change, never while the pen is down (`src/view/heading-warm.ts`).
- **Headings as pictures in the companion note.** New setting *Headings as pictures* (under *Companion notes*, off by default): under the `### p. N` heading of a page, `- heading: ![[…]]` lines with the picture of each heading (up to 4 per page, 60 per notebook). Pictures are saved in `<Notebook> (Headings)` beside the notebook as `p3-h1.png`, rewritten only when their bytes change, recorded as plugin writes and never deleted; nothing is drawn while a pen is down.

### Planner generator (#118)
- **Planner generator (#118).** The creation dialog has a third tab, *Planner*: choose the year (a calendar year, or an academic year from September to August, or any start month), the language of the names (English or Italian), the first day of the week, the page size and orientation, and which sections to make: a year overview with twelve mini calendars, a calendar page per month, a spread per week (the weekly planner paper), a page per day (the daily planner paper, with hours) and notes pages. The dialog shows the page count and warns above 200 pages (daily pages add 365). Titles, weekday names, dates and hours are typed text boxes and the rules are paper (month pages sit on grid paper whose spacing is the calendar cell), so there is no ink in a new planner. Pages link to each other, year to month to week to day and back, with the notebook's page links (`[[Planner 2026.notebook#p14&id=d71|…]]` in a text box, which stay right when pages are moved), and the year, every month and the notes get an outline bookmark. The link text shows on the page, as every link written in a text box does. A new setting *Planner week starts on* (Automatic follows the device language). No format change; the pages are built in one pass (a few milliseconds for a year of day pages). Pure calendar math (leap years, ISO and Sunday-start week numbers, academic ranges, month grids) in `src/model/calendar.ts`, the generator in `src/model/planner.ts`; tests `tests/calendar.test.ts`, `tests/planner.test.ts`; harness `doc=planner`.

### Floating reference window (#107)
- **Floating reference window (#107).** A small window floats over the page while you write and shows a reference, read only: a page of this or another notebook (drawn like a thumbnail: ink, PDF background, pictures, text), a PDF page, a picture of the vault or a Markdown note. Open it from the page sheet (*Show in reference window*), the command palette (*Open a reference window…*), the selection bar of a text box that holds a link (*Open as reference*), the preview card of a page link and the file menu (*Open as reference*). Drag it by its header, resize it from the corner, fade it with the slider, minimise it to a chip or close it; fingers pan and pinch-zoom the reference, the S Pen does nothing inside it and writes normally outside it. Its place, size and opacity are kept on the device (one place per orientation) and it stays inside the page area after a rotation. The picture is drawn at the window's size and again only after a resize or a zoom has ended. New setting *Reference window snaps to the edges* (Reading). No document format change.

### Drag files onto a page (#79)
- **Drag files straight onto a page (#79).** Drop pictures (PNG, JPG, WebP, GIF) on the page: they are stored in the assets folder and placed at the drop point in page coordinates, several files in a cascade, as one undo step, and selected so they can be moved at once. Drop a PDF: choose *Insert pages after this one*, *Insert as images on this page* (the first 8 pages) or *Annotate in a new notebook*. Files dragged from Obsidian's file explorer work too (pictures and PDFs are copied into the assets folder; a note or notebook becomes a `[[link]]` text box). A dashed frame names the target page while you drag; a locked page or a read-only notebook shows a blocked frame and refuses. Anything not taken (other types, HEIC photos, empty or too large files, more than 20) is named in one notice. No format change.

### Quick share of a page (#30)
- **Quick share of a page (#30).** *Share page…* (the page menu, the page sheet and the command palette) and *Share selection…* (the selection menu) open a small dialog: what to share (the page, the selection or some pages), the format (PNG image or PDF) and the *Clean copy* switch of 0.19. The files are drawn by the export pipeline (the same page renderer, PDF writer and clean-copy filter as *Export…*) in memory, then handed to the system share sheet with `navigator.share({ files })` when `navigator.canShare({ files })` says the device takes them (Android WebView, Chrome). Nothing is written in the vault on that path, and closing the share sheet writes nothing and shows no error. Where file sharing is not available (desktop, an old WebView), or the share sheet refuses the files, a copy is saved with the export naming rules (next to the notebook for a PDF, in the notebook's *(PNG)* folder for images) and a notice offers *Open* and, for a single image, *Copy image*. If the browser wants a fresh tap because drawing took a while, the notice offers *Share* with the files kept in memory. A selection is always one PNG of its region; a PNG share is at most 10 pages (use PDF for more); a file above 25 MB gets a warning. The last format, scope and clean choice are remembered per device (local storage, three fields, never synced). No format change; the stroke pipeline is untouched. Pure logic in `src/model/share-page.ts` (tests: `tests/share-page.test.ts`), dialog and flow in `src/view/share-page.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/share-check.ts` (`doc=share`). The real share sheet needs hardware verification.

### Ghost guide lines (#94)
- **Ghost guide lines on blank paper (#94).** After a first line of handwriting on blank paper, two faint dashed guides (the baseline of the line you wrote and the next one) appear near the pen and fade after a short pause. The baseline and line height are inferred from the newest strokes when a stroke ends (drawings and descenders are ignored, a slightly tilted line is followed); nothing runs per frame. Settings: *Ghost guide lines* (off / while writing / always on blank paper) and *Guide spacing* (auto or 6-10 mm). Overlay only on the live layer: never saved, exported or printed. No format change. Pure logic in `src/model/ghost-lines.ts` (tests: `tests/ghost-lines.test.ts`). Harness: `dev/harness/ghostlines-check.ts` (`doc=ghostlines`).

### Review fixes
- **Links in text boxes show their label.** A text box that holds `[[Note|alias]]` (the planner's page links, links typed with the pen) now shows only the alias, or a short name when there is none (`Folder/Plan.notebook#p3` shows `Plan p. 3`), in the link colour with an underline, on the page, in thumbnails, in PNG, SVG and PDF exports. The box layout, its bounds and its tap areas follow what is shown; editing the box still shows the raw markup and the file keeps it. The planner sizes its link fonts from the label. `src/ink/wikilink.ts`.
- **Companion headings.** Analysis reuses the open notebook's warm index, or keeps one per notebook (a few, least recently used dropped) and runs in idle slices that look at the pen first. A heading picture is marked with the ink it shows (a PNG text chunk): the same mark is left alone (no rewrite on every device and session), another mark is redrawn, and a file without the mark is the user's and is never written over (the picture takes the next free name, `p3-h1 2.png`). The pictures are no longer recorded one by one (the plugin-written record is bounded); the `(Headings)` folder is recognised by name in the shared-files inbox.
- **Drop files.** *Insert as images* no longer stores the PDF in the assets folder; the pages are drawn from memory. Nothing is written until the range is chosen and the History is about to take the change; if it refuses or a write fails, the files this drop created are moved to the trash again (files that already existed are never touched). The same applies to dropped pictures.
- **Version diff restore** only restores into the notebook the versions belong to. **Share page** keeps the file name of a scattered range short (`Maths p1-199 (100 pages).pdf`). **Planner** links name the notebook created after `{{year}}`-style placeholders are filled in. **Ghost guides** redraw on a new stroke only when they had started to fade. **Reference window** shows another pane's open notebook from that pane's live document, and reads a changed file only when the pen is idle and the window is not minimised.
- **Review fixes, round 2.** Selecting the dropped elements now happens after the History took the change, so a selection that fails can no longer move the dropped pictures to the trash. The reference window stops showing another pane's notebook once that pane opens a different one (it reads the file from disk instead). A heading picture that was cropped or annotated in an editor that keeps the PNG text chunks is no longer rewritten: the plugin also stores a checksum of the pixels it wrote and takes the next free name when they differ. Textbook page-reference chips are placed on the text as drawn (a link shows its label) and skip references inside a link label. In PDF export an emoji before a link no longer shifts the coloured and underlined span. The shared files inbox hides a `(Headings)` folder only when its notebook sits beside it, so your own folder with that name keeps showing its files.
- Groups and locks: merging a conflicted copy that only differs in locks or groups is now counted as a change (the conflict dialog no longer says "nothing to merge" over a working Merge button, and the recovery union keeps the other candidate's locks and groups). The merge brings the flags with one command per page instead of one per item, so a large drawing no longer takes seconds to merge, undo or redo.
- Groups and locks: a merge no longer puts an item into a group of one (the copy was offered again on every open); `mergeCovers` checks the lock before the conflicted copy is moved to backup.
- Groups and locks: a cross-page drag and *Split page* no longer carry a group id to another page. Members that arrive together form a new group, a lone one leaves its group. *Split page* keeps locked elements on the page and keeps a group whole on one side.
- Groups and locks: a locked marker, box or equation no longer opens its editor (so it cannot be deleted from there). Selections made by find, select similar, the states tools and stamps are completed to their groups. The pieces of a cut stroke stay in its group only while another member exists, and the repair summary no longer counts an invalid group twice.

## 0.19.0 — 2026-11-05

Handwritten tasks and a lecture you can follow up from your notes: checkboxes drawn with the pen that tick the tasks in your Markdown notes, a lecture log line in the daily note and an automatic snapshot at the end of each lecture, a gallery of the figures of a lecture, a clean copy of the notes to share, a list of things to look up later, typed text that Obsidian's search can find, and notebooks made from note templates. The notebook format moves to version 17: 0.18 and older open a v17 file read-only and never overwrite it.

### Handwritten checklists (#103) and document format 17
- **Handwritten checklists (#103), document format 17.** A small square drawn with the pen is offered as a checkbox (a *Make checkbox* chip next to it; the setting *Squares drawn as checkboxes* can also make it one at once, or leave it as ink). A checkbox can also be inserted from the Insert sheet. A tap with the pen, a mouse or a finger ticks it (one undo step, refused on a locked page); the tick is drawn above the ink and in exports. The handwriting on the same line to its right is its label: *Select with text* selects both, and the crop of a task is the box and its line.
- **Send to tasks.** *Send to tasks…* on a selected checkbox adds `- [ ] <label> ![[picture]] [[page]]` to the daily note, the tasks note or a chosen note (the same code and dialog as *Make task*, with an optional text), ending in a block id `^nb-…` that the plugin wrote. Ticking the checkbox (or undoing, redoing, merging) then ticks that one line of the note and nothing else; if the line is really gone (the note was edited since the link was made and read twice, a minute apart, without it) the checkbox is unlinked with a notice. *Unlink task* stops the link.
- **Open items.** The notebook map has an *Open items* filter: the pages that still have an unticked checkbox, with the count on the badge.
- **Document format v17.** A new page element `checkbox` (place, size, colour, optional tick, tick time, typed label and task link), one line. The v16 to v17 migration only refuses a checkbox in a v16 file (large v16 files still stage); 0.18 and older open a v17 file read-only and never overwrite it. Read leniently (an invalid detail is dropped and counted), written strictly, repaired from the line scan. Copies of a page or a selection keep the tick but never the task link; notebooks and pages made from or saved as templates are unticked and unlinked. Merging a conflicted copy keeps one checkbox and the later toggle wins.

### Clean copy export (#239)
- **Clean copy for exports (#239).** The export dialog has a *Clean copy* switch: the PDF, SVG, PNG and Print leave out the study and working layers — placeholders and the other markers (questions, importance, homework), margin comments (and the PDF comment notes) and collapsed sticky notes together with the ink they hide — so the lecture is ready to share; *Also leave out open sticky notes* is an option. Boxes, text, pictures, equations, the page header and invisible ink (still governed by *Show solutions*) are kept. The page model is filtered by the pure `cleanPageForExport` before drawing; the notebook is never changed, the format is unchanged and an export without the switch is byte-identical to before. Choosing the pages of "this lecture" is still done with *Some pages*.

### Figures gallery (#228)
- **Figures gallery (#228).** *Figures…* (All actions, the notebook menu and the command palette) opens a grid of every picture placed on the pages (photos, board photos, imported pictures) and every anchored crop that holds a snapshot. A scope switch picks *This section* (the lecture of the page in view; offered only when some page starts a lecture), *Whole notebook* or *Some pages* (typed as 1-3, 5), and kind chips (All / Photos / Crops) show counts. Each card has a page badge; a tap goes to the figure on its page and outlines it for a moment, a long press (or the card's *more* button, or a right click) offers *Copy image*, *Insert into note…* (the 0.18 picture-into-note path, drawn from the figure's place on the page) and *Open original*. Thumbnails are made lazily from the stored files, only for cards in view, one at a time and only when you are not writing, decoded small and kept in a bounded cache (60). Read-only: it never writes the notebook. No format change. Pure logic in `src/model/figures.ts` (tests: `tests/figures.test.ts`), dialog in `src/view/figures.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/figures-check.ts` (`doc=figures`).

### Look up later (#213)
- **Look up later (#213).** *Look up later…* in the selection menu (and the palette) takes a picture of the selection, asks for an optional typed word and appends one line to a Markdown note: `- [ ] ![[crop|150]] word — [[Notebook#p3&id=…|Notebook p. 3]] (date)`. The note is *Look up later.md* in the notebooks folder by default (setting *Look up later note*, made on the first entry), the picture width is a setting, and only a line is ever appended: the rest of the note is untouched, typed text is escaped, and the same line is not added twice. The *Look up later list…* sheet (page sheet, notebook menu, palette) lists this notebook's entries read from the note, with their picture, count and a jump to the page; it is read-only (tick entries off in the note). No format change. Not added as a pen gesture: the gesture system lives in the input files this wave does not touch.

### Typed text in search (O19)
- Companion notes: new setting *Include typed text in search* (off by default). The companion note also lists, per page, the text of sticky notes, margin comments and replies, equation sources, marker labels and the typed labels of checkboxes (with ☐ / ☑ for the state), with a wikilink back to the page, so Obsidian search finds them. Text is escaped, bounded to 4000 characters per page, and written only inside the generated block. No format change.

### Notebooks from note templates (O13)
- **Notebooks from note templates (O13).** The public API (now 1.2.0) has `api.newNotebook({ name, folder?, template?, content?, open? })`, which creates a notebook, optionally from a notebook template, and returns ready-to-paste `link` and `embed` Markdown; `createNotebook` gained the `template` and `templateContent` options. A fenced `notebook-new` block (`name="{{title}} sketches"`, `template="Lecture"`) survives core Templates and Templater and shows a *Create notebook* card; only a press creates the notebook, and then only that block is replaced by `![[…]]`, through `vault.process` with an exact match (no match, or two identical blocks that cannot be told apart: nothing is created). Copy-paste examples for core Templates and Templater in `docs/templates.md`. No format change. Pure parsing and replacement in `src/integration/new-block.ts`. Tests: `tests/new-block.test.ts`.

### Lecture snapshots (#222)
- **Automatic snapshot at the end of a lecture (#222).** When a lecture ends, the notebook keeps a version of itself named after the course, shown in *Restore a previous version* as "Lecture <course> <date> <hh:mm>" with a graduation-cap mark. A lecture ends when lecture mode is left (by you or by the clock), when its class is over, or when the notebook is closed, after you wrote during a timetable class (the same end that writes the lecture log line; switching to another app does not end a lecture, it only saves). The view saves first and the snapshot is made from the file on disk, never from memory; nothing is taken if the save did not finish, if you did not write, or if the notebook holds what its newest snapshot already holds (a fingerprint that ignores the modified time). It goes through the same history folder as every snapshot (`<id>/<YYYYMMDD-HHmm>-lecture.snapshot`; the course is kept in a small lenient `lectures.json` beside them, never rewritten if unreadable). Version history has a *Lecture snapshots* filter when the notebook has any. No format change.
- **Settings (Library).** *Snapshot at the end of a lecture* (on by default: it only does anything for a notebook linked to a timetable class, and the existing interval snapshots are on by default too) and *Lecture snapshots to keep* (default 20 per notebook). Only this feature's own `-lecture` snapshots beyond the count move to the trash (through Obsidian's trash path); ordinary snapshots and their *Versions to keep* count are never touched by it, and it never touches them. Pure logic in `src/storage/lecture-snapshot.ts`, the list pieces in `src/view/history-ui.ts`. Tests: `tests/lecture-snapshot.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/snapshot-check.ts` (`doc=snapshot`).

### Lecture log (#221)
- **Lecture log in the daily note (#221).** With the new setting *Lecture log in the daily note* on (off by default), when a lecture you wrote in ends (lecture mode left or its class over, or the notebook closed during the class's time slot) one line goes into the daily note of that day: `- 10:15–11:45 **Analysis I** · 3 pages · 2 open questions — [[Analysis I#p12&id=…|pp. 12–15]] ^nb-lec-…`. The pages run from the lecture page to the one before the next dated page; open questions are the unresolved question markers on them. The line goes under a configurable heading (*Lecture log heading*, default "## Lectures", added at the end of the note when missing). One line per notebook and class occurrence: it carries a block id the plugin added, and a session that goes on later rewrites exactly that line through `vault.process` (every other byte of the note stays). It follows the daily-note settings: a missing daily note is made only with *Create the daily note if missing*; otherwise the line waits in this device's local storage (bounded: 30 lines, 21 days) and is written once the note exists. No format change. Pure logic in `src/library/lecture-log-logic.ts`, writer in `src/library/lecture-log.ts`. Tests: `tests/lecture-log.test.ts`.

### Review fixes
- Lecture log: a session that goes on rewrites only the part of the line the plugin generated (time, course, counts, link). A quote mark, a task box or list marker you put in front of it, and words you added after the link, stay; a copy inside a code fence is never touched; a line you turned into something else is left alone (nothing is rewritten or added).
- Lecture log: a written line stays in the device's waiting list until a later read of the note, a few seconds on, shows it; a template engine (Templater) that overwrites a brand new daily note after the line was written no longer loses it silently (it is written again, up to 3 times). The vault events that watch the waiting notes are registered once the layout is ready and no longer parse the waiting list on every file.
- Lecture log: turning *Create the daily note if missing* on later no longer makes the daily notes of past days (only the class's own day); a course name with `%%`, `<!--`, `#tag`, `$…$`, `==` or a trailing backslash can no longer comment out or tag the line; the line's id follows the notebook's document id, so renaming or moving the notebook between two ends of a class rewrites the same line instead of adding a second one.
- Lecture end: writing up to 10 minutes after a class's time slot still counts for that class (the lecture log and the snapshot are brought up to date). Switching apps during a class makes no lecture end, log call or snapshot; the one end comes when the mode is left, the class is over or the notebook closes.
- Storage cleanup: lecture snapshots no longer count in the "newest N" window of the ordinary snapshots and are never offered for the trash there; their own *Lecture snapshots to keep* count governs them. A lecture snapshot that is due while an interval snapshot of the same notebook is running waits instead of being skipped.
- Restore a previous version: the list opens from the file names and sizes and no longer reads and parses every copy (no page count in the rows); the chosen copy is checked when you restore it.
- Look up later: the sheet picks the entries of this notebook across the whole shared note before the 500-entry cap and keeps the newest, so a busy shared list no longer hides new entries.
- Notebooks from note templates: the *Create notebook* card shows no button inside the Templates or Templater folder (it is the template's own text); once the notebook exists the button never comes back, and a failure to replace the block shows the link instead; the block it replaces is fixed when you press the button, not by a line number that typing above can shift. `createNotebook` with `open` no longer fails when only opening the new notebook fails.
- Companion search text: bare web addresses (`https://…`, `www.…`) are broken so they do not become links, and the search text of all pages together is capped at 200,000 characters per note (the cut is said once).
- Checklists: a long label no longer blocks saving. Sending a checkbox to the tasks with a text of 201 to 300 characters whose 200th character was a space left a label ending in a space, which the writer refused on every save; the label is now trimmed again after it is cut to 200 characters.
- Checklists: a tick waiting to be written into its task line is no longer lost when you switch app, close the tab or the notebook reloads within the first second: it is noted at once, written when the app is hidden or the notebook closes, kept across a reload, and tried again (4 s, 15 s, 1 min, 5 min) when the write fails.
- Checklists: a task note that is not in the vault yet (the notebook synced before the note did) or was renamed no longer unlinks the checkbox. The link stays and the tick is retried; a renamed or moved note, or a renamed folder, is followed. Only a note that exists, was edited since the link was made and no longer has the line, twice, unlinks the checkbox (see below).
- Checklists: *Duplicate* in the Library gives the copy checkboxes without links to the original's task lines (the ticks stay), so ticking the copy no longer rewrites the original's note.
- Checklists: moving pages to another notebook (move, split, merge into the trash, restore from the page trash) keeps the links of their checkboxes; only real copies drop them.
- Checklists: merging an older copy no longer links again a checkbox you unlinked: linking and unlinking are stamped (`taskAt`, optional, still format 17) and the later decision wins.
- Checklists: a finger tap on a checkbox is ignored while the pen is down or was just used, so a palm resting on the page between strokes no longer ticks it.
- Checklists: the pen ticks a checkbox only on the box itself and 3 pt around it (a finger keeps its larger target), so a dot or a colon written right beside the box stays ink.
- Checklists: the task line is found more safely: copies inside fenced code blocks and the frontmatter are skipped, a task in a callout or quote works, and when two lines carry the same id nothing is changed and a notice says so.
- Checklists: when two copies disagree about a tick at the same time (or both lack a time), the tick wins, and a copy is not reported as covered while its tick is missing, so *Move only* no longer sends a tick to the backups.
- Checklists: the merge summary lists ticked and relinked checkboxes (and resolved markers).
- Checklists: a task line that is gone is reported only when the checkbox was really unlinked; a refused unlink (locked page, read-only notebook) tells nothing and is tried again.
- Checklists: the *Send to tasks* text no longer says ticking the task in the note ticks the checkbox (ticks go from the notebook to the note only).
- Checklists: in the setting *Make it a checkbox*, a square is no longer converted while you are still drawing a tick across it; it waits for the stroke and checks again.
- Checklists: a note path too long to store is refused with a notice when sending a checkbox to the tasks, instead of blocking saves.
- Checklists: the *Open items* tooltip follows a changed label.
- Checklists: an older copy of the note that has not received the task line yet (the notebook synced first) no longer unlinks the checkbox. A line counts as gone only when the note was edited after the link was made and it is read without the line twice, at least a minute apart; otherwise the link stays and the tick is tried again.
- Checklists: a code block that never closes above the task line (for example one still being typed on another device) no longer makes the line read as deleted.
- Checklists: a note renamed to a path too long to store keeps the old link, with one notice, instead of making every later save fail.
- Checklists: following a renamed note is no longer an undo step (Undo does not point the link back to the old name), and it is also followed when the notebook was closed at the time: the renames of notes are remembered on this device for 30 days (a small bounded list, never synced) and consulted when the notebook opens and before a tick finds no note.
- Checklists: the automatic unlink of a deleted task line is no longer an undo step either, so Undo right after a tick un-ticks it. Both are still saved with the notebook.
- Checklists: undoing or redoing a move, a move to another page or a margin change of a linked checkbox after its link followed a renamed note (or was dropped) no longer makes a second copy of the checkbox, which stopped every later save of the notebook. The steps in the undo history are told what the upkeep change replaced and keep the link it left. As a backstop, an undo or redo that would leave a notebook the writer refuses (two items with one id, for example) is not done: the notebook stays as it was, the step leaves the undo history and a notice says so; the edit guard now also refuses duplicate ids. Redoing a tick or a resolved marker puts back the very same object, so the steps above it still find it.
- Checklists: a link that follows a renamed note keeps the time it was made, so a task line deleted from a note edited since the link (and renamed while the notebook was closed) is reported as gone, not as a missing note. The notice about a new path too long to follow is shown once per checkbox in a session, not on every retry.
- Undo and Redo: a refused redo also clears the redo steps made on top of it; a step that throws is taken back exactly (the step is dropped and the same notice shown, nothing is thrown into the view); a notebook that already failed the check before the step is no longer blamed on the step, so the step is kept.
- Lecture log: a line is written again after a template engine overwrote the note only within two minutes of the write. Later (after a restart, or when the app was suspended) the line counts as settled, so a line you deleted or moved is not put back, and a confirm never makes a daily note.

## 0.18.0 — 2026-11-05

Review a page and move between notes more freely: margin comments anchored to a region of the page, an infinite page split into sheets of paper for export, a selection sent into a Markdown note, pages copied to another notebook with a link back, a QR code for a page, the notes that mention a page, annotating a PDF from Obsidian's PDF viewer, and quick tabs for the pages you keep going back to. The notebook format moves to version 16 (an older plugin opens a v16 file read-only).

### Margin comments (#86) and document format 16
- **Margin comments (#86), document format 16.** *Comment* on a selection, or *Comment on a region* (a rectangle drawn on the page), anchors a comment to a region of the page: typed text, optionally a small handwritten sketch, the name of the device that wrote it, created and edited times, a resolved flag and a flat thread of replies. Each comment is a numbered marker in the page margin joined to its region; a tap highlights the region and opens its card (reply, resolve or reopen, edit, delete). The *Comments panel* lists them with an Open / Resolved / All filter and jumps to the region. Every change is one undo step through the history and is refused on a locked page.
- **Document format v16.** A new optional page field `comments`, one comment or reply per line. The v15 to v16 migration only drops a stray `comments` key (large v15 files still stage); 0.17 and older open a v16 file read-only and never overwrite it. Read leniently (an invalid comment is dropped and counted, a region outside the page is moved into it, a reply whose comment is gone becomes a comment of its own), written strictly, repaired from the line scan. Merging a conflicted copy keeps comments and replies from both devices; for one comment edited on both the later edit wins and, when both changed the text, the losing version is kept beside it as a marked "⚠" item and counted in the merge summary. Comments travel with a duplicated, moved or copied page and are joined when a page version is restored; they do not travel with a copied selection, and templates, notebooks made from templates and practice attempts carry none.
- **Exports.** With the setting *Include comments in exports* on (off by default) the PDF export adds the comments as margin notes (a strip beside each page that has comments, with the region outlined and joined to its note) and the Markdown export lists each page's comments (numbered, author, time, resolved, replies). With it off neither export carries a comment, because the list names the devices that wrote them.
- **Settings.** *Show comment markers* (on by default) and *Include comments in exports* (off by default).

### Split an infinite page into sheets (#178)
- **Split an infinite page into sheets of paper (#178).** The export dialog (PDF and PNG) has *Split into sheets* when the pages to export include an infinite page: paper (A4, Letter, A5), portrait or landscape, size on paper (fit the width of the content, or 100%), margin, overlap, *Cut between lines*, page numbers (PDF) and *Leave out empty sheets*. A preview shows the page picture with the sheets drawn over it, redrawn on every change (the picture is drawn once per dialog). One PDF page per sheet in reading order (left to right, then down), numbered "1/6" when asked; PNG writes one image per sheet (`page-001-part-01.png`…). Smart cuts move each horizontal cut into the white space between lines of ink, using stroke and element bounding boxes, and avoid text boxes, pictures, boxes, equations and notes when a gap is near. The PDF writer draws the page once as a form and each sheet clips and places it, so the file does not grow with the number of sheets. Other pages of the export are written as before; output without the option is byte-identical (tested). No format change. Pure logic in `src/ink/export-tiles.ts`. Tests: `tests/export-tiles.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/a4split-check.ts` (`doc=a4split`).

### Selection into a Markdown note (#59)
- **Selection into a note (#59).** The selection bar (and All actions) has *Insert into note* and *Insert into note as SVG*. The selected region is drawn like the task picture (PNG, or the page SVG cropped to the region), stored where Obsidian keeps attachments of the note, and `![[picture|400]] [[Notebook#pN&id=…|↩]]` is inserted at the cursor of the Markdown note you were last in, through the editor so undo in the note works. With no note open a picker offers one and the block is appended at the end. Settings (Integration): picture width, background (transparent or paper) and the link back. No format change. Pure text in `src/model/note-insert.ts`; tests: `tests/note-insert.test.ts`.

### Copy to notebook with a link back (#186)
- **Copy to notebook with a link back (#186).** *Copy to notebook…* (page menu, command palette, and the notebook map's *Select pages* bar) copies the ticked pages into another notebook, at the start, after a page or at the end, through the same careful transfer as merge and extract (new page ids, pictures, PDFs, slide links, bookmarks and states travel; an open destination takes the pages as one undo step). Every copy gets a small grey link in its top margin, *Copied from <notebook> p.N*, that opens the original page; with *Also add “Copied to …”* ticked (its starting state is the new setting *Link on the original when copying a page*) the unlocked originals get *Copied to <notebook> p.N* in one undoable edit. Locked originals are left alone and counted in the notice. The chips are ordinary text boxes holding one page link, so there is no format change. Pure planning in `src/library/copy-with-link-logic.ts`, the run in `src/library/copy-with-link.ts`, the dialog in `src/library/copy-with-link-dialog.ts`; `copyPagesInto` is the one new export of `src/library/page-transfer.ts`. Tests: `tests/copy-with-link.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/linkback-check.ts` (`doc=linkback`).

### Page QR code (#190)
- **Page QR code (#190).** *Show QR code* in the page menu opens a dialog with a large QR code (black on white, crisp at any size and pixel ratio, drawn as one SVG path) of this page's `obsidian://notebook?vault=…&path=…&page=…&id=…` link. Scanning it with the camera of a phone or tablet that has the same vault opens the notebook at that page (by page id first); the existing `notebook` protocol handler is reused, so no new handler and no new parameters. *Copy link* puts the same link on the clipboard (also the way out when a very long path does not fit a code, which holds up to 213 bytes). The encoder is written for the plugin (byte mode, level M, versions 1-10) in `src/model/qr.ts`, so there is no third-party code or licence. No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/qr.test.ts` (Reed-Solomon vector, format/version bits, round trip through a separate decoder at every version). Harness: `dev/harness/qr-check.ts` (`doc=qr`).

### Mentions (O7)
- **Mentions (O7).** *Mentions* (page sheet, notebook menu, command palette) lists the notes that link to the page in view, and in a second group the notes that link to the notebook without a page (or to a page that no longer exists). Each row shows the note name, its folder and one line of context around the link; a tap opens the note at that line in a new tab. A small count badge at the top left of the page area (a button that opens the same sheet) shows how many links name the page in view. Links are found by page id first (`#p3&id=...`), then by number (`#p3`), with the same parser as the hover preview; embeds count too. The list is read from Obsidian's link index (`metadataCache.resolvedLinks` and each note's links and embeds) and refreshed, debounced, when the index changes; a note's text is read only to show the context line (cached read, notes over 1 MB skipped, 60 rows per group). Read-only: it never writes, no change to the notebook format. Pure logic in `src/view/mentions-logic.ts` (tests: `tests/mentions-logic.test.ts`), glue and sheet in `src/view/mentions.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/mentions-check.ts` (`doc=mentions`).

### Annotate from Obsidian's PDF viewer (O10)
- **Annotate in Notebook from Obsidian's PDF viewer (O10).** A pen icon in the header of every PDF view, the PDF's tab / "more options" menu and file explorer menu entry, and the command *Annotate current PDF in Notebook* all do the same: if a notebook already has pages on this PDF (found by the PDF's content hash, the asset id the import stores) a dialog offers *Open existing (page N)* or *New notebook*; otherwise the single-PDF import makes a new notebook (same asset storage, same page-size reading). The notebook opens at the page the PDF viewer shows when that can be read (best effort, else page 1). The source PDF is only read. No format change. Pure search logic in `src/library/annotate-pdf-logic.ts`, glue in `src/library/annotate-pdf.ts`. Tests: `tests/annotate-pdf.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/annotate-check.ts` (`doc=annotate`).

### Quick tabs (#227)
- **Quick tabs (#227).** Pin the pages you keep going back to (the formula sheet during a lecture) and reach them with one tap. *Pin as quick tab* is in the page sheet, the All actions sheet and the command palette, and a long press (touch, pen) or right click on the page number in the toolbar pins or unpins the page in view. A slim strip under the toolbar shows one tab per pin (the bookmark title if the page has one, else *p. N*); tap shows the page, *Back* returns to where you were (also after a jump into another notebook), the current page's tab is highlighted, a tab whose page was deleted is drawn dashed and lands on the nearest page. Reorder by dragging (mouse at once; finger or pen after a long press) or with *Move earlier / later*; *Unpin* removes a tab. *Pin a page from another notebook…* adds a page of, say, a formula-sheet notebook to this notebook's strip: tapping it opens that notebook. The strip collapses to one button with the count (remembered per device), is tighter on a phone and becomes a column beside the page on a short landscape view. At most 12 tabs per notebook.
- **Where the pins live.** Not in the notebook: the document format is unchanged. One file per device, `_meta/quicktabs-<device>.json`, merged across devices like the saved collections (the latest change per pin wins, an unpin is a tombstone kept 90 days and then only dropped once no other device's file still holds the pin, unknown fields and entries are kept, a file that cannot be read or is empty is never overwritten and a copy of a damaged one is kept). Pins point to a page id, so they survive moving pages, and follow a renamed notebook. The Library check treats `quicktabs-*.json` like the other `_meta` sidecars. Tests: `tests/quicktabs.test.ts`, `tests/library-check.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/quicktabs-check.ts` (`doc=quicktabs`).

### Review fixes
- Comments: when two devices edit the same comment or reply differently, a merge keeps the losing version beside the winner as a marked "⚠" item and counts it, instead of dropping it silently. The id of that copy is built from a fingerprint of the whole original id, so it fits the id limit and two comments can never get the same one.
- Comments no longer end up in page templates, notebooks made from templates, or practice attempts.
- Merge keeps a copy's comments that lie in the area the page was enlarged by; restoring a page version moves the page's comments with the changed left margin.
- Text typed in a comment editor whose comment or page has vanished is kept as a new comment instead of being lost; the notice says so only once it worked, and if the page is locked or full the text is copied to the clipboard.
- The "Comments in the PDF export" setting is now "Include comments in exports" and also governs the Markdown export (off by default).
- Copy to notebook with link: a copied page no longer keeps the source's older "Copied to/from" chips, and only the chips this feature wrote (its own labels, in either language, linking to a notebook page) are removed; a small link of your own is kept.
- Selection into a note: the embed goes into the note you chose even if the editor switched notes, or was closed, while drawing, and never lands inside the front matter.
- Quick tabs and saved collections: an unpin or deletion no longer comes back after 90 days; each device adopts the winning state when it rewrites its file, and a tombstone is kept while another file still holds the item.
- Quick tabs: renaming a folder of notebooks updates the tabs in one write.
- Annotate from the PDF viewer: PDFs over 200 MB are refused with a notice; the PDF hash and the notebooks' asset ids are cached for the session (only asset values and asset-map keys are kept), so only new or changed notebooks are read; a large library shows progress with a cancel button; the header button is removed when the plugin unloads.
- Mentions: the badge refreshes only when a note that links (or linked) to the notebook changes, including a link in its properties, when the set of notes linking to it changes (also when the link index is ready only after the notebook opened), or a source note is removed or renamed.

## 0.17.0 — 2026-11-04

Write on and around the page more freely, and keep a large library in order: sticky notes you can type or write on, magnetic snap with alignment guides, merge, split and extract notebooks, a minimap for big pages, a hover preview of page links, an optional PDF copy next to each notebook, saved collections in the library, and a task made from a selection. The notebook format moves to version 15 (an older plugin opens a v15 file read-only).

### Sticky notes (#20) and document format 15
- **Sticky notes (#20), document format 15.** *Sticky note* (quick slot, sheet, command) adds a coloured square note to the page. Type on it (*Edit note*: text, one of the eight palette colours, text size) and/or write on it by hand: ink written on a note belongs to it and moves and scales with it. Move, resize, copy and delete it with the usual selection tools; *Collapse note* turns it into a small icon (its ink is hidden with it and follows the icon) and *Open note* brings it back. Every change is one undo step and is refused on a locked page. Notes are drawn in the page view, thumbnails, PNG, PDF and SVG; the Markdown export has their typed text as quotes.
- **Document format v15.** A new element type `sticky` (a collapsed note also lists the ids of the strokes it hides). The v14 to v15 migration only refuses a note in a v14 file; large v14 files still stage page by page. 0.16 and older open a v15 file read-only as a newer format and never overwrite it. See [architecture.md](docs/architecture.md) section 2.

### Snap and alignment guides (#71)
- **Magnetic snap and alignment guides (#71).** While a selection (ink, a picture, a text box, a shape) is moved or scaled with the handles, it is pulled onto the paper grid (grid, dotted, millimetre paper every 5 mm, ruled paper vertically) and onto alignment lines: the edges and centres of the other items of the page (pictures, text boxes, and written words or drawings taken as one item), the page centre and edges, the margins of a PDF page and the margin line of ruled paper. Thin coloured guides (pink for items, teal for the page and margins, a faint blue for the grid) are drawn on the live layer only during the drag and run from the selection to the item it lines up with. A guide beats the grid on the same axis. Rotation is never snapped; neither is a selection that spans several pages. Settings (Writing): *Snap and guides* (Grid and guides by default, Paper grid, Guides, Off) and *Snap strength* (3-20 px, 8 by default). A temporary way out: hold a second finger on the screen while dragging (with the pen, one finger; with a finger, a second one; wide palm contacts do not count), hold Alt, or turn *Snap* off in the selection bar (it stays off for that notebook view). No change to the notebook format; the stroke engine and the input files are untouched. Pure logic in `src/model/snap.ts` (targets, grid, move and scale snapping, guides), glue in `src/view/snap-glue.ts`, the finger/Alt bookkeeping in `src/view/snap-hold.ts`. Tests: `tests/snap.test.ts`, `tests/snap-glue.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/snap-check.ts` (`doc=snap`).

### Merge, split and extract notebooks (#77)
- **Merge notebooks, split a notebook, extract pages into a new notebook (#77).** The library's notebook menu has *Merge another notebook into this one…* (the pages of the notebook you search for are added at the end or after a chosen page, with an option to move that notebook to the library trash afterwards), *Split at a page…* (the pages from a chosen page to the end become a new notebook beside the first) and *Extract pages to a new notebook…* (tick pages, keep them in the original or move them). In an open notebook the notebook map has *Select pages*: tick cards, then *Extract…* or *Split before this page…*. Copies get new page ids; moved pages keep theirs, so stable links follow them (the plugin offers *Review links* when notes link to pages that moved, and counts text boxes inside notebooks that do, which it does not rewrite). Pictures, PDFs, slide links, bookmarks and drawing states travel with the pages; a locked page keeps its lock in the copy.
- **Safe order.** The destination is written and read back first (a new notebook is validated before it exists, an existing closed one is backed up, re-validated and written only if it still holds what was read; an open one is edited through its tab as one undo step); only then do the pages leave the source, and only those still exactly as they were copied. Locked pages leave their notebook only after an explicit tick; a merged notebook goes to the library trash (never deleted) only when every page of it is in the destination and nothing was written to it meanwhile. No format change. Tests: `tests/notebook-transfer.test.ts`, `tests/page-transfer-run.test.ts`, `tests/transfer-logic.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/pagesplit-check.ts` (`doc=xfer`).
- **Fix.** Adding or removing a bookmark no longer drops the notebook's description and generated cover from the metadata it returns (it affected "Move to notebook…" with bookmarked pages into a notebook that had a description).

### Minimap (#173)
- **Minimap (#173).** A small floating overview of the page in view with the rectangle the view shows. Tap or drag on it (or use the arrow keys) to pan; collapse it to a button; drag its handle to any of the four corners (kept per device). It appears by itself on infinite pages and when zoomed in about twice beyond fitting the page width, and not while the whole page is in view. New setting *Pages > Page overview (minimap)*: Automatic / Always / Never. The picture is the page rendered through the library thumbnail renderer, cached, redrawn at most once every 1.2 s after a change and never while the pen writes. No change to the notebook format or to the ink pipeline. Tests: `tests/minimap-geometry.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/minimap-check.ts` (`doc=minimap`).

### Hover preview of page links (O5)
- **Hover preview of notebook page links (O5).** Hovering a link such as `[[Physics.notebook#p3&id=...]]` in a note shows Obsidian's page popover with the page thumbnail, the notebook name and "Page 3 of 12"; clicking the card opens that page. The plugin registers a *Notebook* hover source with the Page preview core plugin and answers its `hover-link` event, so it follows that plugin's on/off switch and its Ctrl/Cmd setting (default: hold Ctrl/Cmd). A link whose page id no longer exists says so instead of showing another page. The thumbnail is drawn once per file version, only when the pen is quiet, and cached; the notebook is only read. No format change. Android has no hover: Obsidian's own long press is left as it is. Tests: `tests/hover-preview.test.ts`.

### Optional PDF copy (#23)
- **Optional PDF copy next to each notebook (#23).** Off by default. *Settings > Obsidian integration > Keep a PDF copy of each notebook* keeps a PDF named like the notebook next to it (or in *PDF copy folder*) for sharing or for tools such as NotebookLM; a notebook can override the switch from its tab's *More options* menu (*Keep a PDF copy*), which also shows *PDF copy updated hh:mm*. The copy is rebuilt after the notebook was saved and left alone for 30 s (desktop), when the view closes and when the app goes to the background; *Update the PDF copy* can be set to *When I close the notebook* only, which is what *Automatic* means on a phone or tablet. It uses the existing PDF export (equation typesetting and hidden ink are left out), builds page by page and yields to the interface between pages (the PDF writer got a page-by-page entry point, byte-identical output), waits while the pen is on the screen, and is skipped when a content fingerprint (SHA-256 of each page, title, headers) did not change; a notebook edited while its copy was being built is not written, the next save builds it again.
- **Data safety.** The plugin only ever replaces a PDF it created as this copy and that is still byte for byte what it wrote (path, size and SHA-256 are recorded in `_meta/pdfcopy-<device>.json`, one file per device, read leniently, unknown fields kept, never overwritten when unreadable, empty or newer). A file at the name that is not ours, or a copy the user edited, is never touched: the copy goes to *<name> (copy 2).pdf* and a notice says so. The bytes are checked to be a complete PDF before writing and read back after. A renamed or moved notebook takes its copy along (only if it is still ours); a deleted or trashed notebook leaves the copy where it is and it is no longer updated. No change to the notebook format.
- Tests: `tests/pdf-copy-logic.test.ts` (settings, paths, which file may be written, record file, store, fingerprint, timers, page-by-page PDF), `tests/pdf-copy-service.test.ts` (the service on an in-memory vault). Harness: `dev/harness/pdfcopy-check.ts` (`doc=pdfcopy`).

### Saved collections (#120)
- **Saved collections (#120).** The library sidebar has a *Collections* section: a collection is a name and rules combined with AND: tags (any / all; a nested tag matches under its parent), a folder with its subfolders, modified or created in the last 7 / 30 days or in a date range, favourite (starred or pinned) or not, and has a PDF or not. The editor counts the matching notebooks as the rules change; opening a collection filters the library grid through the same place filter and search as the other places (archived notebooks stay out), and the rail shows a live count per collection. Edit with the pencil (rail or header), delete with a confirmation (a notebook is never touched). Stored in `_meta/collections-<device>.json`, **one file per device** like the library state and folder metadata (a single shared `collections.json` would be written by two devices at once): merged on read, the latest change wins per collection, a delete is a tombstone; read leniently, unknown fields and items kept, never overwritten when unreadable or empty (a copy is kept). No change to the notebook format. Not a rule yet: notebook type / template (the library index has no such field). Tests: `tests/collections.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/collections-check.ts` (`doc=library`).

### Task from a selection (#57)
- **Task from a selection (#57).** *Make task…* in the selection bar (overflow) and the All actions sheet opens a small dialog: the task's text (typed, no OCR), an optional due date, and where it goes: today's daily note (Daily notes or Periodic Notes options; the note is created only when *Create the daily note if missing* is on, from its template), the Tasks note set in Settings > Integration, or any note you choose. The line `- [ ] text ![[crop|200]] [[Notebook, page N]] 📅 date` is added at the end of the note with `vault.process` (the rest of the note is untouched; a line already there is not written twice). The picture is a crop of the page around the selection (ink, PDF page, pictures, drawing state in view), saved where Obsidian keeps attachments and remembered as written by the plugin, so the shared-files inbox never offers it. The date goes last because the Tasks plugin reads its signifiers from the end of the line; with *Tasks plugin date for due dates* off it is written as a Dataview field `[due:: date]`. No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/selection-task.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/task-check.ts` (`doc=task`).

### Review fixes
- Sticky notes: copy, cut, duplicate, move to page and delete now carry a collapsed note's hidden ink with it (also across pages and to other devices).
- Sticky notes: scribble erase, the delete pen gesture and the area eraser no longer delete a collapsed note's hidden ink; insert space moves it together with its note.
- Merge: a collapsed copy of a note that the other device has open no longer hides that note's strokes; saving no longer writes ink ids of strokes that are gone.
- Snap: dragging a drawing-state part in the states part editor is no longer pulled toward the grid or guides.
- Merge, split and extract no longer rewrite or trash a closed notebook whose latest ink only lives in the instant-save journal; they ask you to open it once first.
- Merging with "move to trash" now gives the merged pages new ids when the source notebook cannot be trashed, so two notebooks never share a page id.
- PDF copies: a record file that cannot be written now stops the copy with one notice instead of writing "(copy N)" files; unloading stops a running build, and a copy changed meanwhile is never replaced.
