- Make task: typed text can no longer open a comment, a math block or a link; the picture is removed when the line cannot be added; an existing identical task is reported as already there; Enter during IME composition no longer submits.
- Collections: an empty or newer-version file no longer gets an "unreadable" copy, and deletions older than 90 days are dropped when the file is rewritten.
- Merge, split and extract started from an open notebook (its map) now also refuse to rewrite or trash a closed notebook whose latest ink only lives in the instant-save journal, like the library's flows.
- The library check never counts the saved collections file or the PDF copy record files as notebook content.
- Pasting or duplicating a collapsed sticky note selects the note only; its hidden ink is no longer drawn live, boxed or recoloured by restyle.
- Moving a collapsed sticky note with the pen "move to page" gesture or the cross-page drag now always takes its hidden ink, whatever "Ink follows objects" is set to.
- Task from a selection keeps typed web addresses as working links while still escaping comment, tag, link and math openers.
- Extract and split with move re-check the source's journal before creating the new notebook; if the move still fails after the copy, the notice says the pages were copied, the copy gets new page ids, and the source is left as it was.
- The PDF copy no longer treats a copy that failed read-back as its own: it stops for that notebook, tells once, and resumes when the copy is turned off and on.

## 0.16.0 — 2026-11-04

Finish a page, work with PDFs, and get material in and out faster: lock a finished page, copy text from a PDF with its outline and links, move a selection to another page, import many files at once, present a notebook full screen with a laser pointer, collect highlights into a note, and pick up files shared from other Android apps. **The document format is now v14** (optional page lock): a 0.15 device opens a v14 notebook read-only (newer version) and never overwrites it. **Not verified on the device**: unit tests and the browser harness only.

### Move a selection to another page (#78)
- **Move or copy a selection to another page (#78).** *Move to page…* and *Copy to page…* in the selection bar (overflow) and the All actions sheet open a picker built from the notebook map's cards (page thumbnails, the source page left out) with a first card *New page after N* and a *Keep exact position* chip. Moving removes the items from the page and adds them to the end of the target page with the same ids, as one undo step (a new page is created in the same step). The position is kept and pulled into a smaller page; with *Keep exact position* the coordinates stay as they are. A toast offers *Go to page*. Slide links and picture records are notebook-wide, so they stay valid; ink written on a graph moves with the graph (and ink bound to a graph that stays behind is refused with a message). Strokes that belong to a part of a drawing with states leave that part (the part is restored by undo), and the toast says so. Copying makes new ids. A selection that spans several pages is refused with a message. No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/selection-move.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/movesel-check.ts` (`doc=movesel`).

### Bulk import (#80)
- **Bulk import (#80).** Drop several PDFs or images on the library (the view, a folder in the tree, a folder card, a breadcrumb or a Board column) or choose *New > Import files…* / a folder's *Import files here…* (a multiple-file picker, which also works on Android) and a dialog lists them: the notebook name of each (editable, never clashing with an existing notebook or with each other), the folder, *One notebook per file* or *All images into one notebook* (pages in natural file-name order, shaped like the pictures), the paper and the cover for all. The import runs one file at a time with progress and *Stop after this file*, hands the UI thread back between steps, leaves out unsupported, empty, damaged, HEIC or oversized files with a reason, and ends with a summary and *Open*. A drop on a Board column also sets that status. Dropped folders are searched a few levels deep. No format change. Tests: `tests/bulk-import.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/bulk-check.ts` (`doc=bulk`).
- **Review fixes.** The size limits follow the kind: a PDF up to 100 MB, an image up to 50 MB (the plugin API's limit), and the list says which limit a file went over. A PDF that cannot be read, or whose notebook could not be made, no longer leaves its stored copy in `_assets`: the copy this run just created is moved to the trash (never one that was already there, nor one another notebook of the run holds). Two files with the same name and size are listed as a double drop only when a fingerprint of their first and last 64 KB also matches. Tests: `tests/review-w16-bulk.test.ts`.
- **Review fixes (2).** A stored copy is not moved to the trash while any notebook of the library names it (its file may still be arriving from sync) or any notebook is still being downloaded, and not after a failed notebook creation when a notebook file is left at the target path. Tests: `tests/review-w16-round2.test.ts`.


### Presentation mode with laser pointer (#87 #12)
- **Presentation mode (#87).** Command *Present the notebook (full screen)*, *Presentation* in the More sheet and a button in the notebook map: the pages one at a time, full screen (Fullscreen API on the view where available, else an overlay over the window), fitted on a black surround. Next / previous by tap zones (left third back), swipe, the arrow keys, Page Up / Down, Space and Enter (presenter remotes), Home / End, *B* or *.* to blank; Esc or a small auto-hiding exit button leaves; a page counter fades by itself. The pen and the mouse are a laser pointer (red dot, trail that fades in under a second; nothing is written; setting *Pen is a laser pointer while presenting*). A drawing with states is stepped through first (*as drawn*, then each state) before the next page (setting *Present drawings with states step by step*). *Write while presenting* hands the screen back to the notebook for normal writing. Nothing is ever written by presenting: the state shown is the overlay's own choice. No change to the notebook format; the pen button, hover and input policy files are untouched. Tests: `tests/present-logic.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/present-check.ts` (`doc=present`).

### Highlights digest (#90)
- **Highlights digest (#90).** Notebook More menu and command *Highlights digest…* collect every highlighter region (strokes of one colour that are close together, per page) in the note *<Notebook> — Highlights* next to the notebook: a picture of each region (ink, PDF page and states as shown), the PDF text under it on PDF pages (pdf.js text intersected with the highlight, whole words), a link back to the page and the colour, grouped by page or by colour (Settings > Reading). Only the section between `<!-- notebook-digest:start -->` and `end` is rewritten (through `vault.process`); text outside is never touched, a note with an unpaired marker is left alone, pictures are reused on re-runs and stored per Obsidian's attachment settings. No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/highlight-digest.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/digest-check.ts` (`doc=digest`).
- **Review fixes.** A digest picture is written again in place only when the old embed, resolved from the digest note, is the file with the digest's own name in the note's attachment folder and no other note links to it; otherwise a new file is made, so another notebook's or note's picture is never overwritten. An identical picture is not written again (no sync churn). Before the section is added to a note of yours with the same name and no digest markers, a dialog asks: add it at the end, or write a new note (*<Notebook> — Highlights 2*). Tests: `tests/review-w16-digest.test.ts`.
- **Review fixes (2).** When the plain picture name is held by a file the digest may not rewrite, the copy an earlier run made (*... 1.png*) is found again and rewritten, so repeated runs no longer leave a trail of copies. The answer *write a new note* is remembered: the next run updates the *<Notebook> — Highlights 2* note that already holds the digest section instead of asking again and making a *3*. Tests: `tests/review-w16-round2.test.ts`.


### Shared files inbox (#177)
- **Shared files (#177).** On Android, sharing a PDF or an image to Obsidian saves it in the vault (the attachments location); the plugin cannot register a share target of its own, so it now watches that folder. New PDFs and images (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP) that appear directly in the folder show as a *Shared files* card on Home (the newest three, *Show all* for the rest) and as a *Shared files* item with a count in the library rail. Tapping a file (or its menu) offers **New notebook from it**, **Add to notebook...**, **Dismiss** and **Move to trash**.
- **New notebook from it.** A PDF goes through the same import as *Import PDF* (pages with the PDF as background); an image becomes a one-page notebook with the image as the page background. **Add to notebook...** (search list of notebooks) appends the PDF's pages as PDF pages, or the image as a new last page or as an image element on the page in view (the last page when the notebook is closed). The edit uses the plugin API's checked path: an open notebook gets one undo step and is saved by its own session, a closed one is parsed, changed, re-validated, backed up, written only if it still holds what was read, and read back.
- **Nothing is deleted without asking.** The file is copied into the notebooks' `_assets` (as every import does). Afterwards a dialog offers to move the original to the trash (*Cancel* keeps it); *Move to trash* on a file that was not used asks first and goes through `fileManager.trashFile`. *Dismiss* only hides a file on this device (a list of at most 300 in local storage; corrupt data reads as empty) and never touches it.
- **Settings (Library).** *Shared files* (on by default) and *Shared files folder* (empty = Obsidian's attachments folder: the vault root or the folder set in *Files and links*; "same folder as the note" has no fixed place, so a folder must be chosen). Files that were already in the folder when it was first watched on this device are not listed. Ignored: subfolders, hidden files, the plugin's folders (`_assets`, `_meta`, `_trash`, `_backup`, templates) and a PDF named after a notebook next to it (an export). No change to the notebook format.
- Tests: `tests/shared-inbox.test.ts` (folder resolution, filtering, dismissals, queue, edits on closed notebooks, settings). Harness: `dev/harness/inbox-check.ts` (`doc=inbox`), screenshots `dev/harness/dist/shots/inbox-*.png`.
- **Review fixes.** Files the plugin wrote itself outside `_assets` (digest pictures, exported PDFs and page pictures) are never offered: the paths are remembered for 30 days on this device (at most 400, written before the file is) and the digest and export picture names are recognised on their own. After a file was used, the dialog offering to trash the original is skipped when a note links to or embeds the file (a notice names the notes), and *Move to trash* on a row names the linking notes and starts on Cancel. New files follow the same first-watch rule as the scan. An image placed on a locked page is refused before it is stored (no unused copy), and when an edit is applied to an open notebook but its save cannot be confirmed, the file counts as used and a notice says saving is pending, so a retry cannot add it twice. Tests: `tests/review-w16-inbox.test.ts`.
- **Review fixes (2).** Only the digest picture name pattern hides a file from the list by name; a file of yours called `page-001.png` is offered like any other (exported page pictures are kept out by the record of what the plugin wrote). Tests: `tests/review-w16-inbox.test.ts`.


### Copy text from the PDF, PDF outline and links (#45 #46)
- **Copy text from a PDF page (#45).** New action *Select text* (contextual bar of PDF pages, the sheet, and the command *Select text in a PDF page*) turns on a mode in which every contact selects text instead of writing, so no ink can be drawn by accident: drag with the pen, a finger or the mouse over the text, or tap a word. The selection is drawn over the page and a bubble beside it offers *Copy*, *Copy as quote* (a Markdown quote with the PDF name and page and a link back to the notebook page) and clear; Ctrl/Cmd+C copies it too. There is no text layer in the DOM: the text of a page is read when needed (cached per page) and the selection is geometry over it (`src/pdf/text-select.ts`); a word broken with a hyphen at the end of a line is joined again. *Done*, Escape or choosing a tool leaves the mode. One page per selection; a PDF without text (a scan) says so. No change to the notebook format.
- **PDF outline in the notebook outline (#46).** The outline (bookmarks) of each PDF of the notebook is read with pdf.js, destinations resolved to pages, and mapped to the notebook pages **by source page**, so a notebook that shows only a range of the PDF, reordered pages or a page twice still gets the right jumps (an entry whose page is not shown goes to the next page that is, unless a later entry starts there). *Settings > Pages > Outline > PDF outline*: mixed into the generated outline under the PDF's row (default), its own section, or not listed. The result is cached per PDF on this device (the asset id is the content hash), an empty outline too, so a PDF is not parsed again on every open; a file that cannot be read is tried again after a minute.
- **PDF links (#46).** A tap on a link of a PDF page (a finger when it does not write, a mouse click with Ctrl/Cmd, any tap while the notebook is read-only or in the Select text mode) goes to the notebook page that shows the PDF page the link points at (named destinations, page references, next/previous/first/last page); a link to a page the notebook does not show says so. A web address (http, https, mail) asks first in a dialog that shows it; other kinds are refused. A pen tap in the normal mode stays ink. *Settings > Reading > Follow links in PDF pages* turns it off.
- Harness `doc=pdftext` (`dev/harness/pdftext-check.ts`, a fake PDF with an outline and links in `pdftext-fake.ts`). Tests: `tests/pdf-text-select.test.ts`, `tests/pdf-outline.test.ts`.
- **Review fix (outline cache).** The per-device outline cache is also bounded by size: 512 KB in all (the least recently stored PDFs are evicted first) and no outline over 128 KB is stored; one that big is kept in memory for the session so it is read once. Tests: `tests/review-w16-outline-cache.test.ts`.


### Lock a finished page, document format 14 (#124)
- **Lock a finished page (#124).** *Lock page* / *Unlock page* in the page sheet, the multi-selection menu of the pages panel, the notebook map (a lock button on each card) and the command *Lock or unlock the page in view*. A locked page refuses every edit: pen, highlighter, shapes, text, eraser, insert space, moving or restyling a selection, paste, elements, states, paper, size, margins, background, a version restore over it and deletion. The refusal is one guard where commands run (the history), so nothing slips through; the gestures that would write are stopped before they start. A notice says *Page locked · Unlock*. The lock shows beside the page number, as a badge over the page (a tap unlocks), on the thumbnail in the pages panel and on the notebook map. Locking and unlocking are one undo step each. Reading, PDF and slide navigation, export, copy-out, search, tags, bookmarks, moving pages and adding pages still work; the lasso still selects, to copy out. Find and replace leaves text on locked pages as it is and says so. Moving pages to another notebook and the page trash refuse a locked page (unlock it first).
- **Document format v14.** The optional page field `locked: true` (never written as `false`). The v13 to v14 migration only drops a stray `locked` key; large v13 files still stage page by page. 0.15 and older open a v14 file read-only as a newer format and never overwrite it. A page locked in either side of a conflicted copy is locked in the merge, and the merge still brings the copy's ink to a locked page. A duplicate, a notebook made from a notebook or template, and a restore under an id clash start unlocked. See [architecture.md](docs/architecture.md) section 2.
- **Lock fixes after review.** Undoing a restore that brought back a locked page works again. A picture replaced by the storage manager (duplicate merge, smaller copy) is maintenance and goes through on a locked page; the library check still sees the pictures of a merged conflicted copy that was undone. The public API has a new error code `page-locked` (checked before an image file is written; closed notebooks too). Moving pages out, the storage repoint and the board shot no longer report success when the notebook refused the change. *Replace all* says "All matches are on locked pages" when nothing could be replaced. Notebook-wide batches (for example *Update PDF*) are refused as a whole when any affected page is locked: unlock it first. The guard is cheaper with many locked pages.

## 0.15.0 — 2026-11-04

Notebooks start from templates and stay organised: notebook templates, an automatic page header or footer, pen sets per subject, a daily "Today's page", Board and Calendar views of the library, a storage manager, and drawings with states for physics-style figures. **The document format is now v13** (optional drawing states): a 0.14 device opens a v13 notebook read-only (newer version) and never overwrites it. The state shown is your device's own choice; only *Set as default state* saves it in the notebook. **Not verified on the device**: unit tests and the browser harness only.

### Notebook templates (#19)
- **Notebook templates (#19).** *Save as notebook template…* (library card menu, notebook More sheet and command) stores a copy of a notebook (new document, page, stroke and element ids; the assets and slide links its pages use; its cover) in the notebook templates folder (*Settings > Library > Advanced*, default `<templates folder>/notebooks`, i.e. `_templates/notebooks`). Templates are not listed in the library, its search or statistics, nor offered as page templates. The creation dialog has *From a notebook template…*: cards with the cover art, a choice *Pages only* (paper, size and background, no ink) or *With content*, and `{{date}}` (also `{{time}}`, `{{year}}`, `{{month}}`, `{{day}}`) filled in title, description and text boxes. *Settings > Library > Advanced > Notebook templates* renames and deletes templates (deleting asks first and moves the file to Obsidian’s trash). No format change. Tests: `tests/notebook-template.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/templates-check.ts` (`doc=templates`). Custom folder outside the notebooks folder: hidden from the library, but other notebook lists (quick switcher, timetable) may still show it.
- **Review fixes.** Deleting a notebook template now moves it to the library Trash (restorable), like a notebook, instead of Obsidian's `trashFile`.


### Automatic page header (#75)
- **Automatic page header or footer (#75).** Settings → Pages → *Page header*: off / header / footer, with the page number (with or without the total, "3 / 12"), the notebook's creation date and the notebook title, placed left, centre or right, and an option to include it in PNG, PDF, SVG and printed pages. It is drawn on the paper (new `src/ink/page-header.ts`, painted once per visible page in the static layer through a small `Renderer.setPageDecor` hook), never stored on the page and never part of the ink. It keeps clear of the lecture header and of anything else in its strip (it moves to the other edge or is left out) and of PDF or picture pages. No change to the notebook format.

### Pen sets per subject (#134)
- **Pen sets per subject (#134).** Named lists of pens (tool, colour, width; highlighters included) saved in the plugin settings, with three starter sets (Maths, Languages, Science) you can edit, rename or delete. Assign a set to a folder (its notebooks and subfolders) or to one notebook: opening a notebook puts its set on the favourite pens strip (the notebook's own set, then the nearest folder's, then your favourite pens); your favourite pens are never overwritten by a set.
- A *Pen set* chip at the top of the pen and highlighter options opens the switcher: pick a set for this session, *Save current pens as a set...*, use the set for this notebook or folder, *Manage pen sets...* (rename, replace its pens with the current ones, delete with confirmation, folders, restore starter sets). Also in Settings → Writing → Pen sets. While a set is on the strip, saving a slot saves it into that set.
- Assignments live in the plugin settings (`penSetFolders`, `penSetNotebooks`, at most 200 entries each) and are read leniently; an assignment to a deleted set is skipped. No change to the notebook format; no change to the stroke engine or the input files.

### Today's page (O12)
- **Today's page (roadmap O12).** New command *Today's page*, a *Today's page* quick action on Home. It opens the daily notebook (*Settings > Obsidian integration > Daily notebook*, default `<notebooks folder>/Daily.notebook`; created the first time, after a confirmation, with today's page as its first page) at the page whose header is today's date, and adds that page (same size and paper as the last one, one undo step) when there is none. The page is found by its dated header, so running it twice never adds a second page. Read-only or still-loading notebooks are just opened.
- **Link from the daily note (off by default).** With *Link from the daily note* on, the page is linked at the end of today's daily note, once: the folder and date format come from Periodic Notes (when its daily notes are on) or from the Daily notes core plugin; with neither, a notice says so. The note is changed only with `vault.process` (atomic, nothing else in it is touched), and a missing note is created only with *Create the daily note if missing*. English and Italian strings. No change to the notebook format.
- **Review fixes.** The core Daily notes plugin is now read as `getPluginById("daily-notes")` (`{ enabled, instance }`), so it is no longer always "unavailable". A daily note created by *Create the daily note if missing* starts from the Daily notes template (`{{date}}`, `{{time}}`, `{{title}}` filled in; no note is made if the configured template cannot be read) and waits a moment for a synced copy before making a new one.
- **Review fixes (2).** The Daily notes template is found like the core plugin does (as a link path, else by path with `.md`), and an empty or not-yet-downloaded template is unreadable: a notice, no note.

### Board and calendar views (#161 #162)
- **Board view (#161).** *Views* menu in the library header (Cards, Board, Calendar). The Board shows one column per status for the notebooks of the place you are in (the whole library, a subject, a collection): *To do*, *In progress*, *Done*, *To print* by default. Move a notebook by dragging its card onto a column (mouse and pen at once; touch with a long press that lifts the card; dragging near the edge scrolls the board), or with **⋯ › Move to…** on the card (also in the notebook's context menu): the way that needs no dragging. On a phone one column is shown at a time under a strip of column tabs, and a card can be dropped on a tab. A card dropped on a folder of the sidebar still moves the notebook there. A notebook with no status, or whose column was removed, is shown in the first column.
- **Statuses are synced data, not notebook data.** Stored in `_meta/board-<device>.json`, one file per device like the library state and the folder pins: a device only writes its own file, reading merges every device's file, the latest change per notebook wins (a tie goes to the larger device id, a change always takes a time later than the one it read). Keyed by the notebook's document id (path while the id is not known yet). Read leniently: unknown items and unknown fields (of the file and of each item) are kept and written back; an unreadable, empty or newer-version file is never overwritten (a copy is kept and the move is refused with a notice). **No change to the notebook format** (still v12).
- **Board columns (setting).** *Settings › Library › Board columns* (and the pencil on the Board) opens a dialog to rename, recolour, reorder, add (up to 8) and remove columns, saved at once. Default names follow the UI language until renamed. Removing a column deletes nothing: its notebooks show in the first column and come back if a column with the same id is added back (*Reset to defaults*).
- **Calendar view (#162).** A month grid (tablet, desktop) or a week strip with an agenda (phone): each day is marked with dots in the colours of the notebooks written that day and a stronger tint for busier days; tap a day for its notebooks and pages; tap a notebook, or a *Page N* chip, to open it at that page. Built from the library index (every notebook's created / last-changed time, so edits made on another device show as *Changed*) plus this device's writing activity log (pages written per day, last 90 days) without opening any notebook; only when you tap a page this device has no record of, that one notebook is read to find the page. The document keeps no per-page times, so page detail exists for the days this device wrote.
- Tests: `tests/board-state.test.ts` (file round-trip, lenient reading, merge, changes, store, unreadable files), `tests/board-logic.test.ts`, `tests/calendar-logic.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/views-check.ts` (Board, moves with menu / mouse / pen / touch, another device's file, columns editor, Calendar), screenshots `dev/harness/dist/shots/design/*-views-*.png`.
- **Review fixes.** `board-<device>.json` and its unreadable copies are no longer counted as files that could hold an incomplete reference (library check). This device's board file drops cleared statuses nobody contradicts whenever it is rewritten, and once per library session (when every notebook has been read) the statuses of notebooks that are gone and were set over 30 days ago.
- **Review fixes (2).** The 30 days before a status of a missing notebook is pruned are counted from when the notebook was first seen missing (`missingSince` on the item, cleared when it is back), not from when the status was set; the statuses of notebooks in the library trash are never pruned; a clear is kept while another device's file cannot be read; the library is complete only when its files and entries are the same set.

### Storage manager (#128)
- **Storage manager (#128).** New dialog *Storage* on the modal shell, from the library sidebar (*Storage*), the command *Storage: size of notebooks…* and *Settings → Library → Storage manager*. It shows the total and a bar by category (notebooks, pictures and PDFs, backups and history, trash, journal, other), then each notebook with its size (file + the pictures only it uses + its equal share of shared pictures + its backups and version history + its journal), sortable by size, name, files or backups; the rows add up to the total.
- **Unused files** are exactly the library check's verdict (`runLibraryCheck`): nothing named by any notebook of the vault (even one that no longer parses, or sits in another folder), backup, version history, deleted page or deleted notebook in the plugin trash, template, stamp, `_meta` file, open notebook (document and undo history) or the instant-save journal is ever listed, and nothing is offered while a file that could name an asset is not downloaded or unreadable, or until this device has seen the file unused for a day. Moving them uses the check's recoverable folder `_trash/assets/` after a confirmation that lists each file.
- **Duplicate pictures.** Images are hashed only when another image has the same size. A picture stored twice under another extension (same id) loses the extra file (every notebook finds the other by id: `resolveAssetFile` now also tries the other image extensions); a copy under another id has its notebooks pointed at the one named by its own content, in one undo step for an open notebook (`src/model/asset-ops.ts`: `planAssetRepoint` composes `AddAsset`, `SetPageBackground`, `ReplaceElement`, `SetMeta` and the new `RemoveAsset` into one `Batch`).
- **Heavy pictures.** Pictures a readable notebook uses, over 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 MB (a chip, remembered on the device). *Downscale* writes a smaller copy (at most 2048 px on the long side; PNG with transparency stays PNG, anything else becomes JPEG; kept only if at least 10% smaller; GIF is left alone) and points the notebooks at it. The original is kept in `_assets/` (undo, backups and other notebooks may still name it) and moves to `_trash/assets/` from *Unused files* once nothing names it.
- **Old backups and copies.** `.bak` backups and version-history snapshots older than 90 days (never the newest backup of a notebook, never the newest snapshots, never those of a notebook that is gone; copies made to keep something unreadable or unsaved are listed but not ticked) and conflicted copies identical to their notebook go to the trash after a confirmation (`fileManager.trashFile` for backups, the library trash for copies). Copies that differ are only listed, with *Open*.
- Notebooks are only edited when it is safe: an open one through its view (one undo step, saved by its own session), one that is open but busy is skipped, a closed one after a backup, from text that passed the save check, only if the file still holds what was read, and read back. A notebook that does not parse is never written.
- The scan is asynchronous, chunked and cancellable (Stop, or closing the dialog); unchanged notebooks come from the session cache.
- No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/storage-manager.test.ts`, `tests/storage-scan.test.ts` (unused detection), `tests/storage-backups.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/storage-check.ts` (`doc=storage`; screenshots in `dev/harness/dist/shots/design/storage-*`).
- **Review fixes.** Merging the same picture stored under two extensions now leaves the extra file in place while any notebook record names it (library covers and older devices read the path). A closed notebook with edits waiting in the instant-save journal is never rewritten. The newest ordinary backup stays even when a newer recovery copy exists; backups of notebooks that cannot be read now are not offered; a backup whose own document id is another notebook's (a new notebook reusing a deleted one's name) is not offered. The confirmation says Obsidian's *Deleted files* setting may delete backups for good. An open notebook whose save is still pending is reported as changed, not failed.
- **Review fixes (2).** Merging a picture stored twice is not offered while the library check is incomplete, and the library trash and template notebooks are read for records naming the file before it is moved aside. The newest ordinary backup spared is chosen among the backups the notebook owns, not another notebook's.

### Drawings with states, document format 13 (#241)
- **Drawings with states (#241).** Draw a figure once, mark the parts that change and give each state its own arrangement. Open *Drawing states* (All actions, page sheet, palette) for the strip under the page, select ink with the lasso and tap *Make part* in it (a block, a force arrow, a spring; also in All actions): *As drawn*, the states you add, previous / next, play. In a state, tap a part and move, turn or scale it with the ordinary selection handles; the editor row also hides the part or colours it in that state and puts it back where it was drawn. A state never rewrites the strokes: it is applied when the page is drawn, exported (PNG / PDF / SVG use the state in view) or shown as a thumbnail. Every edit is one undo step. Which state is shown is your view's own choice: switching and playing never write the notebook (so two devices that only look make no conflicted copies), and a read-only notebook can switch and play too; *Set as default state* (options menu of the strip) saves the one the notebook opens with, as one undo step. Erasing part of a part's stroke (segment or area eraser) keeps the pieces in the part; changing the page margins moves the parts' turning centres with the ink; the part editor's selection has no colour or width rows (its handles are the pose); Markdown exports and live excerpts use the state in view like the other exports. The ink tools skip a part a state has moved (switch to *As drawn* to edit the drawing). Elements (text, images, equations, graphs) are not parts: only ink is.
- **Document format v13.** New optional page field `states` (parts of stroke ids with a pivot, named states with per-part poses, the state shown), written on one line of the page, pruned on write, read leniently (invalid entries are dropped and counted, never the ink), with the v12 to v13 migration (a stray `states` key in a v12 file is dropped; large v12 files still load in stages). Page duplicate, move to another notebook, page trash and restore, page version restore, conflicted-copy merge and repair all carry the states. A 0.14 device opens a v13 notebook read-only (newer version) and never overwrites it. Details in `docs/architecture.md`.
- Not verified on the device: unit tests and the browser harness only.

## 0.14.0 — 2026-11-04

The notebook adapts to the device and to the lecture: the layout follows phone, tablet and desktop in either orientation, the toolbar adapts to the page and to what you use, lecture mode starts from the timetable, and notes remember the slide they were written beside. **The document format is now v12** (optional slide links): a 0.13 device opens a v12 notebook read-only (newer version) and never overwrites it; a 0.13 device that saves the timetable drops the lecturer field. **Not verified on the device**: unit tests and the browser harness only.

### Layout for phone, tablet and desktop (#430)
- **Layout by orientation and size (#430).** New pure classifier `src/ui/viewport-class.ts`: a view is phone / tablet / desktop, portrait / landscape, and "short" (landscape with little height), from its width, height and aspect, with hysteresis so rotation, a dragged split or a keyboard opening does not flicker. The notebook view and Home / the library put `nb-vp-phone|tablet|desktop`, `nb-vp-portrait|landscape`, `nb-vp-short` and `data-nb-viewport` on their root; other surfaces (toolbar, context bar) can style from them.
- Notebook view in short landscape: toolbar and context bar lose their vertical padding; the PDF side-by-side split no longer flips back and forth near its threshold and has a wider divider when held sideways. Home and the library: header and padding shrink in short landscape, the folder grid uses three columns, a tablet held sideways keeps four quick actions. Dialogs use the whole height of a short landscape window.
- No change to the notebook format.

### Adaptive toolbar (#392 #393 #402)
- **Toolbar by page type (#392).** The kind of the page in view (slide, PDF, image, ruled, grid, plain) is read from its background, paper and size. A slide puts the snapping highlighter, PDF search and margins first and the PDF bar says *Slide*; ruled paper marks the pen and text tools, grid paper the shape tool and puts the ruler first among the quick actions, slides and PDF pages mark the highlighter. Tools never move: a small dot marks the ones a page invites. New action *Snap highlighter to text lines* (PDF bar, All actions, command) beside the existing highlighter option.
- **Toolbar that learns per notebook (#393).** Quick slots are ranked by what is used in the notebook in view (counts per notebook, in the device local storage, bounded to 60 notebooks and 60 actions each; a notebook uses its own counts after 8 uses, the device counts before). Press and hold a quick slot, or a row of *All actions*, to pin it (up to 3): pinned slots always come first, in the order pinned. *Settings > Appearance > Reset the adaptive toolbar* forgets the counts and the pins.
- **Selection bar by content type (#402).** Ink, picture, text and mixed selections each put their own actions first (stamp, select similar and link for ink; white to transparent, layer order and transform for pictures; link for text; align and layer order for mixed) and the bar says which it is. An empty colour strip (picture or object selection) is no longer shown.
- Three settings, one per family (*Toolbar follows the page*, *Toolbar learns from each notebook*, *Selection bar follows what is selected*), on by default; with one off that part of the toolbar is exactly the designed order. No change to the notebook format. Tests: `tests/adapt-toolbar.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/toolbar-check.ts` (doc=toolbar, doc=toolbar-pdf).
- Back stays a quick slot while there is somewhere to go back to (with room for three slots), even when a page-type boost or learned use would rank other actions above it.

### Lecture mode (#401 #438)
- **Automatic lecture mode from the timetable (#401, #115, #214).** New setting *Study → Class timetable → Lecture mode* (*Off* / *Ask me* (default) / *Automatic*). While a class of the timetable is in progress (with the existing *Start early* lead) and the notebook open in a view belongs to it (the notebook or folder it links to, or, for an unlinked class, the notebook named like the course), the view offers lecture mode with a small banner (*Start* / *Not now*) or switches to it by itself. Lecture mode puts the contextual lecture bar (board shot, markers, new lecture, end-of-lecture panel) on and shows a quiet *Course · until HH:MM* chip with an *End* button. It ends with the class and says so inside the app; nothing is sent outside it. It never starts, asks or ends while the pen is on the screen (it waits for the pause). *Not now* and *End* are remembered for that class and day. A *Lecture mode* command (also in the All actions sheet) switches it on and off by hand, with or without a class. Opening the notebook from Home's Continue card or the timetable offer goes through the same rule. The contextual lecture bar now follows lecture mode instead of "a class is on anywhere".
- **Lecture page prefilled from context (#438, #210).** A lecture page made by lecture mode, by *Open the current class* or by *New lecture* (when the course and date are the class in progress) now carries, under the header, a second text box with the time slot, room and lecturer (`09:00–11:00 · B2 · Prof. Rossi`); when a PDF is open beside the notes (or the page is a PDF page) it also says `Slides: [[deck.pdf#page=12]]` (a link for a PDF of the vault). The *New lecture* dialog shows what will be added. New optional *Lecturer* field in the timetable editor and `lecturer` in `_timetable.json` (older files read as before). Ordinary text boxes: no change to the notebook format (still v11).
- **The lecture page is added by you, never by the clock.** Lecture mode itself still starts by itself in *Automatic*, but the lecture page is only added by a user action: *Start*, the *Lecture mode* command, or, in *Automatic*, the first stroke you write during the lecture (it is added at the end of the notebook without moving the page you are writing on, as its own undo step). Opening the notebook, the 30-second check and coming back to the app change nothing, so a device that wakes up with an older copy of the notebook is never marked as having local changes before sync has delivered the newer one. The setting's description says so. A short notice "Lecture page added" with a *Go* button tells you when the page was added behind your first stroke. In a read-only notebook (the user's read-only mode, sync safety's *Open read-only*, a newer format) *Start* and the command add no page and say so; nothing is written.
- *Not now*, *End* and "the page was added" are remembered for the day in the device's local storage (a bounded record per notebook, only today's classes): reopening a notebook no longer asks again about a class you turned down, and no longer adds a lecture page you deleted or whose header you changed.
- **Timetable file: unknown fields are kept.** `_timetable.json` keeps fields this version does not know, at the top level and in each class, and writes them back unchanged (a known field always wins). Caveat for devices on different versions: 0.13 does not know `lecturer` and drops it when it saves the timetable, so saving the timetable on a 0.13 device removes the lecturers set on a 0.14 device; update every device before editing the timetable.
- Tests: `tests/lecture-mode.test.ts`, `tests/lecture-memory.test.ts`, `tests/timetable.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/lecture-check.ts` (`doc=lecture`; screenshots in `dist/shots/design/lecture-*`).

### Smart add (#410)
- **Smart "+" (#410).** The "+" (add page) button now shows a small strip with the two or three most likely new pages as one-tap chips: the same paper as this page, an exercise page, a notes page for the slide in view when a PDF is beside the notes, the paper your last added pages used (e.g. Cornell) or a blank page, plus *More...* which opens the page sheet with every way to add a page. Nothing is added until a chip is tapped; the strip goes away when the pen goes down, the page changes, after ten seconds or on Escape. The ranking is a deterministic pure module (`src/model/smart-add.ts`) fed by the page in view, the context and what you added before in that notebook (kept on this device, not in the notebook file: one bounded record for all notebooks, 60 notebooks with 12 additions each, replacing the per-notebook storage keys of earlier 0.14 builds, which are read once and removed). No format change.
- The Obsidian command *Add page* (and any hotkey bound to it) still adds a page right away, with the same paper as before 0.14; the strip has its own command, *Add page: suggestions*. Only the on-screen "+" opens the strip.

### Dynamic sections (#415)
- **Dynamic sections (roadmap 415).** *Now* (the class in progress, notebooks open in a tab, notebooks edited on this device in the last four hours), *To complete* (open gap / question / homework markers, skipped pages, PDF slides without ink, practice copies not yet written) and *Changed on another device* (a notebook file newer than what this device has last seen and not written here). They are panels at the top of the library dashboard (five rows each, *Show all*), places with the usual grid and list, sidebar items with counts and shortcut chips under Home's Continue row; each hides when empty and each row opens its notebook. *Mark as seen* clears the Changed section; opening a notebook marks it seen. Rules are pure and tested (`src/library/dynamic-sections.ts`).
- The per-device "seen" record is kept in the device's local storage (never synced), so there is no change to the notebook format or to any synced file. The library index gets an optional `emptyPages` field (pages to fill); the index version is now 6, so an older index is rebuilt once and every notebook gets it.

### Notebook map (#408)
- **Notebook map (roadmap #408).** A button in the page panel's header (grid icon) opens a dialog with a grid of page thumbnails for the whole notebook, each with badges for the states the page is in: open **question**, **gap** to complete, **homework** (with the earliest due date), **key point**, **unfinished practice** and **nearly empty** page. Filter chips with a count per state narrow the grid (tap the active chip to clear it); tapping a card goes to the page; the page in view is outlined and the first page of each lecture (#223) has an accent edge. Resolved items do not count.
- Pure aggregator `src/model/notebook-map.ts` (no 'obsidian'), cached per page revision (`NotebookMapCache`: a page whose markers, text, tags or stroke count did not change is not recomputed; moving a page only refreshes its index). Thumbnails come from the page panel's cache and are drawn lazily, at the first pause in writing. Unfinished practice is judged from the page (a practice copy with nothing written yet) unless the caller passes the ids of finished attempts. Nothing is stored: **no change to the notebook format** (still v11).
- Tests: `tests/notebook-map.test.ts`. Harness: `dev/harness/nbmap-check.ts`, scenarios `nbmap-*`.

### Slide-linked ink, document format 12 (#226 #417)
- **Notes remember their slide (#226), format v12.** Ink written while a PDF page is open side by side remembers that slide: a small badge next to the ink shows the slide again when tapped (it opens the PDF in the pane if needed); *Show slide* prefers the slide of the selected ink. The link is part of the stroke's undo step. Settings: *PDF & reading → Link notes to slides*, *Slide badges*.
- **Slide strip (#417).** For each slide: its page, the notes pages linked to it, the exercise page after it, the notes written beside it or in its margin, and its questions; each chip goes there. From the side pane's header, the actions sheet and the palette (*Open the slide strip*).
- **Format v12**: an optional document table `slides` and an optional stroke field `slide`; the v11 → v12 migration only drops such keys found in a v11 file. 0.13 opens a v12 notebook read-only (newer version) and never overwrites it. See `docs/architecture.md` (*Notes ↔ slide link*).
- **Slide links: review fixes.** Repairing a damaged v12 file keeps the slide table and the links (only a dangling link is dropped); a deleted page restored from the trash gets its slide badges back (same undo step); an over-long PDF path or source can no longer make a notebook unsavable (`linkStrokes` omits the path or does not link, `checkContent` validates the table). Moving pages to another notebook and page templates leave the ink unlinked, as documented.
- Tests: `tests/model-v12.test.ts`, `tests/slide-strip.test.ts`, `tests/repair.test.ts`. Not verified on hardware.

## 0.13.0 — 2026-10-31

The design system reaches every remaining surface, the Home is restructured, and two roadmap features land: the generated outline (#407) and just-in-time tips (#425). The art engine gets a new family (veins), OKLCH colour, glow, keyword motifs, growth, a persistent cache and off-main-thread rendering. **Not verified on the device**: unit tests and the browser harness only; manual steps 500–679 in `docs/testing.md`. No change to the notebook format (still v11).

### Polish and review fixes
- **Art off the main thread, in the app.** Home, the library, the creation dialog, the dialog headers and the Zettelkasten pages now ask the art engine for pictures through `applyArt` (`src/ui/art/art-ref.ts`): a cached picture is set at once; otherwise the card is drawn without art and the picture arrives from the Worker + OffscreenCanvas path (IndexedDB cache first, main-thread fallback), fading in over 0.28 s (not with reduced motion), keyed by seed and size so a re-render never flickers, and never after the element is detached. Opening Home prepares the art of the first twelve folders in the background. The 0.12.3 guards stay: a failed picture leaves the element without art, three failures switch the art off.
- **Veins is selectable.** The creation dialog's style picker, the folder art button and folder metadata offer *Veins* (*Venature*); a biology, anatomy or nature name starts a new folder or notebook on veins until you pick a style yourself. A notebook stores veins as the `branches` style with a `veins~` seed prefix (`src/model/cover-family.ts`), so the file format is unchanged and 0.12.x devices open the notebook and draw branches. A folder stores `art: "veins"` in the sidecar; a 0.12.x device does not know that value and keeps the folder item aside unread (the folder shows its default look there until it is updated).
- **Toolbar at 740 px landscape.** A normal-size toolbar that would keep only two quick slots at 44 px uses the 40 px targets of the compact preset when that gives three, so Back is a quick slot again; wider views and tablets in portrait keep 44 px. The harness checks three quick slots (new `phoneLandscape` 740 x 360 device).
- Art cache: the IndexedDB cache is kept per vault, and its 160-picture cap is applied when the store opens and on the first write of a session (before: every twentieth write, which a short session never reached). There is no settings action that resets it yet.
- Fixes from the independent review: Home's Quick page card and the ribbon/command used together no longer make "Quick notes 2" (one shared creation); the writing-activity save waits for a pause in writing; *Open in new tab* on a recent page reveals the notebook if it is already open instead of opening a second editable view.
- Known: a 0.12.x device rewriting its own folder sidecar drops folder pins (and pins do not follow a rename); cosmetic, pins are per device file.
- Tests: `tests/cover-family.test.ts`, `tests/polish-art-persist.test.ts`, `tests/polish-review-fixes.test.ts`, additions in `tests/ds-notebook.test.ts` and `tests/create-dialog.test.ts`. Harness: the worker path must really run where OffscreenCanvas exists (art gallery and Home).

### Home and sidebar
- **Home, revised structure (same look).** A greeting that follows the clock ("Buonasera, Alex · 3 pagine scritte oggi"; new setting *Appearance → Your name*); a *Continue* row with a hero card showing a large page thumbnail of the lecture in progress (the timetable's class, its linked notebook or course folder; a button to start a notebook when the course has none) or, without a class, the notebook you left off in, and a *Quick page* card that opens a new blank page in Quick notes; low quick actions (icon and title, the description is the tooltip); a three-column folder grid (two on a tablet in portrait, one on a phone) ending in the dashed *New folder* card; *Recent notes* grouped by notebook with a page thumbnail on every row, a ⋯ menu (open page, open in a new tab, show in the library, pin, favourite) and a subject filter; *Statistics* rebuilt as a pages-per-day chart (7 / 14 days), days in a row, estimated writing time, PDFs annotated (a hint that starts the import when there is none) and the most active subject with a bar split by subject colour. Widgets that are zero are hidden or become a hint.
- **Sidebar.** A *Pinned* section above *Vault* (notebooks and folders; pin from their ⋯ menus, from Recent notes and from a folder's menu; every item opens on tap); folders show their page count next to the notebook count.
- **Writing activity** (pages and minutes written per day, per device, in the device-local storage next to the recent pages log; no change to the notebook format); folder metadata gains a `pinned` field (same sidecar file, merged per field like the colour).
- Tests: `tests/home-greeting.test.ts`, `tests/home-stats.test.ts`, `tests/home-pins.test.ts` (greeting, activity log, pages per day, streak, subject split, writing time, grouping, pages per folder, the Continue choice, pinned folders, Quick page logic). Harness: `dev/harness/home2-check.ts`; Home scenarios for the lecture, a class without a notebook and no class; full-page shots in `dist/shots/design/home-*-full.png`.

### Generated outline (#407)
- **Generated outline (roadmap #407, combines #15, #102 and #46).** The page panel's outline mode now merges four sources into one nested list ordered by page and position: **bookmarks** (title, colour, category, as before), **PDF slide changes** (each PDF file, and each slide where the PDF page under the notebook page changes), **large handwriting** as headings, and **markers** (questions, homework and the importance markers; gaps to complete are not listed). Tap a row to jump to its page; bookmarks keep their "…" menu; the category filter still filters bookmarks.
- **Headings from handwriting size, no OCR and no AI** (`src/ink/headings.ts`, on the lines of `segmentText`, #255): a line is a heading when its letters are at least 1.7 times the page's usual letter height (weighted median of its lines; the notebook's usual height on a page with fewer than three lines), it has two or more strokes, it starts a block (nothing written just above it) and starts near the left of the text. Letters 2.4 times the usual or more make a top-level heading, the others a second level. The outline shows a picture of the ink (nothing is read, so there is no text), cached per heading.
- **Pure builder** `src/model/outline-builder.ts` (no 'obsidian'): the text layout of a page is cached by the identity of its strokes, so an unchanged page costs a pointer comparison per stroke. Nesting follows a level stack (PDF and bookmark, slide, heading, marker). Nothing is stored: **no change to the notebook format** (still v11).
- Settings → *Pages* → *Outline* (a group, open when a source is off): a switch for each source (bookmarks, slide changes, large handwriting, markers). Italian translation included.
- The outline rows are restyled with the design tokens (icon squares, nesting guides, 44 px rows, "you are here" on one row) under `.nb-ui`; rows wrap to two lines in the narrow panel.
- Tests: `tests/outline-builder.test.ts`; harness scenarios `outline-{light,dark}-{tablet,phone}` (`dev/harness/outline-check.ts`).

### Zettelkasten in the design system
- **The Zettelkasten surfaces on the design system (DESIGN.md §3, §4, §5).** The Inbox is a page of its own: header with the count, a faint generated art band, the *Start processing* pill and refresh; filter chips with counts (sources) plus a *Show processed* toggle; one panel per notebook in its subject colour with item rows (icon by kind, first line, page and PDF page, *Changed* / state pills) that expand in place into the ink on its paper, the text and the actions (icons, one accent action). Phone layout under 600 px. While the first scan runs the list shows placeholder rows; an empty inbox teaches what arrives here (quick notes, PDF highlights and comments) with the generated illustration; a cleared one says *All caught up* and offers *Show processed* / *Show all sources* / look again.
- The card dialog (*Make card*) is a design-system dialog: icon, title and one line, the ink, labelled fields, the card type as three tiles (permanent, literature, fleeting, each with its colour and a hint), the tags previewed as they will be saved, `Cancel` / `Create card`. The card-list picker shows the id, the title and where the card is (orphans in warning colour); the slip-box confirmation is the same dialog style. Enter still creates, Escape still cancels.
- Behaviour is unchanged: same service calls, same keys. New strings in English and Italian. Harness: `dev/harness/zettel-check.ts` (`doc=zettel`) checks the layout, the targets, every control and the empty states, with screenshots in `dev/harness/dist/shots/design/zettel-*.png`.

### Notebook toolbar, bars, sheets and radial menu
- **The notebook's editing chrome on the design system** (DESIGN.md): the toolbar's tool and history groups are quiet capsules, the pen indicator, page indicator and save dot are legible at a glance, the options popovers, the *All actions* and page sheets and the contextual bar are floating glass panels (radius, hairline, elevation, backdrop blur), sheet rows have an icon square (accent when on, danger tint for destructive ones) and wrap long labels, the radial dial is the navy disc with a hairline ring and an accent halo in both themes, notebook tabs are pills, the heavy-page and practice banners are glass cards with an icon square (decoration, never a control), the status bar item and the page-operations menu follow the tokens. Light and dark, and *Follow Obsidian theme*. Behaviour is unchanged: every control works as before.
- **Touch targets**: the toolbar buttons are 44 px at the normal size and 40 px at the compact size (were 40 and 36); on a view too narrow for eight 44 px buttons (a 360 px phone) the normal size uses 40 px so the row of tools stays on one line (`toolbarScaleFor`); on touch the sheet's close button, tabs, banner buttons and chips are 44 px. Motion, blur and glows are off under reduced motion.
- No change to the notebook format, the canvas, the input or the stroke pipeline.

### Dialogs on one modal shell
- Every dialog, prompt and floating card now follows DESIGN.md on one shared shell (`src/ui/components/modal-shell.ts`, pure parts in `modal-shell-model.ts`): a header with an icon square, a title, an optional line under it, a close button and a faint generated art band (decoration only), a scrolling body and a footer with a secondary and a primary button, all under `.nb-ui` with `--nb-*` tokens, light and dark, and "Follow Obsidian theme". Sheets full height on phones, 44 px targets.
- Restyled, behaviour unchanged: resize / style / open-notebook / new-notebook pickers, page arrange (move, duplicate, paper, size), export (PDF, PNG, Markdown), go to page and the page finder (prompts), paste, PDF page range, practice, attempts, lecture pack, exercise paper, pen test, shared clips, find and replace, the colour picker, the equation editor, the stamp palette and the destructive-action confirmations.
- New strings: `ui.modal.cancel`, `goto.title`, `quicknav.title`, `modal.open.title` (English and Italian).
- Tests: `tests/modal-shell.test.ts` (every surface is on the shell, CSS scoped and balanced, tokens only). Harness: `dev/harness/modals-check.ts` screenshots every dialog at tablet and phone widths, light and dark (`dist/shots/design/modals-*.png`). Not verified on the device: manual steps 540-559 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Just-in-time tips (#425)
- **Just-in-time tips** (roadmap 425): a short tip the first time a situation occurs, shown once. Seven situations: the first notebook opened, the pen first touching a notebook, the pen first hovering with the side button set to open the radial menu, the first PDF imported, the first lasso selection, a finger touch right after the pen being ignored by palm rejection, and the first Inbox item. At most one tip every 4 minutes per session and never while the pen is writing (it waits for the pause). The bubble has a close button and *Turn off tips*.
- Settings, *Appearance & language*: **Tips** (on by default) and **Show tips again**. The seen tips are stored in the plugin settings (`hintsSeen`), not in a notebook: no change to the notebook format.
- Code: `src/ui/hint-logic.ts` (registry and rules, pure), `src/ui/hints.ts` (service and `hint(id)`), `src/ui/components/hint-bubble.ts`; one-line hooks in `notebook-view.ts` (opened, lasso), `library/host.ts` (PDF) and `zettel/inbox-service.ts` (Inbox). Pen and palm tips come from passive pointer listeners, with no change to the stroke pipeline.
- Tests: `tests/hints.test.ts`; harness scenario `doc=hints` (`dev/harness/hints-check.ts`).

### Art engine
- **Space-colonization art.** A new family, `veins` (organic venation: a leaf with a midrib, a river delta, a radial rosette), grown by the space colonization algorithm; deterministic from the seed. It is used by an `ArtSpec` without a style (and by the harness); folders and notebooks still store one of seven styles, so the app does not offer it yet.
- **OKLCH colour.** Tints, shades, glow colours, the cover gradient and the new `rampFor` palette helper are mixed in OKLCH instead of RGB: hues stay true and ramps are perceptually even.
- **Multi-pass glow.** Glow lines get a soft wide halo pass, a tighter glow and the crisp line, built from wide strokes (no `shadowBlur` or `filter`); the halo is skipped on wide bands.
- **Keyword motifs.** A fixed rule table maps title words (English and Italian: matematica / math, biologia / anatomy, fisica, storia, chimica, diritto, informatica...) to the art families that suit them; names without a keyword keep their old art. No AI.
- **Growth.** New optional `ArtSpec.growth` (0..1, `growthForPages(pages)`): the art gets denser, and from half way carries faint companion layers.
- **Faster and off the main thread.** A persistent image cache in IndexedDB (works without it) and `artImageAsync` / `coverImageAsync` / `emptyStateImageAsync` / `prewarmArt`, which render in an inline Worker on an OffscreenCanvas with a main-thread fallback. The in-memory LRU and the 0.12.3 failure guard are kept; the public API of the art engine is unchanged.
- Art gallery harness page: every family, growth steps, keyword motifs, pipeline checks.

## 0.12.5 — 2026-10-31

Pen gestures and rests, after 0.12.4 on the Samsung tablet. **Not verified on the device**: reproduced and fixed in unit tests that replay pen sequences against the real pointer controller, and checked in the browser harness; the manual steps are 660-665 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Fixed
- **A pen gesture started by resting the nib (setting *Long press starts a gesture*) no longer turns into the eraser.** Cause, reproduced by a test: since 0.12.4 a bare `contextmenu` up to 500 ms before the nib lands (it was 150 ms) makes that contact "button held, waiting for tap or drag". If the nib then rested for the gesture's 0.65 s and moved, the move counted as the drag: the gesture was cancelled and the sign was replayed as the eraser (or as the setting's tool). A stray `contextmenu` just before a landing is common (the WebView's own long-press, the contact before). Now, when a rest of the nib can really be taken (the hold-still radial menu is on, or *Long press starts a gesture* is on with gesture mode off and an ink tool), a contact that waits on a `contextmenu` alone and has rested for 650 ms (the time the gesture takes the rest) is a rest: the contact is ink, the rest goes on to the gesture or the menu, and the sign is drawn as usual. Before that time nothing changes: a still lift (even after 640 ms) is still a tap and opens the menu, a move is still a drag. The same rule applies when the WebView cancels the contact. Once decided, a later signal cannot turn the ink into the eraser. A contact that carries the button's own bit (`pointerdown` or `pointermove` with `buttons & 2`) is still the button however long the nib rests; with no rest feature on, nothing changes from 0.12.4 (a slow drag with the button still erases from the first sample). Tap with the button held (shorter than 350 ms) still opens the radial menu; drag still erases; a press in the middle of a stroke still keeps ink.
- **Checked, not the cause:** a side-button signal sets `penButtonSeen`, which only switches off the *automatic* hold-still radial menu default (`effectivePenHold`); it is read nowhere else, and *Long press starts a gesture* never depended on it (it also switches the hold-still menu off by itself). A test taps the button first and then rests and draws: still a gesture contact.
- No other hold feature changed: hold-still radial menu, the long-press gesture and the side button's *Pen gestures while held* behave as in 0.12.3 whenever no button signal is involved (checked against 0.12.3 over 1,440 synthetic sequences; the only differences are the intended 0.12.4 side-button rules).

### Changed
- *Pen button test* (diagnostics for the drag that still opens the radial menu on the tablet; which signal it sends is not known yet): every line of the log now also shows the time since the pen's landing (`dDown`) and since its lift (`dUp`); the lift line says how long the contact lasted and how far the nib went from where it landed (`still (a tap)` or `moved (a drag)`); the plugin's signal and decision are written next to the event that changed them; a bare `contextmenu` that no pen contact follows within the before-window is logged as `plugin: ... => decision: menu (hover click ...)`, so a menu that opens by itself is told apart from a tap (`menu (tap)`, `menu (tap, contextmenu after the lift)`); *Copy log* starts with one line of the settings the decision depends on (side button action, before / after windows, still radius, long-press time, hold-still menu, long-press gesture, click arms eraser). The test now decides rest-then-draw as the plugin does, keeps `SIDE BUTTON` on a barrel `pointerdown` next to the decision, and says `eraser armed` (not `menu`) for a hover click when *Button click: next stroke erases* is on. Pointer type, `button`, `buttons`, pressure and `isPrimary` were already on every line.
- New internal setting handed to the input policy (`restGesture`, from *Long press starts a gesture*); no new user setting, no format change.

## 0.12.4 — 2026-10-31

The S Pen side button on the Samsung tablet (Obsidian for Android), reworked around what the device really does. **Not verified on the device**: checked in unit tests that replay recorded pen sequences and in the browser harness only. Manual re-check: steps 493-499 in `docs/testing.md`. No change to the notebook format (still v11) or to the stroke engine and renderer.

What the device does: pressing the button with the pen in the air sends nothing the app can see (Obsidian's WebView does not pass hover on). The button shows only when the nib TOUCHES with it already held, and then only as a trace around that contact. The behaviour is now exactly two cases, whatever the form of the trace:

1. touch with the button held and lift without moving (a tap, within 12 px): the **radial menu**, where the nib landed, with no dot of ink;
2. touch with the button held and move: the **eraser for the whole contact**, from its first sample, with no ink (one undo step), also when the setting is *Radial menu on click*.

### Fixed
- **Root cause (reproduced by replaying recorded sequences against 0.12.3; which of them this tablet really sends has not been read from the device): a contact that carried the button opened the menu and then inked.** A bare `contextmenu` (no `pointerType`, `button` -1, `buttons` 0) with no pen contact in progress was taken for a click of the button while hovering, and a 150 ms timer opened the radial menu. When the pen's `pointerdown` came later than that (a `contextmenu` more than 150 ms before it), the menu opened on contact and the stroke that followed was plain ink. The same happened when the `contextmenu` came after the pen had lifted, outside the 350 ms window, even after a long drag (the menu opened after the drag). Tests: `tests/side-button-contact.test.ts` (about half of its sequences fail on 0.12.3).
- **One decision per pen contact** (`src/ink/button-contact.ts`, pure logic): every form of the signal is accepted and linked to the contact it belongs to: a bare `contextmenu` up to 500 ms before the pen down, during the contact (within 350 ms of the landing), or up to 400 ms after the pen up; a `pointerdown` with `button` 2 or `buttons & 2` and the nib touching; `buttons & 2` or the experimental `button 0` move within 350 ms of the landing; and the `pointercancel` the WebView sends right after a `contextmenu`, after which the contact goes on (a new `pointerdown` or more events of the same pointer within 250 ms) as the same logical contact; if nothing continues it, a contact that never moved was a tap. While the contact waits for tap or drag its ink is live but never committed: a tap discards it and opens the menu, a drag discards it and replays the whole path as the eraser (or the setting's tool: lasso, previous tool, gestures) from the first sample.
- **A button pressed in the middle of a normal stroke no longer switches it to the eraser** (`contextmenu`, `buttons & 2` or the `button 0` move later than 350 ms after the landing): ink goes on. A nib resting still for 350 ms is still a long-press, not the button. The pen's own eraser end (`buttons & 32`, `button` 5) is unchanged.
- **No menu during or after a drag.** The `buttons & 2` press during a young stroke, the chord event and a late `contextmenu` no longer open the menu; a `contextmenu` after a drag is ignored (a short drag within the window is retracted, one undo step, and replayed as the eraser, as before). The pressure-0 press while hovering (Samsung) and the pen key are unchanged.
- **Setting *Button before contact* is now 500 ms** by default (it was 150 ms, saved by every earlier version; a saved 150 ms reads as 500 ms). The same time is how long a bare `contextmenu` with no pen contact waits before it counts as a click while hovering (so a hover click acts up to 0.5 s late, and *Button click: next stroke erases* arms after the same wait). Two contacts with the button in a row are both recognised (the second `contextmenu` is no longer dropped as a repeat of the first press).
- With the side button set to *Erase while held*, *Lasso while held*, *Previous tool while held* or *Pen gestures while held* (no menu in the setting), a contact with the button held is the tool from the first sample, as before: there is no menu to wait for.

- **Review fixes.** (1) The second trace of one contact (a `contextmenu` just after the lift of a tap, an eraser drag or a cancelled contact) is a repeat of that contact: it no longer waits for the next landing and turns a plain stroke into the eraser. (2) After a `pointercancel` the contact goes on only when the pen lands within 24 px of its last sample; a landing farther away is a new contact (no eraser segment between the two points, and the dropped contact opens no menu). (3) A `contextmenu` up to 0.8 s after a contact that moved lifted never opens the menu; it can still be the button held for the contact that lands next. (4) A pen-typed `contextmenu` (Windows hover barrel click) is never taken for the button of the last contact: no retract. (5) The 150 ms to 500 ms migration of *Button before contact* runs once (`penButtonWindowsMigrated`), so 150 ms chosen afterwards is kept. (6) A slow move that leaves the radius after more than 350 ms still erases from the landing point. Known limit: an eraser contact the WebView cancelled and that goes on as a new pointer is recorded as two Erase undo steps (the stroke engine is untouched).

### Changed
- *Pen button test*: two new lines show the signal the plugin saw (`contextmenu Δ=+40 ms`, `pointerdown buttons=3`, `pointercancel after contextmenu`) and what it decided (`menu (tap)`, `eraser (drag)`, `ignored: <reason>`, English and Italian); they are included in *Copy log*. The intro explains that on Android the button is seen only while the nib touches.
- Setting texts say it honestly: on Obsidian for Android the button is seen only when the pen touches; a tap with the button held opens the radial menu, a drag with it erases (*Pen side button*, *Button click: next stroke erases*, *Button before / after contact*, *Button seen as a pen move*, which replaces the wording of *Button pressed while writing*: it no longer switches a stroke in the middle).

### Added
- Tests: `tests/side-button-contact.test.ts` (each signal form x tap / drag / no button / pressed in the middle of a stroke, for *Radial menu on click, erase while held*, *Radial menu on click*, *Erase while held*, *Lasso while held* and *Nothing*, plus `pointercancel` and the decision logic) with a replay rig (`tests/pen-contact-rig.ts`); older tests of the mid-stroke switch were rewritten to the new rule.
- Harness: real-event scenarios for the tap (menu on the lift, no dot), the drag (eraser, no menu, no ink) and a press in the middle of a stroke (ink).


## 0.12.3 — 2026-10-30

Fixes for what 0.12.2 showed on the tablet (Obsidian for Android, Italian, a vault with no notebooks): an empty creation dialog, a Home with only its title and search pill, an empty *Tag* page, a rail **+** sitting on the brand, and navy panels in the settings. **Not verified on the device**: checked in unit tests and in the browser harness only. Manual re-check: steps 485-492 in `docs/testing.md`. No change to the notebook format (still v11).

### Fixed
- **Root cause, found by audit and reproduced in the harness (likely, not confirmed on the device): every generated picture threw, and took the surface around it down.** The art engine made its canvas with `activeDocument.createEl("canvas")`. Obsidian's `createEl` on a node *appends the new element to that node*; on a Document that means a second root element, and the browser throws `HierarchyRequestError: Only one element on document allowed`. The harness's stand-in for Obsidian made `Document.createEl` a detached create, so it never failed there. With the stand-in made faithful, the harness shows exactly the tablet's screenshots: the header band's art is drawn before *+ Nuovo* and the bell, so the header stopped after the title and the search pill and Home / the *Tag* page drew nothing below it; the creation dialog's art band is drawn before its tabs, so it stayed an empty bar with Obsidian's ×. The rail (no art) and the settings tab were fine. Every detached element is now made with the global `createEl` (the art engine, thumbnails and paper previews, scan, images, PDF pages, excerpts, math, and the canvas that measures text boxes in `src/ink/renderer.ts`: one token, no change to how strokes are drawn). If the device still shows the problem, the error cards below name the real cause.
- **Decoration can no longer break a layout.** The art engine guards itself (`artImage`, `coverImage`, `emptyStateImage`, `folderIconSvg`): a failure is logged once, the caller gets no picture, and after three failures the engine stops trying for the session. The components guard their decoration again (`decorate()` in `ui/components/dom.ts`): subject cards fall back to a plain folder icon, the creation dialog's cover to its plain colour, paper tiles to their label. Home's header art is drawn after its controls.
- **Failures are visible.** Each part of Home, of the library dashboard and of the library view (rail, header, content) and the creation dialog (as a whole, and its preview and form separately) is drawn on its own; one that throws shows a small error card in its place, *Impossibile mostrare …: <message>* with the first stack frame, in the style of the settings tab's error, and the rest is still drawn.
- **No + in the rail.** The compact **+** next to the brand is removed (it sat on *Quaderni / per Obsidian*). Creation is the header's *+ Nuovo*, aligned in the header row beside the search pill and the bell, and Home's *Azioni rapide*, as in `home.png`.
- **Settings look native.** The settings tab drops its navy panel and band backgrounds: Obsidian's own surface with subtle borders (`--background-modifier-border`), Obsidian's text and accent colours, and the search pill without a band behind it (no longer sticky). Section headers, rows, toggles and spacing are unchanged. *Appearance → Style* still styles the plugin's own surfaces.
- Audited and **not** found in `src/`: built-ins newer than Chrome ~105 (`Object.groupBy`, `toSorted`, `Set` methods, `structuredClone`, `Promise.withResolvers`, …), `roundRect`, `ctx.reset` (feature-checked), `letterSpacing`, `checkVisibility`, the popover API, the regex `v` flag; no member of a Modal, ItemView or SettingTab subclass shadows a field of its Obsidian base. esbuild now targets `es2020` / `chrome100` (was `es2021`).

### Added
- Settings → *Diagnostics* → **Device info** (*Informazioni sul dispositivo*): the WebView's Chrome version, Obsidian's API version, the platform flags, the screen and the art engine's state, with the full user agent and a *Copy* button.
- Tests: `tests/compat.test.ts` (no too-new built-in in `src/`, no `createEl` on a Document, the esbuild target), `tests/robustness.test.ts` (the art engine drops decoration, error card text, device info), and `tests/settings-tab-members.test.ts` now checks every Modal, SuggestModal, ItemView/FileView and SettingTab subclass in `src/` against its base's fields.
- Harness: the stand-in for Obsidian appends on `Document.createEl` like Obsidian; a **hostile** mode (`doc=empty&hostile=1`) deletes newer built-ins and risky canvas APIs and makes the art engine and `toDataURL` throw, and checks that Home, the library, the *Tag* page, the empty state, the quick actions, *+ New* and both tabs of the creation dialog are drawn without art and without an error card (`dist/shots/design/hostile-*.png`). The settings scenarios draw on Obsidian's modal surface and check there is no panel background and that *Device info* is shown. The empty-vault checks now require no rail + and *+ New* aligned with the search pill.

Repository and documentation only: no change to the plugin or the notebook format.

### Added (repository)
- **README rebuilt as a showcase**: a banner and a gallery in dark and light (Home, library, writing with the radial menu and its colour and shapes rings, both tabs of the creation dialog, settings, generated art), badges, *Why Notebook*, install buttons for Community plugins and BRAT, grouped feature highlights, quick start, FAQ, roadmap numbers and contributing links. The long manual moved, unchanged apart from the 0.12.0 additions and the community directory now being live, into `docs/features.md` (features, settings, file format, now format 11), `docs/install.md` (all install routes, debugging on Android) and `docs/development.md` (commands, releasing, harness, roadmap page).
- **Public roadmap page** (`site/index.html`, GitHub Pages): counters, a progress bar, filters by status, area and priority, search, every feature with its status and release, and *Most requested* (open `feature-request` issues from the public GitHub API, sorted by 👍). No external fonts, cookies or analytics. Data from `scripts/roadmap-data.mjs` (docs/roadmap.md + CHANGELOG.md → `site/roadmap.json`), published by `.github/workflows/pages.yml`.
- Issue forms (`feature_request.yml`, `bug_report.yml`, `config.yml` linking to Discussions → Ideas) and `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- Harness: `dev/harness/banner.html` renders the README banner (1280 × 400), the social preview (1280 × 640) and the roadmap page's art band (1600 × 240, very low intensity, no text) with the art engine, the cover painter and a pen stroke from the real pen outline; `npm run harness:check` screenshots and checks them, and the new `npm run harness:media` copies them and downscales the curated screenshots into `docs/media/`. The settings tab is also screenshotted on a light desktop, and the harness has stand-ins for the *Writing*, *Export* and *Diagnostics* section icons (it showed two letters). Headless Chrome helpers moved to `dev/harness/chrome.mjs`.
- `.github/repo-settings.md`: description, topics, labels, homepage, Pages, Discussions and social preview settings for the maintainer.

## 0.12.2 — 2026-10-30

Two fixes for what 0.12.1 showed on the tablet. **Not verified on the device**: checked in unit tests and in the browser harness only. Manual re-check: steps 478-484 in `docs/testing.md`. No change to the notebook format (still v11).

### Fixed
- **Settings tab:** "Impossibile mostrare le impostazioni: this.name is not a function". Obsidian 1.13+ sets `name` and `icon` fields on a `SettingTab`, which shadowed our private `name()` helper. The helpers are renamed, with a regression test, and the harness stub now sets those fields.
- **Creation is always reachable, and an empty vault teaches the first steps.** On a vault with no notebooks, Home showed only its title and the search pill: no button to create a notebook or a folder. (The harness, on an empty vault, drew 0.12.1's statistics and quick actions, so what failed on the device after the header is not known; see below for the guard.) Now:
  - Home and the library end their header with a primary **+ New** pill (*Nuovo*), and the rail has a compact **+** next to the brand, also in the phone's rail sheet.
