Both open the New menu: New notebook, New folder, Import PDF, Scan (when available), Lecture from slides, Loose sheet. The library's old round + is replaced by the same pill.
  - Home's quick actions are always shown.
  - With no notebooks, Home and the library (*All*, *Subjects*, an empty notebooks folder) show an empty state: an illustration from the art engine's `emptyStateImage` (an icon with reduced motion), *Start your first notebook* (*Inizia il tuo primo quaderno*), one line, and *Create a notebook* (primary), *Create a folder*, *Import a PDF*. Home's statistics wait for the first notebook.
  - *Subject folders* with no subject folders shows a dashed *Create a folder* tile that opens the creation dialog's Folder tab, on Home, in the library dashboard and on the subjects page. An empty folder offers the same three buttons for that folder; lists that fill up by themselves (favourites, recent, tags, collections, archive, search) only explain how.
  - Each Home section is drawn on its own: a section that throws is logged (`[notebook] Home: the … section could not be drawn`) and the rest of Home, with creation, stays. A failing inbox bell no longer stops the header either.
  - The page title element is no longer called `titleEl`, the name of Obsidian's own view-header title field on an `ItemView`.
  - New shared components `ui/components/new-button.ts`, `empty-state.ts`, `create-tile.ts`; DESIGN.md §3 and §5 ("Creation is always reachable") and `docs/design-ui.md` §11 are updated.

### Added
- Harness: `doc=empty` scenarios (Home and the library, tablet and phone, dark and light) on a vault with no notebooks and no notebooks folder. They check the + New pill and its menu, the rail's +, the quick actions, the empty state and the dashed tile, and that each opens the real creation dialog on the right tab with pen, finger and mouse. Screenshots in `dev/harness/dist/shots/design/empty-*.png`.

## 0.12.1 — 2026-10-30

Fixes for two regressions of 0.12.0 found in Obsidian for Android: the new Home and the library rendered unstyled, and the plugin's settings tab was gone. **Not verified on the device**: the fixes are checked in unit tests and in the browser harness only, which now loads Obsidian's real button and input rules and runs the whole plugin's start-up. The exact error on the tablet could not be read from here, so part of the cause below is an inference; please report the developer console output if it happens again. Manual re-check: steps 470-477 in `docs/testing.md`. No change to the notebook format (still v11) or to the stroke pipeline.

### Fixed
- **Settings tab missing, and probably the unstyled Home too: one failing start-up step stopped the whole start-up.** `onload` ran some thirty registrations in a row with no guard, and registered the settings tab last. Any exception thrown before that line rejected `onload`. Obsidian then never registered the tab, and very likely never applied the plugin's `styles.css`, while the views registered earlier (Home, the library) still opened from the saved layout. That matches what the tablet showed: a working but completely unstyled Home and no *Notebook* entry under Community plugins. 0.12.0 also rebuilt the settings tab on Obsidian 1.13's declarative API, which Obsidian calls inside `addSettingTab` (to index it for search), and nothing outside Obsidian had ever started the plugin as a whole: the harness built each view on its own. Which step threw on the device is not known: the whole start-up runs cleanly against the harness's stand-in for Obsidian, so the failing call depends on the real app. The fix:
  - Every start-up step is guarded (`main.ts` `step()`): a failure is logged with the step's name, listed in `startupFailures` and reported once in a notice ("some features could not start (…)"), and the other steps still run.
  - The settings tab is registered first.
  - `onunload` copes with services that did not start.
  - The settings tab cannot fail Obsidian's call: `getSettingDefinitions()` never throws. If the definitions cannot be built it returns an empty list, so Obsidian 1.13+ falls back to the hand-drawn tab. Every row's `render` and `visible` callback is guarded, so one broken row cannot take the tab down, and a tab that cannot be drawn shows the error instead of a blank page.
  - An unreadable `data.json` no longer stops the start-up: the plugin runs on the defaults and never writes them over the file in that session (zero data loss).
- **Home, the library and the creation dialog showed Obsidian's grey buttons.** Obsidian's own stylesheet styles every button and text input with selectors of specificity 0,1,1 (`button:not(.clickable-icon)`, `button:hover`, `input[type=search]`: grey fill, shadow, fixed height and padding, no wrapping). These beat the plugin's single-class component rules (0,1,0) in real Obsidian. The harness's stand-in for Obsidian's CSS used a weaker `button` rule, so its screenshots looked right. Every rule of the 0.12 design-system section is now scoped under a new `nb-ui` class, set on the Home/library root and the creation dialog: exactly one class more per rule, so the rules keep their order among themselves. A reset inside the scope neutralises Obsidian's defaults, so component styles always win. The harness's base CSS now contains Obsidian's real button and input rules and the `is-mobile` / `is-phone` / `is-tablet` body classes. With them it reproduces the grey rail items, *Show all*, period dropdown and row menus of 0.12.0, which the new check catches.
- Not a cause: the 0.12.0 release's `styles.css` is byte-identical to the repository's, the stylesheet has balanced braces and no syntax a current Android WebView would drop, and the classes the CSS needs are set by the views themselves, not by the harness.

### Added
- Diagnostics: when Home or the library opens and the plugin's stylesheet is not applied, a notice says so (once per session) and the console logs it.
- Harness: a `doc=plugin` scenario runs the real `NotebookPlugin.onload` against the stub (`dev/harness/plugin-check.ts`). It checks what the plugin registers (settings tab, three views, commands, ribbon) and draws the registered settings tab through both of Obsidian's paths: 1.13+ definitions (the stub now has `update()` and a declarative renderer) and `display()`. It asserts that every section and every row is drawn. It also injects three failures: a start-up step that throws, settings definitions that throw, and an unreadable `data.json`.
- Harness: Home, the library and the creation dialog fail the check when the root is not a flex row, the rail is not a flex column, the subject cards are not on a grid, the tokens do not resolve, or any button shows Obsidian's default button look.
- `tests/styles.test.ts` fails on any design-system rule that is not scoped under `.nb-ui`.
- `npm run harness:check` starts Chrome with `--no-sandbox` when it runs as root (containers, CI).

## 0.12.0 — 2026-10-30

Document format v11: notebooks saved by 0.12.0 open read-only (with a backup) in 0.11.x and older; a v10 notebook is read unchanged and becomes v11 on its first save. The library index is rebuilt once (index v5). Checked in tests and the browser harness only; not verified in Obsidian or on Android hardware. Manual steps 407-469 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Added
- **Design system & Home / Library** (steps 430-449).
  - **Notebook home** (`src/library/home-view.ts`, `home-surface.ts`): a new view and command *Open Notebook home*; the ribbon icon opens it. Subject folder cards, recent notes (this device's recently edited pages), statistics over 1, 4 or 12 weeks computed on the device (notebooks created, pages edited, PDF notebooks changed, tags used, with a sparkline), quick actions (New notebook and New folder through the registry actions `new-notebook` / `new-folder`, Import PDF, Scan). Home and the library are two surfaces of one view: one index, one reading pass, one thumbnail queue.
  - **Inbox bell** (`src/library/inbox-bell.ts`) in the Home and library headers: the number of Zettelkasten Inbox items to process, a popover (a sheet on a phone) with the items and the Inbox's actions, and *Open inbox*. `zettel/index.ts` exposes the Inbox service (`inboxService(plugin)`).
  - **Design tokens** (`styles.css`, *Notebook design tokens*): the `--nb-*` palette of DESIGN.md §2 for the dark and light themes and the *Follow Obsidian theme* mapping on `body.notebook-style-obsidian`; the older token names are aliases, so the notebook view follows the same palette.
  - **Components** (`src/ui/components/`): subject card, section header, chips and filter chips, list row and pills, favourite row, KPI tile, quick action, search pill, navigation rail, icon square.
  - Decoration that switches off with reduced motion: generated art behind the page header, on subject cards, smart collections, quick actions and empty states, and a soft glow behind section titles (`src/ui/art/generative-art.ts` and `src/ui/palette.ts` are stubs of the art module until it is merged).
- **Creation dialog** and format v11 (steps 450-469).
  - **Creation dialog** (`view/create-dialog.ts`): one modal with the tabs *Notebook* and *Folder*. Notebook: a portrait cover preview (generated line art in a colour, or an image from the vault or the device) with *Change cover*, name, optional description, folder list with icons, page template tiles drawn by the real paper renderer (every paper kind), saved templates, page size A4 · A5 · B5 · Letter · Custom (mm) and orientation; the notebook is created through the usual checked path and opens. Folder: a folder card preview with *Change colour* and *Change pattern*, name, description, location, an icon row plus a search of every Lucide icon, eight subject colours plus the colour picker. Name errors show under the field (empty, invalid characters, already there); Enter submits; on a phone it is a full-height sheet with the preview on top. Faint generated art in the header, a glow behind the preview and on the selected template tile, off with reduced motion.
  - Registry actions `new-notebook` (Notebook tab) and `new-folder` (Folder tab; command *New folder*). The command *New notebook* keeps its id `create` and opens the dialog; the library's *New notebook* buttons and *New notebook here* open it too.
  - **Folder metadata** (`library/folder-meta*.ts`): colour, icon, description and art style of a folder, stored per device in `_meta/folders-<device>.json`, merged on read (latest change wins per folder and field), following renames and moves; unreadable files are never overwritten. Read API `getFolderMeta(path)` / `onFolderMetaChange(fn)` on `host.folderMeta` for the library cards (see `docs/architecture.md`).
  - Format v11: `meta.description` and `meta.coverArt` (`{ kind: "art", style, seed }` or `{ kind: "image", asset }`), with the v10 → v11 migration, lenient read and strict write.
  - Page size preset **B5** (176 × 250 mm), also in the page size menus.
- **Generated art** (steps 425-429).
  - Procedural subject art (`src/ui/art/`, DESIGN.md §2.6): deterministic line art from a seed and a subject colour in seven families (branches, cells, waves, contours, flow, orbits, grid), with a right-to-left fade and a soft glow in the subject colour. `drawArt`, cached `artImage` (also faint header bands through `intensity`), `coverImage` (portrait notebook covers), `emptyStateImage` and the glossy `folderIconSvg`; `artEnabled()` switches it all off under reduced motion. Images are cached (LRU, 40 entries) by spec, size and pixel ratio. Not used by any surface yet; not verified on a device.
  - Subject palette (`src/ui/palette.ts`): the eight colours of DESIGN.md §2.3 and `colorFor(name)`, a stable hash into them.
  - Harness: an art gallery page (`dev/harness/art.html`), screenshotted by `npm run harness:check` in dark and light at desktop, tablet and phone widths, with timing, determinism and cache checks.
- **Settings** (steps 407-413).
  - New setting *Appearance & language → Style* (`appearanceStyle`): *Notebook* (default, the `--nb-*` tokens of DESIGN.md §2) or *Follow Obsidian theme* (the tokens mapped onto Obsidian's variables). For now it styles the settings tab, the first surface built on these tokens.
  - The PDF reading options (*Snap highlighter to text*, *Dim backgrounds*, *Dimming strength*, *Crop PDF margins*) and *Night mode*, until now only toggled from a notebook, are also in the settings tab (same keys).
- **S Pen** (steps 414-424).
  - S Pen click while hovering: Obsidian for Android reports it only as one bare `contextmenu` (no `pointerType`, no pointer in progress). Such an event over the notebook view, with no pointer down and no text field focused, now opens the radial menu (at the event's position, else the last pen position, else the centre of the page area). A finger long-press (a `contextmenu` while a finger is down, or within 600 ms of a long contact), a mouse right click and text fields keep their behaviour (`ink/pen-hover.ts`).
  - Setting *Button click: next stroke erases* (`penButtonEraserNext`, off by default): the hover click arms the eraser for the next pen stroke only, shown by a small ring on the current-pen indicator; a second click, a tool change or Escape disarms it.
  - Settings *Pen key* (`penButtonKey`: off, F9 to F12 or a captured key) and *Pen key action* (`penButtonKeyAction`: radial menu or next stroke erases, default the latter): the key acts as the side button in the active notebook view (no repeats, not in text fields or modals, only that key cancelled), with a warning when an Obsidian hotkey uses it. Meant for an S Pen mapped to a key with Key Mapper + Shizuku. The rows are in Settings → *Pen & input* (`PEN_BUTTON_EXTENSIONS`); the pen key row (dropdown, key capture, hotkey warning) is drawn by `view/pen-button-settings.ts`.
  - *Pen button test*: a read-only Capacitor inventory line (platform, plugin headers and keys, bridge-like globals; nothing is called); key events show `key`, `code` and `repeat` in the last-event line too.
  - The side button seen only as a `contextmenu` around a pen contact (device finding: on the Samsung tablet tested, the pen's pointer events never show the button). A bare `contextmenu` at most 150 ms before or 350 ms after the pen's pointerdown (new settings `penButtonContactBeforeMs`, `penButtonContactAfterMs`, in *Pen & input → Advanced*) makes the whole contact the eraser: when it comes after the landing, the live ink of the contact is dropped (nothing committed) and the samples so far are replayed as the eraser; when it comes only after the pen up of a short flick (within the after-window of its landing), the flick's stroke is undone (one undo step) and replayed as the eraser. Later in the stroke, the ink so far is committed and the rest of the contact erases, never touching that kept ink (in *Stroke* mode it used to delete the stroke just written, in *Segment* mode to cut its tail; the same for `buttons & 2` and the chord event); the pen up ends it. A nib resting still (within 12 CSS px) for 350 ms is a long-press, not the button, even inside the after-window, which is now at most 400 ms (larger saved values read as 400). With a finger or palm on the page, or lifted less than 600 ms before, a `contextmenu` during a pen stroke changes nothing. The event is always cancelled. A hover click now waits for the before-window. The stroke pipeline is unchanged: only which tool the contact uses.
  - *Pen button test*: notes the order and the gaps between the pen's pointerdown, the `contextmenu` and the first pointermove (`contextmenu Δ=+42 ms after pen down`), and shows a verdict line (button held at contact, during stroke, while hovering).
  - The side button held while the nib touches: a pen landing with `buttons & 2` or `button` 2 and pressure above 0 now starts the button's tool (the eraser by default) instead of opening the radial menu; with pressure 0 (a press while hovering) it still opens the menu. `buttons & 2` / `button` 2 or `buttons & 32` / `button` 5 appearing on a move of an ordinary stroke keep the ink so far and make the rest of the contact the eraser (or the button's tool). The stroke pipeline is unchanged.

### Changed
- **Settings tab reorganised by task** (DESIGN.md §6; not verified in Obsidian or on a device, manual steps 407-413 in `docs/testing.md`). Sections in a fixed order: *Writing*, *Pen & input*, *Pages & navigation*, *PDF & reading*, *Library & files*, *Sync & safety*, *Export* (shown once it has a setting), *Obsidian integration*, *Study*, *Appearance & language*, *Diagnostics*. Each section has an icon header and its most-used options first; the rest sits in a collapsed *Advanced* group. Dependent options appear only while their parent is on (for example the companion note options, *Dimming strength*, *Fast scrollbar* in continuous scroll, the edge zones), and the options of pen gestures, the class timetable and the Zettelkasten stay collapsed while the feature is not in use. A search pill at the top filters every setting by name, description and section in English and Italian (accents ignored), keeping the section headers of the matches. Names are short and in sentence case, with at most one line of description (strings rewritten in English and Italian). Same keys, defaults and behaviour; no change to the notebook format.
- One definition list (`src/view/settings-schema.ts`: section, group, order, visibility, control) now drives both the declarative tab of Obsidian 1.13+ and the hand-drawn tab of older versions, which had drifted apart (the timetable options were missing from the 1.13+ tab, *Show card regions* did not redraw the regions there). The per-feature `display…Settings` / `…SettingDefinitions` helpers are gone. The *Pen & input* section has an extension point for the S Pen button settings (`PEN_BUTTON_EXTENSIONS`).
- The colour picker's eyedropper button is optional (absent where there is nothing to pick from).
- Creation dialog, Folder tab: the preview is now the library's own subject card (shared component `ui/components/subject-card.ts`: glossy folder icon with the chosen symbol, name, description, the folder's line art seeded by the path it will have), shown wide above the form instead of a portrait card. Notebook tab: a shuffle button next to *Change cover* draws a new variation of the pattern (new seed); the cover uses the real `coverImage`. Not verified in Obsidian or on a device.
- The S Pen settings (*Button click: next stroke erases*, *Pen key*, *Pen key action*, the side button timing) are in the settings tab (*Pen & input*, timing under *Advanced*) and no longer at the top of the *Pen button test*, which keeps its diagnostics. Their descriptions are shorter (one line).
- Design tokens: one global `--nb-*` set (DESIGN.md §2.1/§2.2) for every surface, with *Style → Follow Obsidian theme* as `body.notebook-style-obsidian`; the settings tab and the creation dialog no longer declare their own copies.
- **Library rebuilt** (`src/library/library-view.ts`): a navigation rail (Home, Notebooks, PDF, Tags, Settings, the folder tree with subject colours, Archive, Trash, Check library) that folds to icons on a tablet in portrait and becomes a sheet on a phone; header with search, tag filter, sort, grid / list and *New*; filter chips; subject cards; smart collections (To study, Favourites, With PDF, Recent, Exams, computed and shown only when not empty); frequent tags; recent and favourite notebooks with page thumbnails; the grid of all notebooks drawn 60 at a time; search results grouped as folders, tags and notebooks. Pin, archive, manual order, drag and drop, the library check, import, scan, lecture, move, rename and delete keep their code paths.
- The *Favourites* filter now also lists pinned notebooks (the *Favourites* smart collection).
- Library index v5: entries also count PDF pages and open markers (gap, question, homework), for *With PDF* and *To study*, and carry the notebook's v11 cover (`coverArt`; an image cover as the vault path of its asset).
- Home and library cards read the folder metadata (`host.folderMeta`): colour, symbol on the folder icon and art style of each subject folder, falling back to `colorFor(name)` and `artStyleFor(name)`; they redraw when it changes. Notebooks made with a cover show it in the grid instead of their first page.
- *New notebook* and *New folder* of Home and the library (quick actions, *New* menu, folder menu) open the creation dialog (registry actions `new-notebook` / `new-folder`, or `host.createNotebook(folder)` / `host.createFolder(parent)` inside a folder); the library's old folder-name prompt is gone.

### Fixed
- **Repair kept too little of a v11 notebook's details.** When the image of a notebook's cover was lost, the cover failed the check and the repair fell back to the bare metadata, dropping the description and a valid generated cover too. The description and the cover are now checked on their own (like the bookmark details): a valid description and a valid generated or image cover are kept, and only an image cover whose picture is gone is dropped (and counted in the repair summary).
- **Custom page size followed the orientation instead of the numbers.** 300 × 200 mm typed with *Portrait* selected made a 200 × 300 page. The millimetres are now applied as typed; the selected orientation follows the typed numbers, and choosing the other orientation swaps the two fields.
- **Creation dialog: names that cannot work are refused inline** (English and Italian), for notebooks and folders: the plugin's own folders directly in the notebooks folder (`_assets`, `_backup`, `_meta`, `_stamps`, `_templates`, `_trash`, any case), a trailing dot or space, and Windows device names (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`, with or without an extension).
- **A new notebook or folder could write over one whose name differs only in case** on a case-insensitive disk (Windows, macOS, Android storage). The dialog's *already there* check and the creation itself now ignore case (Obsidian's insensitive lookup when present, else the folder listing), and the dialog checks the name as it will be created (the same clean-up as the file name). `vault.create` is called only when nothing exists at that path in any case; a taken name gets " 2", " 3"… as before.
- **Check library: device sidecars no longer make the check incomplete.** `folders-<device>.json`, `library-<device>.json`, `library-check-<device>.json` and their `.unreadable-…` copies cannot name an asset, so they are no longer read as possible references; an unsynced or empty one no longer stops the check from offering anything. Every other file that can name an asset is still read.
- **Subject cards: the generated art no longer runs under the text.** On the light Home cards at tablet width the drawing showed under the count ("3 notebooks"). The art now takes only the space beside the icon and text (in a row from the end of the text to the right edge, in a tile the column right of them), in both themes and at every width; a long name narrows the art before it is cut.

## 0.11.2 — 2026-10-30

No change to the notebook format (still v10). Clean-up for Obsidian's community Scorecard (runtime code and styles); not verified in Obsidian or on a device.

- `tests/` and `dev/` are now lint-clean under the same rules (about 1,800 problems fixed, no rules disabled), and `npm run lint` checks all three folders.
- New `DESIGN.md`: the visual system (tokens, subject palette, type scale, components, surfaces) taken from the reference designs in `docs/design/reference/`, and the rule that every feature adds its settings organically.

### Changed
- `npm run lint` now also runs `eslint-plugin-obsidianmd` 0.4.2 (`recommended`, type-checked; MIT, devDependency only) on `src/**` and `package.json`; a commented block in `eslint.config.mjs` extends it to `tests/**` and `dev/**`.
- Destructive buttons (delete marker or box, remove bookmark, library confirmations) use `setDestructive()` on Obsidian 1.13+ and the same `mod-warning` class as before on older versions (`ui/destructive.ts`).
- The settings tab no longer calls the deprecated `display()` itself: on Obsidian older than 1.13 a language change redraws through the tab's own drawing method (same content).
- Deleting the plugin's own bookkeeping files (presence files, page-trash entry files, library-trash meta files and empty item folders) goes through one documented helper, `deletePluginFile` (`storage/plugin-files.ts`), with exactly the previous behaviour; user content still goes only through `fileManager.trashFile`. Every call site is listed in `docs/architecture.md` ("Deleting files").
- Styles: print rules without `!important` (higher specificity instead) and with the `page-break-*` names only; the screen-reader live region of the radial menu hidden with `opacity` instead of `clip-path`; MathJax's output matched by its `jax` attribute instead of the `mjx-container` element name.

### Fixed
- Lint findings in runtime code: `globalThis` in the Zettelkasten id suffix (now the `crypto` global, same fallback), `document.createElement` in page thumbnails (now `activeDocument.createEl`).

## 0.11.1 — 2026-10-30

No change to the notebook format (still v10). S Pen side-button detection and a much more complete Pen button test log; not verified on the device. Manual steps 404-406 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Fixed
- **S Pen side button: the Pen button test showed nothing while the button was pressed** (user report, Samsung tablet, 0.10.1), while Chrome's pen-tracker page on the same tablet shows the press (`pointerdown`, `button 0`, `buttons 1`, `pressure 0` with the pen hovering). The root cause could not be proven without the device. What the code did differently from pen-tracker: the test listened to seven event types (`pointerdown` / `move` / `up` / `cancel` / `rawupdate`, `contextmenu`, `auxclick`) in the bubble phase on a 140 px area only, so an event that targeted another element, that a handler above the area stopped, or that arrived as another type (mouse compatibility events only, `pointerover` / `enter`, capture changes, touch, selection) was never shown; the notebook only listened on the page element. Now:
  - The **Pen button test** records every pointer, mouse, touch, `contextmenu`, `auxclick`, `click`, capture, selection and key event **at window level in the capture phase**, wherever it is dispatched, with type, `pointerType`, pointer id, `button`, `buttons`, pressure, primary flag, target element and both timestamps. Like pen-tracker it also shows the values of the last event (including tilt), and a count per event type. Hover moves of pointer, mouse and `pointerrawupdate` fold into one line each; the log keeps 80 lines. If the count does not change while the button is pressed, the button does not reach Obsidian at all.
  - While a notebook is open, the side-button detector also listens **at window level (capture phase) over the whole notebook view**: a press over the toolbar or another bar lying on the page, or one stopped before it reached the page, still opens the radial menu (only when the pen is over the page, never a stroke). Events that reach the page are handled once, by the page's own listeners as before. Events outside the view (modals, other views, Obsidian's own UI) are ignored.
  - A `mousedown` that no pointer event announced (no `pointerdown` within 500 ms, no finger within 1 s, not derived from touch), with `button` 0 or 2 while the pen hovers, now counts as the side button too, in case the WebView sends the button only as a mouse event.
  - **Native long-press and context menu are cancelled, as pen-tracker does** (on Samsung the button may surface as a `contextmenu` or a long-press, and a WebView that handles it itself cancels the pen stream with `pointercancel`): a `contextmenu` anywhere in the notebook view (capture phase, not passive, on the view only) is cancelled when it comes from the pen, has no `pointerType`, or the pen is hovering or touching, and is judged as a side-button signal like the others (a bare nib long-press on the page is still not the button). Text fields keep their menu; modals and the rest of Obsidian are untouched. The pen's `pointerdown` on the page (the stage, `touch-action: none`, or its canvases) is now cancelled also when it draws nothing (read-only, palm edge); controls on the page keep their default. The Pen button test area cancels `contextmenu`, `auxclick` and the pen's `pointerdown` too, and the whole test content has `touch-action: none` (the log still scrolls vertically); the window-level log stays passive.
  - Unchanged: a click while hovering opens the radial menu on release; holding the button and touching erases; a pressure-0 pen `pointerdown` after hovering is the button, never a stroke start (a real contact has pressure above 0); the press during writing stays experimental. The stroke pipeline (smoothing, shape, rendering) is untouched.
## 0.11.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware or in Obsidian. Document format v10: notebooks saved by 0.11.0 open read-only (with a backup) in 0.10.x and older. Manual steps 384–403 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Upgrade notes
- **Document format v10** (0.10 wrote v9). Notebooks saved by 0.11.0 open **read-only** (with a backup) in 0.10.x, 0.9.x and older builds, which read up to v9. v9 files are read unchanged and rewritten as v10 only on the first edit. The only change is the new `excerpt` element; nothing else changes in how notebooks are saved.

### Added
- **Anchored excerpts** (roadmap 105, format v10; manual steps 384-403). Cut a region of a page (a PDF page or any other page) and drop it on another page; the excerpt remembers exactly where it came from.
  - *Excerpt to page…* on the contextual bar of a lasso selection, on the lasso bar with nothing selected, in the *All actions* sheet (Insert) and as a command. With a selection the region is the selection's box (plus 4 pt); with nothing selected the next drag on a page outlines the region. The go-to-page picker then asks where it goes: this page, the next page, a new page after this one, or any page by number or bookmark. The excerpt is placed at the region's size (scaled down to fit the page), selected, and added in one undo step.
  - *Copy as excerpt* (same places) puts the excerpt on the plugin clipboard: paste it (lasso → *Paste*) on any page of **any notebook**.
  - The excerpt is an element like a picture: it can be selected, moved and resized with the lasso (uniformly: it never rotates or stretches), copied, duplicated, deleted, reordered among pictures, and ink written on it moves with it.
  - **Jump back**: a pen, finger or mouse tap on the badge in its top-right corner (also in read-only mode) (a stroke that starts on the badge and moves stays ink) (or *Go to source* on the contextual bar of a selected excerpt) goes to the source: the same notebook scrolls to the page and outlines the region for a moment; another notebook is opened at the source page (found by its stable id, so it still works after pages were reordered) with the region outlined.
  - **What is drawn**: an excerpt of a page of the same notebook is drawn **live** from its source (writing on the source shows in the excerpt a moment later); otherwise, and in exports, thumbnails and the Markdown page images, it shows the **snapshot** taken when it was made (a PNG image asset in `_assets`). See docs/architecture.md (v10).
  - **A source that is gone** never breaks anything: a deleted source page turns the badge grey and a tap says "Source not found"; a source notebook that was moved or renamed is found by its document id (its path, then its name, then the whole vault), never another notebook with the same name, and the stored path is updated; when it is not found a tap says so. The excerpt keeps showing its snapshot (or a placeholder card when it has none) and nothing is changed or lost.

### Changed
- *Page information* counts excerpts; the files of a page include excerpt snapshots; the library check never reports a snapshot as unused; the PNG export and printing draw the snapshot; the clipboard between devices and the lasso across pages carry excerpts. Every path that copies pages or elements (duplicate, page restore, page trash, *Move to notebook…*, templates, stamps, clipboard, conflicted-copy merge, repair) keeps excerpts and brings their snapshot asset along.

## 0.10.1 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware or in Obsidian. No change to the notebook format (still v9). Manual steps 380-383 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Added
- **Language setting** (*Settings, Language*): Automatic (follows Obsidian, default), English or Italiano; applies at once to the settings tab and to anything opened afterwards. Open notebooks update when reopened, and command names follow the language chosen at startup (a notice says so). The message tables now live in `src/i18n/en.ts` and `src/i18n/it.ts` with a `LANGUAGES` registry, so adding a language is one file; `tests/i18n.test.ts` checks that English and Italian have the same keys, that every key used by `t("…")` in `src/` exists, that no key was dropped, plus empty strings, `{placeholders}` and duplicate keys.

## 0.10.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware. No change to the notebook format (still v9). Manual steps 275–379 in `docs/testing.md`.

### Added
- **Navigation** (roadmap 363, 353, 18, 50, O15; steps 275-280, 315-334).
  - **Tabs** (roadmap 363; steps 275-277): the new setting *Tabs of open notebooks* (off by default) shows a compact strip above the toolbar that lists the notebooks open in Obsidian (the leaves of the notebook view type) and switches between them; two notebooks with the same name show their folder. The strip appears when two or more notebooks are open. A two-finger sideways swipe on the toolbar strip (or the tab strip) goes to the next or previous notebook, whether or not the tab strip is shown; the page never reacts to it, so the pen and one-finger gestures are untouched. The commands *Go to the next / previous open notebook* do the same from the keyboard. Only Obsidian's public workspace API is used.
  - **Automatic line break** (roadmap 353; steps 278-280): the new setting *Automatic line break* (off by default). On ruled or grid paper, when the pen lifts within the right 12 % of the visible area, the view scrolls smoothly after a short pause (never while the pen or a finger is down; any touch cancels it) so that the start of the next line is under the hand: the left edge of the page at the left of the view, one line height below the pen. If that would leave the line in the bottom band, the view also moves up. In one-page-at-a-time mode it stays on the page; it does nothing when the whole page width already fits, on blank or dotted paper, or when the next line is off the page (automatic next page owns that). When it applies, it replaces the bottom-band auto-scroll for that stroke.
  - **Back and forward through visited pages** (roadmap 18; steps 315-320). New actions and commands *Back* and *Forward* (registry ids `nav-back`, `nav-forward`). They remember deliberate jumps only (a page link, a thumbnail or bookmark in the page panel, *Go to page number…*, a search result, a marker, the fast scrollbar, opening another notebook or a recent page), within and across notebooks, up to 50 places, for the session only (nothing is saved). Back returns to the place you actually left; going back and then jumping elsewhere discards the forward places, as in a browser. Entries follow a renamed notebook and are skipped when the notebook or page is gone. They show as quick slots of the toolbar (once there is a history and the width allows), in the *Go to* section of the page sheet and the *All actions* sheet, and on desktop the mouse's back and forward buttons use them while the pointer is over a notebook. No format change.
  - **Fast scrollbar with a page preview** (roadmap 50; steps 321-327). In continuous scroll a thumb along the right edge appears when you scroll (or when a mouse nears the edge) and fades. Dragging it runs through the notebook; a bubble shows the page number and a thumbnail of the page. The thumbnail comes from the page panel's cache and is drawn only when the thumb rests, so a fast drag costs no more than a pan. Setting *Fast scrollbar* (on by default); not shown in one-page-at-a-time mode.
  - **Status bar item** (roadmap O15; steps 328-332). On desktop Obsidian's status bar shows "p. 3/12 · Saved" for the active notebook (page in view; saved, saving, unsaved changes or save failed); clicking it opens the page panel. Obsidian mobile has no status bar, so no item is created there.
- **Clipboard and pages** (roadmap 356, 359, 362; steps 281-294).
  - **Date and time stamp** (roadmap 356; steps 281-283): *Date and time stamp* in the All actions sheet and the command palette inserts a text box with the date and time where the pen last touched the page (the middle of the page in view when there was no recent touch), in the format set in *Settings > Date and time stamp format* (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm` by default; `[text]` stays as written; month and weekday names follow Obsidian's language). One undo step; the box is selected afterwards.
  - **Lasso selection across pages** (roadmap 359; steps 284-288): a lasso that reaches into another page selects the ink and elements inside it on every page it touches. Move, scale, rotate, delete, restyle, copy, cut, duplicate and layer order work on the whole selection; after a move each item belongs to the page under it, as one undo step. A lasso inside one page works as before.
  - **Cross-device clipboard** (roadmap 362; steps 289-294): *Copy to other devices* writes the selection to `_meta/clipboard-<device>.json` (this device's own file, last five copies, 3 MB cap); *Paste from another device…* lists the copies of the other devices, newest first, and pastes the chosen one with new ids. Only the copy action writes; an unreadable, empty or newer-version file of this device is never overwritten (a copy is kept and the copy is refused).
- **Selection** (roadmap 63, 64, 38, 65; steps 295-314; no format change).
  - **Rectangle selection** (roadmap 63; steps 295-297): the lasso tool has a second shape. *Rectangle selection* is a toggle on the lasso's bar, in the lasso's tool options (tap the lasso again: *Lasso* / *Rectangle*), in the All actions sheet and a command. Dragging draws the box from corner to corner; when the pen lifts it selects exactly what a lasso of that outline would (same rules, and invisible ink stays out unless the solutions are shown). A tap still picks the object under it. The mode is not remembered after the notebook closes.
  - **Select by colour or tool** (roadmap 64; steps 298-300): *Select similar* on the second row of a selection's bar opens *Same colour*, *Same tool* and *Pick colour or tool…*. The same two are in the All actions sheet and are commands; the picker (*Select by colour or tool*, also on the lasso's bar, in the sheet and as a command) lists the tools, the colours and the tool-and-colour pairs on the page with how many strokes each would select. A colour also takes text boxes of that colour; a tool takes strokes only. The result replaces the selection.
  - **Align and distribute** (roadmap 38; steps 301-304): *Align and distribute* on the second row of a selection's bar has *Align left / centre / right / top / middle / bottom* and *Distribute horizontally / vertically*; all eight are in the All actions sheet and are commands. "As groups": each picture, text box, equation, graph, box or marker is one item, and strokes that touch or lie within 6 pt of each other are one item, so a written word or a drawing is never torn apart. Items move rigidly, to the edge or centre of the whole selection; distributing makes the gaps between neighbours equal and needs three items. Ink written on a selected picture or text box goes with it when *Ink follows objects* is on. One undo step each.
  - **Rotate, mirror, exact size** (roadmap 65; steps 305-308): *Rotate, mirror, size* on the second row of a selection's bar has *Rotate 90° clockwise / anticlockwise*, *Mirror left to right / top to bottom* and *Exact size…*; all are in the sheet and are commands. They are applied to the vector points in page coordinates (pressure and everything else kept), about the centre of the selection; pictures turn by 90°. Mirroring flips ink; pictures, text boxes, equations and graphs have no flipped form in the format, so they are moved to the mirrored place and keep their orientation (a notice says so). *Exact size* opens a panel with width and height in pt (stroke thickness included), *Keep proportions* on by default, and keeps the top-left corner; text boxes and equations scale in proportion only. One undo step each.
  - **The selection bar has a second row** for these (Select similar, Align and distribute, Rotate / mirror / size), above the main row and under the colour rows, so the first row keeps the entries of wave 0.9 (copy, cut, duplicate, paste, delete, to front, to back…).
- **Library** (roadmap 54, 27; steps 335-349).
  - **Pinned notebooks, manual order and an archive** (roadmap 54; steps 335-342). From a notebook's menu: *Pin to the top* (pinned notebooks form a *Pinned* group at the top of every folder, tag and favourites view), *Archive* (the notebook disappears from every view, search, count and tag list; the sidebar shows an *Archived* entry, once something is archived, where *Restore from the archive* brings it back) and, in a plain list, *Move up* and *Move down*. The sort list has a new *Manual order*: drag a card onto another (mouse, pen, or long press on touch) to take its place, or use the menu entries; choosing *Move up* / *Move down* from another sort first keeps the order as shown and then moves. Notebook files are never moved or renamed to archive them, and nothing is written into a notebook. The state is kept **per device in the vault**: `<notebooks folder>/_meta/library-<device>.json`, one file per device like the Inbox and the practice copies, so two devices syncing through Google Drive or OneDrive never write the same file; reading merges them (per notebook and field the latest change wins). Notebooks are keyed by their document id, so renaming or moving them keeps their state. An unreadable or empty own file is never overwritten (a copy is kept and the change refused with a notice).
  - **Library check** (roadmap 27; steps 343-349). *Check library: unreadable notebooks and unused files* (sidebar of the library, All actions, notebook menu, command palette). It lists the notebooks that cannot be read (only listed: it never touches a notebook), those saved by a newer version, and those that are not downloaded yet or cannot be read right now (never reported as damaged). It finds the files in `_assets` that nothing uses and reports the space they take. An asset is unused only when no notebook anywhere in the vault names it and nothing that can bring a notebook back does either (version history and restore copies, the plugin trash, templates, stamps, `_meta`); those are reported as *kept*. *Review and move aside…* lists every file with its size and moves them, after an explicit confirmation, to `<notebooks folder>/_trash/assets/` inside the vault (a rename, never a delete; to put one back, move it back into `_assets`); the check runs once more first and a file that is in use again, changed, or not an asset file is left alone. While any file that could use an asset is not downloaded, no asset is called unused; empty asset files are not judged, and a file is offered only after an earlier check on this device, at least a day before, also found it unused.
- **Export and view** (roadmap 51, 81; steps 350-364).
  - **Read-only mode** (roadmap 51; steps 350-356). *Read-only mode* (All actions → View & input, command) switches the open notebook view to read-only: the pen, a finger and the mouse never write, erase or edit, no tool can be chosen, undo and redo do nothing, and nothing can be inserted, pasted, deleted or reordered (the history itself refuses to run commands, as a last guard). Scrolling, zooming, the page panel, links, search, page-by-page mode and exports keep working; a finger always pans and zooms, and a tap on a link opens it. A lock button in the toolbar shows the mode (it replaces the pen indicator and the favourite pens, which cannot be used) and switches it off with one tap. It is a view state only: nothing is stored in the file, nothing is written or backed up, and it ends when the notebook is closed. Whatever was being written is finished and saved normally just before it switches on. It reuses the view's existing read-only path (the same "not editable" state as the other-device banner and hidden PDF annotations) but is independent of them.
  - **Export all pages as PNG images** (roadmap 81; steps 357-359, 363). *Export…* has a new format, *PNG images* (also *Export pages as PNG images…* in the All actions sheet and the notebook menu, and a command): one PNG per page in a vault folder (suggested: *Notebook (PNG)* next to the notebook, created when missing), at a scale of 1x to 4x (1x = 72 dpi; very large pages are drawn smaller, as in the Markdown export), with the same *Contents* choice as the other formats (ink only gives transparent images). Files are named by the real page number (`page-003.png`) and a name that exists gets " 2", " 3"…; nothing is replaced and nothing is deleted when it fails.
  - **Page ranges** (roadmap 81; steps 358-359). The *Export…* dialog has *Some pages…* for every format: type pages like `1-3, 5, 8-`; a page that does not exist is refused with a message instead of being skipped. A PDF of a range is named `Name p2-4.pdf`, an SVG range is one file per page.
  - **Print** (roadmap 81; steps 360-362). *Export…* → Print (also *Print…* in the All actions sheet, the notebook menu and the command palette) draws the chosen pages like the PNG export, puts them one per sheet in a container that is only visible when printing, and opens the system print dialog; there you can also save as PDF. Pages are scaled to the sheet; a very long infinite page flows over several sheets. Nothing is written to the vault. Printing exists on desktop only: on a phone or tablet, which have no print dialog, the *Print…* actions and the format are hidden (use PNG or PDF export instead).
- **Colour** (roadmap 7, 68; steps 365-379).
  - **Free colour picker and eyedroppers** (roadmap 7 and 68; steps 365-379). The colour row of the tool options and the radial colour ring now offer a free colour picker: a saturation / brightness area, a hue bar, a hex field (`#rrggbb` or `rgb`), the recent custom colours and an eyedropper button. The last 12 custom colours are remembered in the plugin settings (`recentColours`; no change to the notebook format). The *Eyedropper* picks the colour under the next contact on the page by reading the rendered canvases at the device pixel under it (so the device pixel ratio and the zoom are taken into account), with a swatch above the contact while it is down. The *Style eyedropper* takes the tool, colour and width of the stroke you tap (hit test on the vector points in page coordinates; a shape stroke gives the pen). Three new registry actions and commands: *Colour picker…*, *Eyedropper: pick a colour from the page*, *Style eyedropper: take colour, width and tool from a stroke*.

### Changed
- **With the format v9 maths elements**: a selection across pages, *Copy to other devices* and *Paste from another device…* carry equations and graphs like any other element (checked by the strict validation before anything is written). Ink bound to a graph keeps its binding when a selection moves between pages; a pasted copy gets new ids, so its ink follows the geometric rule, as with an ordinary paste. The maths actions of the contextual bar (*Edit*, *Convert to ink*) act on the equations and graphs on the page the selection started on.
- The library index entries keep the notebook's document id (`id`, optional; no index version bump: entries without it are filled in the background from the start of each file, cheaply).
- The page drawing of the Markdown export moved to a shared module (`view/page-images.ts`) used by the PNG export and printing; its output is unchanged, except that a picture that cannot be loaded now counts in the "images could not be included" notice too.
- The radial dial's custom colour button opens the new picker instead of the system colour dialog, and its eyedropper uses the new page eyedropper (which now also ends on Escape or a tap on the toolbar instead of swallowing it).
- Dragging, scaling or rotating a selection with the handles now goes through the same pure routine as the new operations (`applyPlacement`); the result and the undo step are the same.

### Fixed
- **Library check (roadmap 27)**:
  - a picture that arrived through Google Drive or OneDrive before the notebook that uses it is no longer offered for removal because of its file time (which is the other device's). A file is now offered only if an earlier check on this device, at least a day before, also found it unused (a per-device record in `_meta/library-check-<device>.json`, conflict-safe like the library state).
  - unused files are no longer sent to the system or Obsidian trash (a deletion that syncs to the other devices) but moved inside the vault to `<notebooks folder>/_trash/assets/`; the confirmation says where they go and how to put them back.
  - notebooks that are open now count: their documents, undo and redo history and pending media, and the instant-save journal, are read before judging and again right before moving; if one cannot be inspected the check reports it and offers nothing.
  - backups, version history and trash copies are now accepted only as whole JSON; a copy that is only partly downloaded (cut off, NUL bytes, does not parse) makes the answer unsure instead of hiding the assets it should have named.
  - while a notebook is unreadable or not downloaded the check still lists it but no longer offers any file for removal.
- **Archive (roadmap 54)**: a sync conflict copy of a notebook (same document id) is no longer hidden when the original is archived, and archiving a copy no longer hides the original: copies are keyed by id and path.
- **Selection tools** (review fixes). A rotated picture resized to a non-uniform size no longer comes out hugely distorted (its own axes are stretched, not the page's). *Rotate 90°* no longer turns a graph's frame under its plot or turns ink written on text boxes, equations and graphs: those move their centre and keep their orientation, and their ink moves with them. Ink bound to a graph (`<graph id>~…`) now stays with its graph in align, distribute, mirror and rotate even with *Ink follows objects* off. A change the document refuses (read-only lock, size guard) no longer leaves a selection on copies that are not in the notebook. *Select same colour* works from a selection of text boxes alone, and says so when nothing matches. *Exact size* tells you when the size was limited or text boxes kept their proportions.
- **Paste from another device no longer binds a picture to a junk file.** The picture records of another device's clipboard file are not trusted: only `_assets/<id>.<ext>` images are read (from this vault's own assets folder, never the path written in the file), at most 50 MB, only when the file is complete, and only if its content hashes to the id. A placeholder or half-downloaded picture is skipped with a notice that it is not synced yet.
- **A graph moved or deleted across pages takes its ink with it.** The ink bound to a moved graph (and, with *Ink follows objects*, the ink written on an object) goes to the page its object goes to and floats with the selection while it is dragged; deleting objects across pages offers to delete their ink, like on a single page.
- **A paste bigger than the page** (a cross-page copy, a clip shared from a device with a bigger page) is scaled down uniformly to fit, with a notice, or refused when it would shrink below 5%, instead of landing mostly outside the page.
- **Graph ink in a pasted or duplicated clip** is bound to the copy of its graph, so it stays clipped to it and follows it.
- **Exporting a photo-heavy notebook to PNG or Markdown** frees the decoded pictures of earlier pages (at most 8 pictures and 96 MB are kept between pages).
- **Printing a long range** is capped at 100 pages per job (with a notice suggesting a range) and uses a lower resolution above 10 and 40 pages, so the print preview cannot exhaust a WebView.
- A selection transform that the document refused (content guard, read-only) now drops the floating selection instead of leaving objects that are not in the document.
- **Copy to other devices**: one clipboard store for the whole plugin (two notebooks open at once can no longer lose an entry), entries a newer version wrote no longer make every later copy fail as "too large" (they are dropped last, oldest first, and never otherwise), and another device's clipboard file larger than 8 MB is skipped without being read.

## 0.9.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware, or in Obsidian with its maths engine (the browser harness uses a stand-in for MathJax). The manual steps added in this version are numbered 233 to 274 in `docs/testing.md`; the ones that belong to each area are named below.

### Upgrade notes
- **Document format v9** (0.8 wrote v8). Notebooks saved by 0.9.0 open read-only (with a backup) in 0.8.x and older builds. v8 files are read unchanged and rewritten as v9 only on the first edit. Nothing else changes in how notebooks are saved.

### Added
- **Equations and function graphs** (format v9; steps 233-248).
  - **Equations** (#110): *Insert equation* (All actions → Insert, command) opens an in-place editor with a live preview typeset by Obsidian's maths engine, a palette of structures (fraction, roots, power, subscript, matrix, integral, sum, limit, system, lines aligned on =), display or inline style, automatic numbering "(n)" in reading order, size and colour. A new equation takes the size of the handwriting next to it. The typeset glyphs are stored as a vector outline in the element, so equations are drawn, exported (PDF, SVG) and converted without the maths engine. *Edit* and *Convert to ink* on the contextual bar of a selection; converting turns the glyphs into strokes in one undo step.
  - **Function graphs** (#113): *Insert graph* adds a graph and opens its panel: cartesian, parametric, polar, implicit and piecewise functions as expressions; parameters with sliders that update the curves live; roots, maxima and minima, intersections (marked and labelled), tangent at a point, shaded area with its value, table of values; axes, grid, labels, steps, degrees or radians. Pan by dragging inside the graph and zoom by pinching or with the wheel while the panel is open; ink written on the graph keeps its graph coordinates. Exported as vectors. Expressions are read by a small parser written for the plugin (no `eval`, no access to anything but the graph's own variables and parameters).
- **Obsidian and input** (roadmap 370, 372, 384, 385; steps 249-256).
  - **Notebook commands in the Obsidian mobile toolbar** (roadmap 384). Every command now carries the icon of its action, so it shows with that icon in *Settings > Mobile > toolbar*. Obsidian offers no API for a plugin to suggest toolbar items (the toolbar is configured by the user), so nothing is added automatically; the README lists the commands worth adding.
  - **Bookmark a page in Obsidian's Bookmarks pane** (385). New action and command *Add page to Obsidian bookmarks* (page sheet, All actions, command palette). It uses the core Bookmarks plugin's instance (`app.internalPlugins`, not a published API), detected at run time: if the plugin is off or has another shape you get a notice and nothing happens. The bookmark is a file bookmark with the stable page subpath `#pN&id=...`, so opening it lands on the page even after pages moved; a page already bookmarked is not added twice.
  - **Page turner** (372). Arrow keys, PageUp, PageDown and space (Shift+space goes back) turn pages while a notebook is the active view; Bluetooth presentation clickers send these keys. Nothing happens while a text field or text box is being edited, while a stroke or a drag is in progress, with a dialog or menu open, or with Ctrl/Alt/Meta held. Setting *Turn pages with the keyboard* (on by default). Android's volume keys are handled by the system and never reach web content, so they cannot be used.
  - **Toolbar size** (370). Setting *Toolbar size*: compact (36 px buttons), normal (40 px, unchanged) or large (48 px buttons, 24 px icons). It scales the toolbar, the contextual bar, the option popovers and the radial dial, and the layout engine counts fewer quick slots when buttons are bigger. On a narrow phone the row of tools wraps onto a second line in large. The browser harness checks that nothing overflows at phone width in large and compact.
- **Text and objects** (roadmap 357, 361, 379, 389; steps 257-264).
  - **Layer order** (357): *To front*, *To back*, *Forward* and *Backward* for the selected strokes and elements, in one undo step. *To front* and *To back* are in the contextual bar of a selection; all four are in the All actions sheet and are commands for hotkeys. Strokes can be reordered (a highlighter can go behind pen ink); elements are reordered among elements of the same kind, because the page always draws images, then boxes, then text boxes under the ink (and marker badges above it), so ink never goes under an element and a picture never goes above a text box. *Forward* and *Backward* step past the nearest overlapping item. The selection is released after the move so the new order can be seen.
  - **Find and replace in text boxes** (361): *Find and replace in text boxes* (All actions sheet and command) with *Replace with*, *Match case*, *Whole word*, a list of results with their page (tap to look at the page; the arrow also closes the panel), *Replace* for the chosen result and *Replace all*. The search is literal, not a pattern; handwriting, marker labels and box titles are not searched. *Replace all* is one undo step for the whole notebook.
  - **Page information** (379): *Page information* in the page sheet and the palette: size, paper, background, strokes and points, objects, files and their size, tags, bookmark, markers, and dates. A page has no dates of its own in the file, so the notebook's created and saved dates are shown, plus when this device last edited the page when it knows.
  - **A page in the middle of a notebook** (389, extends 95): with *Add a page when writing at the bottom* on, writing in the bottom tenth of a page that is not the last shows a chip *Add a page after this one*. One tap inserts a page with the same size and paper right after it; nothing is ever added by itself in the middle. The chip goes away when the pen goes down, after a few seconds, or when you change page, and does not come back for that page. The last page still appends automatically.
- **Export and view** (roadmap 367, 376, 381, 382; steps 265-274).
  - **Export choices** (roadmap 381; steps 265-267). New *Export…* in the page sheet, the *All actions* sheet, the notebook menu and the command palette: choose PDF or SVG, one page or the whole notebook, and what is drawn: **ink and background**, **ink only** (writing, text boxes, pictures, boxes and markers, with no paper or PDF behind them; an SVG is transparent, a PDF page stays white) or **background only** (the paper pattern and the PDF or picture, no writing). A whole notebook as SVG writes one file per page (`Name p1.svg`…); file names say the choice (`Name ink.pdf`, `Name p3 background.svg`) and an existing file is never replaced. Invisible ink still follows the old rule: it is exported only where *Show solutions* is on. What an export leaves out is not loaded either, so an ink-only export never rasterises the PDF. *Export notebook as PDF* and the page's PDF and SVG entries are unchanged.
  - **Export as Markdown with page images** (roadmap 382; steps 268-270). New command and menu entry *Export as Markdown with page images…*: choose a vault folder (created when missing) and a resolution (100, 150 or 200 dpi); it writes `page-001.png`… (each page drawn like the PDF export: sheet, PDF or picture background, paper, pictures, boxes, text, ink, markers; no night-mode colours; invisible ink only where the solutions are shown) and `notebook.md`: the title, each page as `![](page-001.png)`, the text of its text boxes below it in reading order, and a heading for every bookmarked page (its title, or *Page n*). Nothing is overwritten: a name that exists gets " 2", " 3"… and `notebook.md` is written last, so it only exists when every image does. Very large pages are drawn smaller (at most 8192 px a side and 16 million pixels).
  - **Night mode** (roadmap 367; step 271). *Night mode* in the *All actions* sheet and the command palette (a view setting, kept with the plugin settings, on for every notebook): a dark page with light ink. It is applied when drawing only: near-black ink becomes light grey, other colours keep their hue and are lightened until they read on the dark page (contrast at least 4.5:1), the highlighter is drawn lively with a "screen" blend (also while it is being written), paper lines are dimmed, text boxes follow the ink colours, and PDF and picture backgrounds are dimmed with a dark veil (not inverted). Stored colours, exports, the thumbnails in the page panel and the library, and the companion notes are not affected.
  - **Crop PDF margins** (roadmap 376; steps 272-274). *Crop PDF margins* on the PDF contextual bar, in the *All actions* sheet and as a command: a view setting for small screens. Each PDF page is measured once at a very low resolution (about 100 px wide, in the background, nearest pages first; the result is kept for the session only) to find where its non-white content is; the view then fits that box to the screen width instead of the whole page, so the white side margins fall off screen. Ink and text written in a margin widen the box, so nothing becomes unreachable. In *continuous* mode the zoom follows the union of the boxes of all pages, and only when every page of the notebook is a measured PDF page (a plain page keeps the whole width); *page by page* mode fits each page on its own. Only the zoom and the pan limits change: the document, ink coordinates, the renderer and the exports are untouched. Top and bottom margins are not cropped.

### Changed
- **With the other wave 0.9 features**: equations and graphs are content for *Export…* (drawn with *ink only*, left out of *background only*), are mapped by night mode (on screen only), keep their own band in the layer order (graphs above images, equations above boxes), can carry ink like text boxes and images, are drawn in the Markdown page images, and equations are written in `notebook.md` as `$$…$$` (or `$…$` inline, `\tag{n}` when numbered). *Page information* counts them.

### Fixed
- **Saves can no longer be stopped by an edit**: the graph panel puts ranges and areas in order (or removes them) and its draft is checked strictly before it is kept; every edit in the notebook is now checked like the writer checks before saving (`checkContent`), and an edit that would be refused is undone with a notice.
- Scaling an equation or a text box stops at the largest size the format accepts (400 pt); a scaled equation outline that would be too long is dropped and typeset again; a graph frame stops at 4,000 pt (a larger one in a file is clamped and counted).
- Graphs have a work limit (250,000 evaluations for the curves and 150,000 for roots, extrema, intersections, tangents and areas; at most 1,024 initial samples and a 400 × 400 implicit grid): twelve functions such as `sin(99999x)` take a fraction of a second instead of about 20 s, the graph shows "≈" and the panel says it is simplified; typing in the panel redraws once the typing pauses.
- Ink on a graph stays the graph's ink after a pan or zoom: the moved strokes are bound to the graph by their id (no format change) and drawn clipped to its frame (also in PDF, SVG and the Markdown page images), so ink carried outside by a zoom comes back with the next zoom; moves in one session start from the original points, so rounding does not pile up.
- Markdown export escapes the syntax of Markdown and Obsidian in text boxes (links and embeds, tags, maths, comments, highlights, HTML, tables, block ids, code).
- Equations refuse `\href`, `\url`, `\style`, `\class`, `\cssId`, `\html`, `\data`, `\require`, `\unicode` and `\mmlToken` before they reach MathJax.
- The session cache of typeset equations is least-recently-used (2,000 entries) instead of being cleared at 500, so a large export keeps every equation it typeset.
- *Convert to ink* checks that each equation is still on the page after typesetting and computes the stroke positions right before the change.
- *Add page to Obsidian bookmarks* gives the bookmark a creation time like the Bookmarks pane's own entries and only uses the core plugin when it reports itself enabled.

### Development
- `src/math/` (pure): expression parser, adaptive sampler with discontinuity handling and clipping, marching squares, numerical roots, extrema, intersections, derivatives and integrals, the graph scene shared by every renderer, outline tracing, equation layout and *Convert to ink*. Tests: `tests/model-v9.test.ts`, `tests/math-expr.test.ts`, `tests/math-graph.test.ts`.
- The harness stub provides `loadMathJax`, `renderMath` and `finishRenderMath`.
- Final run of 0.9.0 (development PC): `npm test` 2263 tests, `npm run harness:check` 31 scenarios, lint 0 errors, build ok.

## 0.8.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware. The manual steps added in this version are numbered 178 to 232 in `docs/testing.md`; the ones that belong to each area are named below.

### Upgrade notes
- **Document format v8** (0.7 wrote v7). A v7 file is migrated when it is opened and written as v8 by the next save. **Files written by 0.8.0 open read-only in 0.7.x and older** (no data is rewritten there).
- **Per-device sidecar files in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/`.** The Inbox state, the practice links, the textbook settings and the record of moved pages are written one file per device (`inbox-<device>.json`, `practice-<device>.json`, `textbooks-<device>.json`, `page-moves-<device>.json`) and merged when read, so two synced devices never write the same file. The files the earlier builds shared (`practice.json`, `textbooks.json`) are still read and are never written or deleted, so no record is lost.
- **Homework task lines** in companion notes now end with a block id, `^nb-<marker id>`, which ties the task to its marker. Page links the plugin writes now carry the page id (`#p3&id=<page id>`); companion notes are rewritten once with the new links.
- Plugin API 1.1.0 (`getPageLink(file, page | pageId)` and `openNotebook(file, page | pageId)`).

### Added
- **Markers, structured boxes and invisible ink** (format v8; steps 178-187).
  - **Markers** on the page: *To complete* (#201, records the time and the slide in view: the PDF beside the notes or the page's own PDF page), *Question* (#202, resolved or open), *Important* !, *Very important* !!, *Key point* ★ (#203) and *Homework* with what to do and a due date (#209). Place them from the lecture contextual bar (during a class), the *All actions* sheet (Lecture), the radial dial (shapes ring → *Markers*) or the commands: the next tap places a badge at that point, a drag places it about a region. Badges are drawn above the ink, sharp at any zoom. A tap on a badge resolves or reopens a question or gap, cycles ! → !! → ★, and opens the dialog of a homework marker; the lasso moves and deletes them like other objects. Undo works for every change.
  - **Structured boxes** (#204): *Draw a structured box*, drag a frame around ink and choose Definition, Theorem, Example, Proof or Remark; a tap on its label changes the kind or adds a title. Boxes and markers annotate a region and do not own ink: moving or deleting one leaves the ink under it alone.
  - **Companion note**: boxes become callouts (`> [!theorem] Title`) with a live embed of the boxed region, homework becomes Obsidian tasks (`- [ ] … 📅 YYYY-MM-DD ^nb-<id>`, `- [x]` once done), inside the generated region only (your own text is never touched). A task ticked in the note (by hand or by the Tasks plugin, with its done date) stays ticked when the note is regenerated, and the marker is marked done in the notebook as one undo step when the notebook is open and editable on the device that updates the note (otherwise the tick waits in the note); a tick the plugin wrote follows the notebook, so a task reopened there is unticked again.
  - **End of lecture panel** (#223; lecture bar, sheets, page sheet, command): open questions, gaps, homework, importance markers and boxes of the current lecture (from its *New lecture* header) or of the whole notebook; tap a row to go there, tick to resolve, *Edit* to change it.
  - **Invisible ink** (#100): a pen mode (*Toggle invisible ink*) whose strokes are saved hidden; they show while the mode is on or with *Show solutions* (notebook or current page, view only, never saved). Hidden ink is not erased, selected, scribbled out or deleted by pen gestures while hidden, and is left out of PDF and SVG exports unless the solutions are shown; thumbnails and embeds never show it. *Insert space* still moves hidden ink with the rest of the page so that solutions stay next to their exercise.
  - **Format v8** (`docs/architecture.md`): new element types `marker` and `box` and the stroke flag `hidden`, with a tested v7 → v8 migration. Invalid optional details (including a marker label or box title that reading has to normalise) are dropped and counted on read, with a notice and a backup before the next save, never written. Duplicating, restoring, trashing and restoring, moving, copying and pasting pages and selections, templates, repair and the conflicted-copy merge keep the new data (a marker resolved on one device only is resolved after the merge, not duplicated).
  - All these actions are entries of the action registry (`src/ui/actions.ts`); the command ids are `marker-*`, `end-of-lecture`, `invisible-ink`, `show-solutions`, `show-page-solutions`. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Pen gestures** (roadmap 139; steps 188-199). Draw a small shape with the pen and the notebook does something, without ever touching normal writing.
  - **Gestures never start while you write.** They start only in *gesture mode* (toggle *Gesture mode* in a quick slot or the All actions sheet, or the command *Toggle pen gesture mode*; a badge shows while it is on), while the S Pen side button is held when *Pen side button* is set to the new *Pen gestures while held* (or *Radial menu on click, pen gestures while held*), or, if you turn on *Long press starts a gesture* (off by default), when the pen rests on the page for about 0.65 s before the stroke moves (the dot written so far is dropped; this turns off the hold-still radial menu). A finger and the eraser never start a gesture. The default side button action is unchanged.
  - **Preview before you lift the pen:** the recognised command is named in a label beside the nib and the device vibrates briefly when it changes; lifting runs it. Move the pen back along the line to where it started and lift: the gesture is cancelled and nothing happens. A stroke that matches nothing shows *Not recognised* and does nothing.
  - **Context-aware:** a circle around ink selects it (a circle with nothing inside is nothing); a scribble over ink deletes it (the existing scribble detector; nothing under it, nothing happens); "^" inserts space at its tip (the same engine as the Space tool, amount in settings, default 48 pt); a "#" drawn in one stroke opens the page tags; a five-pointed star drawn in one stroke bookmarks the page (again: removes it); a "?" opens the search; a line (an arrow head is optional) from a selection to a thumbnail in the page panel moves the selection to that page (kept in place, shifted if the page is smaller).
  - **Custom gestures:** *Settings, Pen gestures, Custom gestures* (or the command *Custom pen gestures*): choose a name and any Obsidian command, draw the gesture three times on a pad. The three drawings must look alike and must not look like a built-in gesture. Matching is a small template matcher in the style of the $1 recogniser (original code; see `docs/research.md`), tolerant of size, position and a few degrees of tilt, not of rotation or direction; two custom gestures too alike are never guessed between. Stored in the plugin settings (not in the notebooks).
  - **Cheat sheet** (settings, the badge, the command *Show the pen gesture cheat sheet*) lists every gesture with its drawing; each built-in gesture can be switched off there or in the settings.
  - **Gesture ink is never saved.** For gestures that change the page (delete, space, bookmark, move) the history records the drawn line as ink first and the effect after it, so one Undo restores the page and gives the line back as ordinary ink, and a second Undo removes it. Gestures that only open something (select, tags, search, a custom command) leave no undo step.
  - No change to the file format or to the writing pipeline. Thresholds were tuned on synthetic strokes only. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Page operations on several pages, stable page links, quiet while writing** (roadmap 378, 388, 374; steps 200-206; no change to the notebook format).
  - **Several pages at once** (378): the page panel gets a select button (and a long press on a thumbnail, Ctrl/Shift+click on a PC), ticks on the thumbnails, *Select all pages shown* and *Select a range*. *Actions* offers move to the start, the end or a position, move to another notebook (the existing dialog, pages ticked), duplicate, bookmark / remove bookmarks, add tags / remove a tag, change paper, change size (each page keeps its orientation), export the pages as one PDF, and delete through the page trash (a copy of every page is on disk first; the last page is never deleted; a page edited or replaced while the copies were being written gets a new copy before it is deleted, and if the pages keep changing nothing is deleted and a notice says so). Each action is one undo step (`src/model/page-ops.ts`, `src/view/page-ops-menu.ts`).
  - **Quiet while writing** (374): while a pen is on the screen and for 1.5 s after it is lifted, the plugin's own notices wait and are shown at the first pause (a notice repeated meanwhile is shown once), and non-urgent background work waits too: page-panel and library thumbnails, the library re-index after a save, version-history snapshots, the timetable offer. Errors are never held back: failed saves, backups of unreadable or conflicting files, integrity problems and a failed save before a version restore are shown at once. Obsidian's own notices and those of other plugins cannot be held back. Only pens count (`pointerType` `pen`); notices from the toolbar, stamps, selection actions and Zettelkasten still use `new Notice` directly.
  - **Stable page links** (388): the links the plugin writes (copy link, insert link and embed, page suggestions after `[[Notebook#`, `obsidian://notebook` links, companion note page links, PDF annotation note links, the API) name the page by id, `#p3&id=<page id>` (`p3` stays for readability and for readers that only know numbers). Following a link or an embed resolves the id first, so a reordered notebook still opens the right page; a deleted page says so. *Move to notebook…* keeps each page's id, so the links follow the page; only a page whose id the target already has gets a new one, and that old → new pair is recorded in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/page-moves-<device>.json`.
  - **Update links to notebook pages…** (command): lists, without writing anything, the old `#pN` links it would pin to a page id, the links whose page number it would fix, and the links whose page is now in exactly one other notebook; you untick what you want to leave and apply. Wikilinks and Markdown links are handled (`(`, `)`, `#` and `?` are encoded in a relocated Markdown link, and the text replaced is taken exactly as written); links inside fenced code (also in blockquotes, callouts and lists), `%% %%` comments and the frontmatter are left alone, a link is never moved to a sync conflicted copy (`Notes (1).notebook`), and the list shows the text of the line each link is on. Everything it cannot decide (several notebooks with that name, page number out of range, page gone, id in several notebooks, unreadable notebook) is listed and left alone.
- **Zettelkasten inbox** (roadmap Z4; steps 207-211; no change to the document format).
  - Commands **Open the inbox** and **Process the next inbox item**. The Inbox lists what is still to process: the blocks of writing on the quick-notes sheet (separate jots on a page are separate items) and the highlights and comments on PDF pages (candidates for literature notes, with the PDF and page as their source). Select an item to see its ink and text, then make a **permanent** or **literature card** (the card dialog starts with a title taken from the text, the type, and the PDF source; the typed text or the highlighted PDF words become the first content of the *Transcription* section), **link it to an existing card** (search the cards), **discard** it or **skip** it; the next open item is then selected. **Nothing is deleted**: discarding or linking only marks the item as processed, and *Show processed* lets you reopen it. A block that gets new writing after it was processed comes back.
  - The processed state is stored in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/inbox-<device>.json`, one small file per device, written only by those actions and merged when read (the latest decision wins). An unreadable file is never overwritten (one copy is kept); an existing empty file is treated as not ready, the change is refused with a notice, and the file is not written over.
  - `registerInboxSource` is the interface the marker elements will plug into; they are not listed yet. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Quick textbook reference** (roadmap 208; steps 212-214; no change to the document format).
  - **Set textbook for this notebook…** (command) chooses a textbook PDF of the vault for this notebook, for the notebooks of its folder (a course) or for all notebooks, with an optional page offset (for books whose printed page 1 is not PDF page 1) and name; **Remove a textbook…** undoes it. Stored in `<notebooks folder>/_meta/textbooks-<device>.json`, one file per device, merged when read (for a textbook the latest setting or removal wins); written only by those commands.
  - Typed text such as "p. 134", "pag. 134", "p134", "pp. 134-136" or "page 7" in a text box becomes tappable: a thin underline marks it and a tap opens the textbook at that page, in the PDF pane beside the notes, or in Obsidian's PDF viewer when the pane is showing other slides (long press or right click offers both and the other textbooks). The text itself is unchanged. The area is hidden while the text tool is active.
  - **Insert textbook reference** (command) types "p. N" at the last pen position seen in the view, as one undo step. **Open textbook at a page…** opens the textbook at a typed page. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Redo the exercise** (roadmap 303; steps 215-224; no change to the document format).
