  - **Redo this exercise…** (page sheet and command): choose what to hide in a practice copy of the page: all your ink, only some colours or tools (pen, highlighter, shapes), or only the lasso selection. PDF and image backgrounds, text boxes and pictures stay. The copy goes right after the page, or to the end of an *Exercises* notebook (created in the notebooks folder when it does not exist; the name and the choice are remembered on this device), as one undo step in the notebook it goes to. The dialog says how many strokes will be hidden before anything is made. The copy leaves gap, question and homework markers out (they would add a second homework task and count again as open items); importance markers and boxes are copied. A plain *Duplicate* still copies every marker with new ids, on purpose.
  - **Linked to the original without a format change**: the copy is a normal page tagged `practice` and `attempt/<n>` (the tags travel with the page), and `<notebooks folder>/_meta/practice-<device>.json` keeps the link (practice page id and original page id, notebook document ids with a path hint, attempt number, date, what was hidden, time spent). It is written only by *Redo this exercise* and the timer's *Stop*, one file per device, every device's files merged on read (attempt times add up across devices), keeps one line per attempt in a fixed order, and never overwrites a file it cannot read.
  - **Original, Attempts, numbering**: a strip over a practice page has *Original* (goes to the original, in this or another notebook), *Attempts* (the original and its attempts, numbered, dated, with the time spent) and the commands *Go to the original exercise* and *Attempts of this exercise*. Redoing a practice page starts again from the original sheet, so each attempt starts from the same page.
  - **Peek at the solution**: hold *Peek* in the strip (or use the command, which switches it on and off) to see the original's hidden ink over the practice page; let go and it disappears. It is only drawn on screen, never saved or exported.
  - **Timer per attempt** (optional): *Start* and *Stop* in the strip add the time to the attempt's record; a running timer stops and is saved when the notebook view closes.
  - Not done: variants of the exercise with different numbers (they need the variables and drawings with states of #182 and #241, which do not exist yet). Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Links written with the pen** (roadmap O8; steps 225-232; no format change: a link is ordinary text in a text box).
  - **Links in text boxes**: `[[Note]]`, `[[Note|alias]]` and `[[Note#Heading]]` typed in a text box are drawn as links (link colour, underlined; the brackets stay visible so the text, its wrapping and the editor always agree; embeds `![[…]]` are left alone). A finger tap on a link (or Ctrl/Cmd+click with a mouse) opens the note in a new tab; the text tool still edits the box. Links to notes that do not exist are drawn dimmed and dashed, and tapping one offers *Create note* (Obsidian's new-note location; a name with folders is taken from the vault root).
  - **`[[` suggester in the text editor**: typing `[[` opens a list of the vault's notes (and of the notebooks), ranked by name; after `#` it lists the headings of the note. Arrow keys and Enter/Tab choose, Escape closes it, a tap chooses; the typed text becomes `[[note]]`.
  - **Ink links**: with the lasso, circle some ink and tap *Link to a note…* in the selection bar (also the command *Link selection to a note…*): a chooser lists notes (or links to a name that does not exist yet). The note becomes a small text element `[[Note]]` placed above the ink, with a link badge; tapping the label or the ink under it (with a finger, or Ctrl/Cmd+click) opens the note. The ink is not changed, and with *Ink follows objects* it moves with the label. The selection bar also has *Open link* for a selected text box with a link.
  - **Handwritten `[[`**: two bracket-shaped strokes side by side (`src/ink/bracket-detect.ts`: a spine with a hook at each end, both on the same side; not `l1`, `||`, parentheses, `[]` or letters) make a *Link to a note…* chip appear next to the ink, also while the name is being written to the right of the brackets on the same line. Nothing is converted unless you tap the chip; the chip goes away when you write elsewhere, scroll, undo or after 10 seconds.
  - **Companion notes** already copy the text of text boxes, so links in text boxes and ink links appear in the companion note and in Obsidian's graph and backlinks (the empty lines that stretch an ink link's box are not copied). Not yet verified on Android hardware.

### Changed
- The Inbox, practice, textbook and page-move records are stored one file per device and merged on read (see Upgrade notes).
- Links the plugin writes name the page by id (`#p3&id=…`); the number stays for readability.
- Plugin notices and non-urgent background work wait while a pen is on the screen (see *Quiet while writing*).
- The side button setting *Pen side button* has two new choices (*Pen gestures while held*, *Radial menu on click, pen gestures while held*); the default is unchanged.

### Fixed
- **Hidden ink is safe from scribble erase and pen gestures.** Scratching out visible writing (scribble erase or the delete gesture) no longer deletes the invisible-ink solutions under it, and hidden ink no longer counts for the delete or circle-select gestures, unless the solutions of that page are shown.
- **Deleting a box or a marker no longer offers to delete the ink it frames.** The offer after deleting a text box or an image never counts or deletes hidden ink while the solutions are not shown.
- **Marker labels changed on read are reported.** A marker label or box title that reading has to normalise (extra spaces, line breaks, too long, blank) now counts as a dropped detail, so the notice about repaired details appears instead of the label changing silently.
- **Error notices are never held back while writing.** Failed saves, backups of unreadable or conflicting files, integrity problems and a failed save before a version restore are shown at once instead of waiting until the pen is lifted.
- **Update links: Markdown links to notebooks with parentheses, conflicted copies, fewer false links.** A relocated Markdown link now encodes `(`, `)`, `#` and `?` (a link to `Physics (1).notebook` no longer breaks), the text it replaces is taken exactly as written in the note, a link is never moved to a sync conflicted copy, links inside fenced code in blockquotes, callouts and lists, inside `%% %%` comments and in the frontmatter are left alone, and the list shows the text of the line each link is on.
- **Moved pages keep their ids.** *Move to notebook…* no longer gives every moved page a new id, so stable links can follow the page with *Update links to notebook pages*.
- **An empty sidecar file is never read as "no data".** An existing empty Inbox, practice or textbooks file (a sync app that has not finished downloading it, or a write cut short) is now treated as not ready: the change is refused with a notice saying so, a copy is kept, and the file is not written over.
- **Practice links and textbooks: one file per device.** `practice.json` and `textbooks.json` were shared by all devices, so the last device to write won and sync conflict copies were never read. Each device now writes only its own files and every device's files are merged on read; the old shared files are still read, never written or deleted.
- **Deleting several pages never deletes a version without a copy** (see *Several pages at once*).
- **Practice copies no longer repeat the open items of the original** (gap, question and homework markers are left out of the copy).
- **Homework ticked in the companion note stays ticked** (see *Companion note* under markers).

### Development
- New modules: `src/ink/gestures/` (pen gesture recognisers, pure code), `src/ink/bracket-detect.ts`, `src/model/page-ops.ts`, `src/view/page-ops-menu.ts`; marker, box and hidden-ink code in `model/` and `ink/` with tests, and a tested v7 → v8 migration.
- The browser harness has a scenario for each new feature; `npm run harness:check` runs 31 scenarios.
- Final run of 0.8.0 on the development PC: `npm test` 2110 tests passed, `npm run harness:check` 31 scenarios ok, lint 0 errors, build ok (see `docs/testing.md`).

## 0.7.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware. The manual steps added in this version are numbered 135 to 177 in `docs/testing.md`; the ones that belong to each area are named below.

### Upgrade notes
- **New interface.** The long "…" menu and the page menu are replaced by the **All actions** sheet and the **page sheet**. Command ids are unchanged, so hotkeys keep working.
- **Library index version 4** (0.6 used version 3). The index is a cache in the plugin folder: it is rebuilt once on the first start of 0.7 (it now also keeps bookmark titles). The index of a newer plugin version is ignored and left as it is instead of being overwritten.
- **Presence files** (open on another device) are written to `<notebooks folder>/_meta/presence/`, one file per notebook and device; each device writes only its own files.
- **Per-device local data** is kept in Obsidian's local storage and never synced: the use counts behind the toolbar's quick actions and the *Recent* section, the log of recently edited pages, the safe-mode "opening" marker, the integrity ledger of written versions, and the device id and name.
- No change to the document format (still v7), the writing engine or saving.

### Added
- **New interface** (`docs/design-ui.md`; steps 144-155). Every action of the plugin is described once in an action registry (`src/ui/actions.ts`) and the interface is built from it and from the context:
  - **Toolbar**: tools in the first row; undo/redo, the current pen (opens the tool options), the favourite pens, up to six **quick actions** (ranked by priority and by your own use on this device), the page indicator and the save state in the second; one row on wide views.
  - **Contextual bar** at the bottom of the page: the selection's actions (copy, cut, duplicate, paste, white → transparent, save as stamp, delete, colour and width), paste for the lasso, the PDF page's actions (search, hide annotations, dim, margins, side by side), the side-by-side pane's actions, and the lecture actions while a class of the timetable is on. It can be collapsed.
  - **All actions** sheet (⊞: grouped sections with search and a *Recent* section) and the **page sheet** (tap the page indicator or a thumbnail's menu) with paper and size as chips. On a phone they are bottom sheets. The page sheet starts with a **Go to** section: *Go to page number…*, *Recently edited pages*, *One page at a time*.
  - **Radial dial** at the pen tip, redesigned: undo/redo, a width bubble, a colour bubble, the active tool drawn as a pen, and favourites · pen case · shapes. The colour ring has 12 colours, a custom colour and an eyedropper; the width bubble opens an arc slider; the pen case shows the tools with a stroke preview and "+" to save a favourite.
  - The status banners (safe mode, storage full, integrity, open on another device) and the heavy-page bar are part of the registry too, with their buttons.
  - New command *Tool: space*.
  - A visual system built on Obsidian's CSS variables (surfaces, elevation, radii, spacing, motion with *reduced motion* respected) applied to bars, popovers, sheets, panels, dialogs and the library.
  - Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Sync safety** (roadmap 342-345; steps 170-177). For vaults synced with Google Drive or OneDrive through a folder-sync app.
  - **Open on another device** (342): while a notebook is open, a small file `<notebooks folder>/_meta/presence/<notebook id>.<device id>.json` is refreshed every 5 minutes while the app is in the foreground (on resume only when it is that old) and removed on close. Opening a notebook that another device has open (seen in the last 15 minutes) shows a banner with **Open read-only** or **Open anyway**; it never blocks. Each device writes only its own files, so they cannot cause conflicted copies. Device id and name are stored on the device only; the name can be changed in *Settings, Device name*.
  - **Assets waiting for sync** (343): an image or PDF referenced by a page but not (completely) downloaded yet shows a "Waiting for sync" placeholder instead of an error, the page stays editable, and the file appears by itself when it arrives.
  - **Online-only notebooks** (344): a 0-byte (or NUL-filled) notebook file shows **Not downloaded yet** in the library and on open; it is never treated as damaged, backed up as unreadable or offered for repair, and opens normally when its content arrives.
  - **Check sync safety** (345): command and settings row, plus a notice at startup when new problems are found and on opening a notebook: notebooks folder inside `.obsidian` or a hidden or `.nosync` folder, names OneDrive, Windows or Android storage refuse, long paths, names differing only in case, files of 10 MB / 100 MB or more, and whether files from other devices ever arrive. What cannot be detected (the sync app's own exclusions and limits) is said in the dialog and in `docs/architecture.md`.
  - Not yet verified in Obsidian or on Android hardware.
- **Resilience** (steps 135-143; no change to the document format)
  - **Safe mode** (#346): if opening a notebook crashed or froze last time, the next open uses safe mode: no low-latency ink or predicted ink, no page thumbnails, no companion note, no PDF text read ahead, and PDF and image backgrounds appear only after the ink is painted (they are skipped if the last safe open died while loading them; *Show images and PDFs* brings them back). A banner says so and offers **Open normally** and **Repair…** (the integrity report of that notebook, with *Restore a version…* and, for an unreadable file, the repair); after two failures in a row the repair is highlighted. The "opening" marker is set right before the file is read and cleared one second after the first paint; it is kept per device in Obsidian's local storage, never synced. Closing the tab or switching apps while opening is not counted. Logic in `src/storage/safe-mode.ts`.
  - **Storage full** (#347): a failing save is classified (storage full, read-only or no permission, partial write, other) and shown in a persistent **Not saved** banner with **Retry now**; saving retries by itself with a growing delay (5 s doubling up to 1 minute), and the regular timed saves wait for it instead of hammering a full disk. The document stays in memory and in the instant save journal. Every write (up to 2 million characters, every write while saving is failing) is read back; a partial file is replaced by the previous content at once, and after a full disk the file is touched again only once a space check in the plugin's folder succeeds, so a full disk never leaves a half-written notebook behind when the old one can be put back. Logic in `src/storage/write-guard.ts`; hooks in `DocumentSession`.
  - **Integrity check with checksums** (#390, extends #27): each device remembers (in its local storage) fingerprints of the last five versions it wrote of each notebook. When a notebook is opened or reloaded, a file that is exactly an older version this device wrote (a sync service put back a stale copy), or whose pages changed while its edit time did not, shows an **Unexpected change on disk** banner with **Restore a version…** (version history), **Keep this version**, and, when it replaced the version that was open, **Restore the newer version** (merged back as one undo step). Normal edits synced from another device, metadata changes from the library and version-history restores are never reported. When a reload replaces the open version with such a file or with an unreadable one, the open version is first kept as an `integrity` backup. Nothing is restored automatically. New command **Check notebooks integrity**: reads every notebook (parse, save check, the device's ledger, asset files present and of the recorded size) and lists the problems with *Open*, *Repair…*, *Restore a version…* and *Keep this version*. Logic in `src/storage/integrity.ts`; dialog and banners in `src/view/resilience.ts`.
- **Performance** (steps 156-161; no change to the document format)
  - **Level of detail for zoomed-out strokes** (roadmap 387). When the page is shown below 50 % zoom, and in thumbnails, committed strokes are filled from a simplified shape whose difference from the exact one is at most 0.5 device pixels (Douglas–Peucker; the tolerance steps 0.25, 0.5, 1 and 2 pt are chosen from the zoom and the screen's pixel ratio), cached per stroke and level; at 50 % and above, and on screens dense enough that nothing would be gained, the exact geometry is drawn as before. Strokes written with perfect-freehand (render 1) have their outline flattened and simplified into a polygon; wet-ink strokes (render 2) keep fewer nodes of the same construction. Nothing is saved differently, nothing about writing (wet ink, input, how strokes look at normal zoom) changed. Measured on a PC with a 10,000-stroke notebook scrolled through at 49, 35 and 25 % zoom (`npm run bench:lod`): redraw per frame 52 → 23, 64 → 27 and 81 → 29 ms for render-1 strokes and 61 → 37, 72 → 45 and 91 → 52 ms for render-2 strokes (1x screen); not measured on Android.
  - **Heavy page warning** (roadmap 380). A page with more than 3,000 strokes, 120,000 points or 20 MB of images shows a discreet bar at the bottom of the view (never while writing) with **Split page** and **Dismiss**. Split page moves the lower half of the content (balanced by points, cut in the widest blank gap near the middle) to a new page right after it, with the size and paper of the page: strokes and text boxes keep their horizontal positions and their distances to each other and are moved up so the content starts near the top of the new page; it is one undo step. Pages that show a PDF or a scan only offer Dismiss (their ink annotates the background). Dismiss lasts until the notebook view is closed. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Quick navigation** (roadmap 364, 365, 366; steps 162-169). No document format change.
  - **Go to page…** (command): a quick switcher over every notebook. Type part of a notebook name, a page number (`12` or `p12`, alone or after the name: `physics 12`), a bookmark title, a page tag or words from a text box; results are fuzzy, show a small page thumbnail (drawn lazily, one at a time, cached) and open the notebook at that page. With nothing typed it lists the pages edited last. It uses the library index (a cached copy, brought up to date by reading only changed notebooks); the index now also keeps bookmark titles, so it is rebuilt once after the update.
  - **Recently edited pages** (command, and *Recent pages* in the library sidebar): a list ordered by last edit. It comes from a small per-device log of (notebook id, page id, time), written a few seconds after real edits only (not by scrolling or opening), capped at 100 pages, stored in Obsidian's device-local storage: it is not a vault file, does not sync and is not in the notebook. The page is found by its id when you open it, so it survives reordering; a deleted page shows a notice. The page logged is the one in view when the edit was made.
  - **Go to page number…** (command, and tapping the page indicator in the toolbar, which opens the page sheet with *Go to* first): type `12`, `p12` or the start of a bookmark title.
  - Not yet verified on Android hardware.

### Changed
- The long "…" menu and the page menu are replaced by the *All actions* sheet and the page sheet; the pen side button's radial menu is the redesigned radial dial; PDF search, hide annotations, dim and margins moved to the contextual bar and the sheets.
- Commands are generated from the action registry; their ids are unchanged. Use counts are stored per device in Obsidian's local storage, never in the vault.

### Fixed
- Sync safety: an empty notebook file is no longer always "not downloaded": with evidence of damage (this device wrote the file, journal entries, our own cut-off write) the open document is kept in memory with *Write it back* and *Recover…*, the version that was open is always backed up first, and Repair is allowed after a confirmation.
- Sync safety: a file that is cut off, NUL-padded or changed seconds ago is "still arriving" instead of broken: at most one backup per content (size-capped), polling goes on, a dirty save waits instead of writing over the download, and Repair warns when the file changed in the last few minutes.
- Save read-back: only our own partial write (empty, NUL-padded or a prefix of what was written) is repaired, so a sync client writing in place is never clobbered; when another valid version replaced ours, our text is kept as a `conflict` backup before that version is loaded.
- Integrity: a version-history restore is written with a new edit time, so other devices no longer report it as a sync rollback; *Restore the newer version* is no longer the primary action for a rolled-back file.
- Presence files are refreshed every 5 minutes (and on resume only when that old) instead of every minute, which cut the uploads per open notebook.
- After a long failing save streak, a newer version saved elsewhere is backed up and offered for merge (one undo step) instead of being overwritten with the older local edits.
- Writes above 2 million characters are now read back and compared by length, start and end instead of not being verified.
- *Check notebooks integrity* reports an empty notebook as not downloaded (Repair asks first) and an empty or smaller asset file as still arriving, not as damage.
- An image that fails to decode (usually still being written by the sync app) is loaded again when its file changes.
- The quick switcher saves the notebooks it read into the library index (no re-reading every session), and neither it nor the library saves over an index written by a newer plugin version.
- Save read-back: a write cut inside a multi-byte character (read back ending in U+FFFD) is recognised as our own partial write and repaired.
- Save read-back: a read-back equal to the previous text is a failed write (retried, journal and document kept) instead of "another writer"; another version of the notebook is backed up and offered for merge while the local document stays in memory, and the journal is deleted only once our content is on disk.
- Sync safety: "may be damaged" now needs strong evidence (the replaced text is this device's write of the last 2 minutes, journal entries or our own torn write); *Write it back* is the primary action only then, its confirmation warns that a newer version may be downloading, the current file content is backed up first and a version that arrived meanwhile is not written over.
- Sync safety: a cut-off file is backed up once per session, after it stops changing, instead of once per partial state during a slow download.
- Sync safety: while an open notebook waits for its file, the last readable text stays the journal base (edits survive a crash) and a "recently changed" verdict is reviewed as well.
- Sync safety: a complete notebook followed by NUL bytes opens normally, and an open document stays in memory when its backup fails.
- Sync safety: a save with pending edits no longer writes over an empty file at once; it waits as for a cut-off file (*Write it back* is offered).

### Development
- The browser harness has checks and screenshots for the redesigned interface; `npm run harness:check` runs 29 scenarios.
- Final run of 0.7.0 on the development PC: `npm test` 1819 tests passed, `npm run harness:check` 29 scenarios ok, lint 0 errors, build ok (see `docs/testing.md`).

## 0.6.0 — 2026-10-30

Not yet verified on Android hardware. The manual steps are numbered 64 to 134 in `docs/testing.md`; the ones that belong to each area are named below.

### Upgrade notes
- **Format v7.** Notebooks saved by 0.6.0 may use format v7. 0.5.x opens notebooks saved in format v7 read-only, and each time it opens one it writes a `future-version` backup to the backup folder: update every device to 0.6.
- On a 0.6 device, any edit of a notebook's properties from the library (tags, favourite, cover, bookmarks) rewrites a v6 file as v7 (with a backup of the original), after which 0.5.x devices can only read it.
- The library index (a cache in the plugin folder) is version 3 in 0.6 and 2 in 0.5.x: the index is rebuilt once on the first start of 0.6 (to pick up page tags), and a 0.5.x device discards a version 3 index and rebuilds its own, so with both versions on one synced plugin folder the index is rebuilt after each switch. No notebook data is affected. The library index of a newer plugin version is ignored and left as it is instead of being overwritten.
- Companion notes written by 0.5.0 keep their old tag behaviour until their next update (see *Changed*).
- PDF links: the links file moved from the plugin's folder to `<notebooks folder>/_meta/pdf-links.json`; the old file is copied over once and left untouched.
- Page trash: deleted pages are kept as one file each under `_trash/pages/<notebook id>/`; an older one-file-per-notebook layout is moved over without losing entries.

### Changed
- **Interface redesign** (docs/design-ui.md). Every action of the plugin is now described once in an action registry (`src/ui/actions.ts`) and the interface is built from it and from the context:
  - **Toolbar**: tools in the first row; undo/redo, the current pen (opens the tool options), the favourite pens, up to six **quick actions** (ranked by priority and by your own use on this device), the page indicator and the save state in the second; one row on wide views.
  - **Contextual bar** at the bottom of the page: the selection's actions (copy, cut, duplicate, paste, white → transparent, save as stamp, delete, colour and width), paste for the lasso, the PDF page's actions (search, hide annotations, dim, margins, side by side), the side-by-side pane's actions, and the lecture actions while a class of the timetable is on. It can be collapsed.
  - The long "…" menu and the page menu are replaced by **sheets**: *All actions* (⊞, grouped sections with search and a *Recent* section) and the **page sheet** (tap the page indicator or a thumbnail's menu) with paper and size as chips. On a phone they are bottom sheets.
  - **Radial dial** at the pen tip, redesigned: undo/redo, a width bubble, a colour bubble, the active tool drawn as a pen, and favourites · pen case · shapes. The colour ring has 12 colours, a custom colour and an eyedropper; the width bubble opens an arc slider; the pen case shows the tools with a stroke preview and "+" to save a favourite.
  - A visual system built on Obsidian's CSS variables (surfaces, elevation, radii, spacing, motion with *reduced motion* respected) applied to bars, popovers, sheets, panels, dialogs and the library.
  - Commands are generated from the registry; their ids are unchanged, so hotkeys keep working. New command *Tool: space*. Use counts are stored per device in Obsidian's local storage, never in the vault.
  - Wave 0.7 actions are in the registry too: tapping the page indicator opens the page sheet, whose first section *Go to* has *Go to page number…*, *Recently edited pages* and *One page at a time* (no more "…" toggle); *Split page* for a heavy page; *Check sync safety* and *Check notebooks integrity* in the sheet's *Notebooks* section; the status banners keep their own buttons.
  - No change to the document format, the writing engine or saving. Not yet verified on Android hardware (see `docs/testing.md`, steps U1-U12).

### Added
- **Format v7: bookmarks, outline, page tags, synced position, new papers** (manual steps 64-67)
  - **Format v7**: optional `meta.bookmarkInfo`, page `tags`, document `position` and the new paper kinds, with a v6 → v7 migration. Files saved with it open read-only (with a backup) in 0.5.x. Duplicate page, page restore, conflicted-copy merge, repair and library edits keep the new fields.
  - **Outline with coloured bookmarks** (#15, #85): the Pages panel has an outline mode listing the bookmarked pages in page order with a title, a colour and a category (*Important*, *To review* or a custom one), a category filter and a button to bookmark the page in view; tap a row to jump, its menu edits (rename, recolour, category) or deletes the bookmark. The page menu has *Edit bookmark…*. Thumbnails show the bookmark title and a star in its colour. Every change is one undo step.
  - **Page tags** (#119): page menu *Page tags…* (with the tags already used in the notebook as chips); the Pages panel filters by tag; the library search finds page tags and opens the notebook at the tagged page.
  - **Last position shared across devices** (#125): the notebook stores the page and height where it was last read, so another device opens it there. It is written only together with a content save (never on its own, so reading or scrolling does not touch the file and two devices that only read never fight). A link or search hit still opens its page; a restored workspace keeps this device's page unless another device was in the notebook later; *Continue last notebook* keeps this device's page.
  - **More papers** (#73): Cornell, music staff, isometric, hexagonal, calligraphy (slanted guides), daily planner and weekly planner (2 × 4 boxes, 4 × 2 in landscape), in the new-notebook dialog, the default-paper setting, the page menu, thumbnails and PDF/SVG export. Fine lines fade out when zoomed out as on the other papers.
- **Gestures** (manual steps 68-73)
  - **Finger taps** (roadmap 3): a tap with two fingers undoes, with three fingers redoes (setting *Finger taps*: two undo / three redo, swapped, or off; on by default). A tap needs all fingers to land within 150 ms, move at most 12 px, lift within 150 ms and finish within 350 ms, so panning and pinching never trigger it; touches while the pen is near (palm rejection time), palm-sized or edge-zone contacts, and a pen touching in the middle spoil it. The few pixels the view may have moved are put back, and a tap leaves no ink. A finger that lands among fingers that are already panning or pinching no longer starts a stroke (also with *Draw with finger*).
  - **Scribble to erase** (roadmap 4, setting, off by default): a fast back-and-forth scratch with the pen over existing ink deletes the strokes it crosses; the scribble is not kept and the whole thing is one undo step. Detector in `src/ink/scribble.ts`: at least four direction reversals between legs of 12 pt or more, roughly parallel, mostly piled up instead of travelling along a line (so "mmm" and sweeping zig-zag shading do not count), under 300 ms per leg on average; a stroke is deleted when at least two different legs cross it, and very large strokes (rulings, frames) are left alone. Off by default because a false positive deletes ink.
  - **Auto-scroll while writing** (roadmap 36, setting, off by default): when a stroke ends in the bottom 18 % of the view, the page scrolls up smoothly (320 ms, ease-out) after a 700 ms pause so the tip sits at 40 % of the height. It never moves while a pen or finger is down; a touch cancels it; it stops at the end of the document.
  - **Pen side button** (roadmap 2): the setting *Pen side button* gains *Lasso while held* and *Previous tool while held* (the tool used before the current one; the current tool if there is none). The tool is chosen when the pen touches down. These options are part of the unified side-button setting of 0.5.2 (key `penSideButton`, default *Radial menu on click, erase while held*); the earlier development key `penButtonAction` is migrated (`lasso` stays, `lastTool` becomes `previous`). A selection made with the lasso option stays after the button is released and is cleared when you write.
- **PDF reading** (manual steps 74-78)
  - **Search the PDF text** (#44): command *Search PDF text* and an entry in the toolbar's "…" menu open a search bar over the page. Matches ignore case, accents and ligatures ("caffe" finds "caffè"); at least 2 characters. Results are listed with their notebook page and the words around them, every match is marked on the page (yellow, the current one orange; a transient overlay that is never saved), and next/previous (also the commands *Next/Previous PDF search result*, Enter and Shift+Enter) scroll to it. The text of each PDF page is read once and cached; the search starts at the page in view, handles four pages at a time and gives the screen back every few milliseconds, so typing, scrolling and writing stay responsive on a big PDF, and a new query cancels the old one. At most 500 matches are listed. Marks inside a long line use the character widths of the PDF's font as the WebView measures them.
  - **Highlighter that snaps to the text lines** (#43): the highlighter's options have *Snap to text lines* (off by default). When on, a highlighter stroke over PDF text is replaced at pen-up by straight highlighter strokes, one per line of text it crossed, covering the words it covers (a word counts when at least 40% of it is under the stroke) and as high as the line. They are ordinary highlighter strokes (vector, page coordinates) in the same undo step; a stroke that is not near any text, or made before the page's text was read, stays as drawn. Rotated text is not snapped.
  - **Show or hide all annotations** (#188): command *Show or hide annotations on PDF pages* and a "…" menu entry hide the ink and text boxes on PDF pages (other pages keep theirs) to reread the clean PDF. View only: nothing is saved or changed, editing and the toolbar are off while hidden (a finger still scrolls and zooms), and a banner says so and has the button that shows everything again. Exports, thumbnails and embeds still contain the annotations.
  - **Dim the background** (#199): command *Dim PDF and image backgrounds* and a "…" menu entry with a strength slider (10-80%) draw PDF and image backgrounds lighter so the ink stands out. Kept in the plugin settings (`dimBackground`, `dimStrength`), per device. Exports, thumbnails and embeds are not dimmed (they show the notebook, not one reader's screen); there is no export option for it yet.
- **Navigation and huge notebooks** (manual steps 79-81)
  - **One page at a time** (#14): setting *Scrolling* (*Continuous (vertical)* or *One page at a time*), a toggle *One page at a time* in the toolbar "…" menu and the command *Toggle one page at a time*. In that mode the pages sit side by side and each one is fitted to the view width; a sideways finger swipe (one or two fingers, or a trackpad / Shift+wheel) drags the page along and turns to the next or previous one when it went past about a fifth of the width or was flicked, then the page snaps into place with a short animation (none with reduced motion). A vertical drag scrolls inside a tall page; zoomed in, you pan inside the page and a long push past its edge turns it. Pinch zoom still works. The pen never turns the page (it draws), and the fling after a swipe does not move the page. *Next page* / *Previous page* (commands) animate; the page panel jumps. No change to the file format, the writing pipeline or the ink.
  - **Reopen where you left off** (O16): the workspace state now also keeps the zoom and the scroll position (relative to the fit-to-width zoom and to the page, so they survive rotation or a different pane size), and *Continue last notebook* restores them too (stored in the plugin settings, written at most every few seconds when only the position changed). Nothing is written into the notebook file. A link to a page (`#p3`) still opens that page fitted.
  - **Progressive loading of large notebooks** (#329): a notebook over about 2 MB opens with the page you want shown first, read-only, while the other pages are read and validated in short slices (about 10 ms, nearest pages first) that leave input and painting free. Editing and saving start only when every page has been read and has passed the same validation as before; if a page turns out to be damaged the notebook is treated as an unreadable file (backup, never overwritten). Notebooks with edits in the instant-save journal are loaded in full first, as before. On a PC (`npm run bench:open`, headless Chrome, in-memory vault, not Android): 10,000 strokes on 10 pages, first painted page 207 ms → 150 ms; 100,000 strokes on 100 pages, 1771 ms → 1019 ms. Time until the notebook is complete and editable is about the same or a little longer (10,000: 145 ms plus the paint → 221 ms; 100,000: 1702 ms → 1922 ms) because the first paint now happens inside that window.
- **Library trash, page trash and templates** (manual steps 82-91)
  - **Library trash** (#160): deleting a notebook or a folder (now also a non-empty one) from the library moves it to `<notebooks folder>/_trash/` and remembers its original path. *Trash* in the library sidebar lists the items; *Restore* puts an item back where it was, or under `Name 2` if the name is taken; *Delete permanently* and *Empty trash* ask first (permanent deletion follows Obsidian's *Deleted files* setting). New setting *Empty the trash automatically* (days, default off). The trash is invisible to the library, search, companion notes and conflicted-copy detection; assets are never deleted, so those used only by trashed notebooks stay.
  - **Page trash** (#26): *Delete page* keeps the page as its own file `_trash/pages/<notebook id>/<entry id>.json`, written once and never rewritten (not in the notebook format; assets by id). Page menu *Deleted pages...* lists them with thumbnails and restores one after the page that preceded it as one undo step. At most 50 pages per notebook, 30 days.
  - **Page templates** (#74): page menu *Save page as template...* (content, paper, size, background) stores a small notebook file in the templates folder (setting *Templates folder*, default `_templates` in the notebooks folder); *New page from template...* and a *Template* row in the *New notebook* dialog open a gallery with thumbnails, including built-in Cornell, meeting notes and to-do templates.
- **Lecture pack and exercise page** (manual steps 92-99; no change to the document format or to the writing engine (sampling, wet ink, geometry, stabilizer))
  - **New lecture from slides…** (#191; command, library button and folder menu): choose the course slides PDF (vault or device), then the layout, a header (page, text or none), the course and the date. The lecture notebook is created in the folder and opened, with the slides as page backgrounds; the PDF is stored once like any import.
  - **Slide + notes layout** (#192): one slide per page with a ruled (or other) notes area on the right or below the slide, made with page margins, so there is no format change. The slide is scaled to a fixed size so handwriting has the same scale in every course. Two or three slides per page are not possible yet: they need a format change (documented in `docs/architecture.md`).
  - **Notes page after every slide** (#193): optional blank page of the same size and the paper of your choice after each slide.
  - **Exercise page here** (page menu, toolbar … menu, command): one tap inserts a blank page with your exercise paper (grid by default, changeable with *Exercise page paper…*) right after the current page, the same size as that page; one undo step.
  - The same layout options appear when you insert a PDF into an existing notebook (page menu → *Insert PDF*); the last choice is remembered on the device.
- **Timetable** (manual steps 100-105)
  - **Class timetable** (roadmap 115). Define your weekly classes (course, weekday, start and end time, room, a linked notebook or course folder, alternating odd/even weeks, term start and end dates) from *Settings, Class timetable, Edit timetable* or the command **Edit class timetable**. Stored in the plain JSON file `<notebooks folder>/_timetable.json` (human-editable, syncs like any file; an unreadable file is never overwritten, a copy goes to `_backup/` before it is replaced). Times are local; overlapping classes are flagged in the editor.
    - Opening Obsidian or the library during a class (or up to a few minutes before, setting *Count a class as started this many minutes early*) shows a notice with **Open lecture page**; setting *Open it without asking* does it by itself. The class's notebook opens with a new lecture page (header: course and date, as in New lecture); if that page already exists it is shown instead. With no linked notebook, the one named like the course is used, else a new one is created. Command **Open the current class** does the same on demand.
    - **New lecture** prefills the course from the class in progress (the one linked to the open notebook, else one linked to nothing).
    - The library shows a **Now: <course>** shortcut while a class is in progress.
    - Lecture pages made with a course from the timetable (New lecture prefill, *Open the current class*) get the course as a page tag (v7 page tags), in the same undo step as the new page.
- **PDF ↔ notes view** (manual steps 106-113)
  - **Synchronised PDF and notes view** (#106): command **Open PDF side by side** (also in the "…" page menu) shows a PDF beside the notes: a plain `.pdf` of the vault (read-only, rendered with pdf.js, not copied into the notebook) or another notebook whose pages have PDF backgrounds (its pages with your ink on them). The split is inside the notebook view, not a second Obsidian leaf, because Obsidian on phones and tablets has no split panes in the main area: side by side when the view is wide and landscape, stacked otherwise, with a draggable divider. Turning the PDF page shows the notes page linked to it; scrolling the notes shows the PDF page linked to the notes page in view (toggle **Follow**). A slide without a notes page shows **Add notes page for this slide**: it inserts a page in slide order as one undo step and links it. Also **Link** / **Unlink** for existing pages, commands *Close PDF side by side*, *Link this page to the slide in view*, *Add notes page for this slide*, and the menu entry *Show the slide of this page*. The links are stored in a vault file, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/pdf-links.json`, written through the Vault API (keyed by notebook id and page id, and by the PDF's content id and page number, so renaming the PDF does not break them), written only by these explicit actions and never while scrolling; the notebook format is unchanged. A links file that cannot be read is never overwritten. The pane shows one PDF page at a time and is not remembered across restarts.
- **Stamps and ink on objects** (manual steps 114-123; no change to the notebook format)
  - **Ink attached to objects** (#149): ink written over an image or a text box moves, scales and rotates with it. When the object is moved, scaled or rotated with the lasso (or its handles), the strokes with at least 60% of their points inside the object's box go with it, in the same undo step, and are shown moving during the drag. The link is geometric (nothing is stored in the file); where objects overlap, ink belongs to the topmost one. New setting *Ink follows objects* (on by default). Deleting an object that has ink on it shows a notice with *Delete the ink too* (its own undo step); ignoring it keeps the ink. Logic in `src/ink/attach.ts`.
  - **Personal stamps** (#142): *Stamps* in the toolbar's more menu opens a palette with three built-in stamps (checkmark, star, arrow, drawn with the current pen colour and width) and your own. *Save selection as stamp* stores the lasso selection (strokes, text boxes and images) as `<notebooks folder>/_stamps/<name>.stamp`, a vault file in the notebook document format (one page), so stamps sync with the vault and not with the plugin settings; images are referenced by their content-addressed asset, as in a notebook. Tap a stamp to put it in the middle of the page in view, or drag it onto the page; placed stamps get new ids, are selected so they can be moved at once, and are one undo step. Stamps can be renamed and deleted (to the trash, after a confirmation); a stamp file that cannot be read is listed as unreadable and never rewritten. Logic in `src/ink/stamps.ts`, vault access in `src/storage/stamps.ts`.
- **Zettelkasten** (roadmap M15 Z1, Z2, Z3, Z11, Z12; manual steps 124-128). No change to the document format: card data lives only in the card notes.
  - **Cards** (Z1): *Make card from selection* (lasso) and *Make card from this page* (commands and the notebook tab's ⋯ menu) create a Markdown note in the card folder (setting, default `Zettelkasten/`) with `zettel-id` (timestamp `202610291442` or Luhmann numbering `1`, `1a`, `1a1`, setting *Card ids*), `title`, `aliases`, `type`, `source`, `date`, `tags`, `notebook`, `notebook-id`, `page-id` and `region`, and the ink embedded live (editing the notebook updates the card).
  - **Card subpath** for links and embeds: `#p3&id=<page id>&r=x,y,w,h` (page units). `id` finds the page after it was moved and never shows another page if it was deleted; `r` crops the preview to the region. Plain `#p3` links are unchanged.
  - **Slip-box** (Z2): *New slip-box* (A6 card pages) and *Make this notebook a slip-box*: every page is a card, and a page added on this device gets its note automatically a few seconds after it is saved.
  - **Drawn links** (Z3): an arrow drawn with the shape tool from one card region to another adds a link to each card's `links` property (two-way, shown in the graph); *Link cards joined by arrows on this page* does it for arrows drawn earlier.
  - **Title and transcription** (Z11): typed title in the *New card* dialog or later with *Set card title* (title, alias, and the note renamed from `<id>` to `<id> <title>`); every card note has a *Transcription* section.
  - **Card regions** on the page: dashed outline and id badge, never taking pointer events; setting *Show card regions* and command *Show or hide card regions*.
  - **Orphans**: cards are never deleted; when their page is deleted they get `orphaned: true` (removed again if the page comes back) and are listed by *Show orphaned cards* and *Show cards of this notebook*.
- **Shared tags and public API** (manual steps 129-134)
  - **Shared tags (O2)**: the tag editor of the library (*Edit tags…*) and the new command *Edit tags of this notebook…* suggest the tags that already exist in your vault (the Tags pane), write tags the way Obsidian does (no `#`, spaces become `-`, `a/b` is a nested tag; a tag of only digits is refused with a message) and show the chosen tags as chips. With companion notes off the dialog says that Obsidian's Tags pane and tag search need them and offers *Turn on companion notes*. The companion note is refreshed 2 s after saving tags.
  - **Library filter by tag follows nesting**: choosing `physics` also lists notebooks tagged `physics/optics`, and a nested tag counts for its parents in the sidebar, as in Obsidian's Tags pane.
  - **Public API for other plugins (#168)**: `app.plugins.plugins["notebook"].api` with `version`, `createNotebook`, `addPages`, `insertImage`, `exportPdf`, `getNotebookInfo`, `openNotebook` and `listNotebooks`, typed `NotebookApiError`s and checked arguments. Open notebooks are changed through their own session (one undo step, normal save); closed ones are rewritten only after validation, with a `before-api` backup and a read-back check. Documented, with TypeScript typings and Templater, QuickAdd and Dataview examples, in `docs/api.md`. No change to the document format.

### Changed
- **Companion notes: which tags the plugin may remove.** The tags of the notebook that the note already had (written by you) are no longer treated as the plugin's: removing such a tag from the notebook leaves it in the note. Only tags the plugin added itself are removed when the notebook drops them. Notes written by 0.5.0 keep their old behaviour until their next update.
- Tags typed in the editor are stored normalised (`linear algebra` becomes `linear-algebra`); tags already stored are kept as written, also when you edit the tags.

### Fixed
- Conflicted-copy merge combines bookmark details (title, colour, category) field by field: the main notebook's value of each field wins, missing fields are taken from the copy, differing fields are counted (`bookmarkInfoDiffer`), and the copy is moved away only when every one of its fields is in the result.
- The new paper kinds stay bounded at extreme densities (calligraphy or hexagons at 0.5–1 pt, A0 or infinite pages): at most 30,000 segments per page, falling back to the major lines or to a coarser lattice, so exports are never blank and never freeze.
- Reading position across devices: the workspace layout now records when the page in view last changed, not when the layout was saved, so a notebook left open no longer wins over a position another device stored later.
- *Move to notebook…* keeps the moved pages' bookmarks and their details in the target (under the pages' new ids, in the same write); page tags travel with the page.
- A bad bookmark colour or detail (even of a deleted page) or an invalid reading position no longer makes the whole notebook unreadable: the invalid value is dropped on read and counted, a `dropped-details` backup of the file is kept before it is rewritten, and what is written is still checked strictly.
- The outline's category filter no longer lists categories of bookmarks left on deleted pages.
- The library index of a newer plugin version (synced plugin folder) is ignored and left as it is instead of being overwritten.
- Zettel cards use their own `zettel-notebook` / `zettel-notebook-id` properties instead of the companion note's `notebook` / `notebook-id`, so a device still on 0.5.1 with companion notes on no longer takes a synced card for a companion note and writes into it (cards with the old keys are still read).
- The tag editor no longer drops or renames tags the notebook already has (`2026` removed, `C++` turned into `C`): they are shown as written, flagged when Obsidian would not accept them, and only the tags you remove go; only newly typed tags are normalised.
- Card notes are written only after this device's own saves: a page deleted on another device no longer makes every device write `orphaned` to the same card (conflicted copies with Google Drive or OneDrive). Card frontmatter edits now rewrite only the changed keys' lines, so YAML comments, quoting and your own properties stay as written.
- Slip-boxes create cards only for pages made on this device in this session, so a page that arrives by sync before its card no longer gets a second one; timestamp card ids now carry seconds and a short random suffix, and untitled Luhmann cards a suffix in their file name, so two devices never create the same card file.
- Following a page link with a page id (`#p3&id=…`) can now be resolved by id first (`resolvePageTarget`), reporting a deleted page instead of opening page 3; the notebook view does not use it yet.
- Companion note updates keep `tags` entries they do not read, such as an unquoted `2026` (`tags: [2026, physics]` no longer loses 2026).
- Page trash: every deleted page is now its own file (`_trash/pages/<notebook id>/<entry id>.json`), written once and never rewritten, so two devices deleting pages before they sync no longer lose one of the copies; conflicted copies are read, and the old one-file layout is moved over without losing entries.
- Page trash: a deleted page keeps its bookmark (v7 title, colour and category), and restoring it brings the bookmark back in the same undo step.
- Page trash: a restored page is discarded from *Deleted pages* once the notebook is saved, so it can no longer be restored twice; *Delete page* now waits for the copy and does not delete the page if the copy fails.
- Library: moving a notebook (or a folder with open notebooks) to the trash waits for the open tab's final save instead of a fixed 50 ms, and is refused if the changes cannot be saved.
- Library: deleting a folder that holds other files (.md, .pdf, images) lists them and asks whether to move only the notebooks or everything; such a folder is never emptied from the trash automatically, and the delete and empty confirmations name those files.
- Timetable: saving never overwrites a file that changed since the editor opened it (for example synced from another device), not even after *Start a new timetable*; the draft is copied to the backup folder instead, and identical saves write nothing.
- PDF links: the links file is now a vault file, `<notebooks folder>/_meta/pdf-links.json`, written through the Vault API; the file in the plugin's config folder is copied over once and left untouched.
- API: an edit to an open notebook while a finger, the pen or a gesture (for example a lasso drag) is on the page now waits and reports `busy` instead of dropping the move.
- API: `listNotebooks` and the other calls no longer see notebooks in the plugin trash, the templates folder or the other reserved folders.
- Pen side button *Previous tool while held*: with no previous tool it keeps the current tool instead of erasing.
- Scribble to erase: one Undo after a false positive now gives back both the erased strokes and the stroke you drew.

### Development
- The browser harness gets a scenario for each of the three navigation features (one page at a time, reopening where you left off, progressive loading); `npm run harness:check` runs 24 scenarios.
- Tests: the repair fuzz test is reproducible (seeded ids) and has a longer timeout.
- Final run of 0.6.0 on the development PC: `npm test` 1655 tests passed, lint 0 errors, build ok (see `docs/testing.md`).

## 0.5.2 — 2026-10-30

Hotfix for 0.5.1. Not verified on Android hardware (see `docs/testing.md`). What the S Pen really sends was observed by the user on a Samsung tablet with the Pen button test and Chrome's pen-tracker page; the chorded press during contact (item 3 of *Fixed*) is a hypothesis from one observation and may not hold.

### Fixed
- **The radial menu could not be reached on a Samsung tablet.** With Air command off, the Pen button test saw no event at all when the S Pen side button was pressed *on the page it was tested in*, while Chrome on the same tablet shows the button as a primary press with zero pressure. The menu is now reachable in four ways:
  1. **Side button pressed while the pen hovers** (Samsung / Chromium): `pointerdown` with `button 0`, `buttons 1` and `pressure` exactly 0 (a real nib contact has pressure above 0), also `buttons` going 0 to 1 in a pen `pointermove` / `pointerrawupdate` with pressure 0. It only counts for a pointer that was hovering just before (`buttons 0` seen for the same `pointerId`), never draws and never erases from the pressure-0 phase, and can be switched off (*A zero-pressure press is the side button*). Press the button *before* touching the page: pressed after the nib is down, `buttons` is already 1 and it cannot be told apart.
  2. **Hold the pen still** on the page (see *Changed*).
  3. **Side button pressed while the nib touches** (experimental, setting *Side button pressed while writing*): a dispatched pen `pointermove` / `pointerrawupdate` with `button 0` (instead of -1) while `buttons` stays 1 finishes the stroke so far (kept, never discarded) and the contact then acts as the button's tool until the next such event or the lift. Never from `pointerdown`, never from coalesced or predicted events, only after an ordinary move was seen in the contact, not within 40 ms of the landing, debounced 120 ms; a device whose moves all carry `button 0` never triggers it.
  4. **Finger long-press** (see *Added*).
- The side-button signals that other devices may deliver (`buttons & 2`, `contextmenu`, `auxclick`, a mouse right button after pen activity) are kept.
- Honest texts (English and Italian): the *Pen side button* setting says the button works on Samsung only with the pen hovering, that it may never reach Obsidian on some devices, and names the two alternatives; the Pen button test shows *No side-button signal received yet on this device* until one arrives.

### Changed
- **Pen side button** default is now **Radial menu on click, erase while held**: with the pen hovering, press and release the button to open the menu on the release (at the pen position); press the button and then touch the page to erase (current eraser mode, size and filter) for as long as the contact lasts in that button session; no menu then. New options: *Lasso while held* and *Previous tool while held* (the others: radial menu only, erase while held, nothing). The setting is stored under a new key (`penSideButton`): the 0.5.x default "radial" is not carried over, an explicit "erase while held" or "nothing" is.
- **Open the radial menu by holding the pen still** is more robust and **on by default only while the device has never delivered a side-button signal** (the app remembers the first signal in the settings: `penButtonSeen`). Once one has arrived the default is off; an explicit choice always wins. The pen must be still from the very first contact: 700 ms (was 600 ms) within 6 px of where it landed. Once the stroke has moved it can never open the menu, however long it then rests, so dots, slow strokes and pauses in a stroke are safe. The discarded stroke leaves no dot and nothing for undo, and the pen is not left "down" for palm rejection afterwards. Never for the eraser end; needs the side button action to open the radial menu.
- The Pen button test shows the event type and the pointer id on every line, labels the pressure-0 press (*side button (pressure 0 primary press)*), the touch while it is held, the lift and the release (with *click: opens the menu*), and `pointermove` with `button 0` during contact as *possible side button*. A pressure-0 press counts as a side-button signal and turns the hold-still default off.

### Added
- **Finger long-press opens the radial menu** at the finger (setting *Open the radial menu with a finger long-press*, on by default): a finger still for 500 ms within 10 px, only while finger drawing is off (pen-only), only a single finger, and not when the palm-rejection window says a pen is near. The ring opens beside the finger, not under it, so the fingertip does not cover the sectors. A pan, a pinch or a two-finger tap that starts before 500 ms is not affected.

## 0.5.1 — 2026-10-30

Hotfix for 0.5.0. Not verified on Android hardware (see `docs/testing.md`); how the S Pen button is reported is the open question.

### Fixed
- **S Pen side button did nothing** on a Galaxy tablet. Detection only looked at a pen `pointerdown` / `pointermove` with `button 2` / `buttons & 2`, and the page prevented `contextmenu` instead of using it. The radial menu now opens from every plausible signal, once per press: pen `pointerdown` with the button, `buttons & 2` turning on in `pointermove` / `pointerrawupdate` (hovering or touching), a `contextmenu` or `auxclick` (button 2) that comes from the pen (never a touch long-press, and never a bare pen long-press on the page), and a mouse right button right after pen activity. A press that lands just after the nib (a stroke younger than 350 ms that has not moved) discards that stroke, so no dot is left; a button brushed during real writing is ignored. The eraser (`buttons & 32`, `button 5`) is never taken for the side button. The stroke pipeline is unchanged.
- On Samsung tablets the system **Air command** takes the button while the pen hovers; the setting text and the new test now say how to switch it off (the name and place may differ by One UI version).

### Added
- **Pen button test**: a button in the plugin settings and the command *Pen button test* open a test area that lists, live, the last 30 events on it (pointerdown / move / up / rawupdate / cancel, contextmenu, auxclick, keydown / keyup) with pointer type, `button`, `buttons`, pressure, primary flag and whether the plugin would treat the event as the side button. It draws nothing. *Copy log* copies the text (clipboard, with the text selected as a fallback). Use it to report what the S Pen sends.
- Setting **Open the radial menu by holding the pen still** (off by default): a fallback that does not need the button. Holding the nib on the page for 0.6 s within 6 px opens the same menu and discards the stroke, so no dot remains; any movement cancels it.

## 0.5.0 — 2026-10-29

Not yet verified on Android hardware (see `docs/testing.md`).

### Added
- **Writing tools**
  - **Insert or remove space** (tool *Insert or remove space*): touch a line and drag down to insert space or up to remove it. Everything below the line moves; what no longer fits on its page continues at the top of the next one, and pages are appended when needed (same size and paper as the last page); removing pulls the following content back up and never takes away more than the empty gap, so ink cannot overlap. An infinite page grows instead of overflowing. PDF and image pages are never mixed with their neighbours: nothing moves onto or off such a page, overflow goes to a new blank page next to it. While dragging, the content below the line is shown shifted by the amount that will really be applied. Text boxes and images move with the ink. One undo step.
  - **Automatic next page** (setting *Add a page when writing at the bottom*, on by default): ink that reaches the bottom tenth of the last page appends a page with the same size and paper, in the same undo step as the stroke. Not for infinite pages or for pages with a PDF or image background.
  - **Selective eraser**: the eraser can be limited to *All ink*, *Highlighter only*, *Pen only* (pen and shapes) or *One colour* (swatches). Works with *Whole stroke*, *Segment* and *Area*.
  - **Erase area** (eraser mode *Area*): draw an outline and everything inside disappears in one gesture and one undo step; strokes crossing the outline are cut at it. Setting *Erase area also removes text and images* (off by default).
  - **Favourite pens**: six slots (tool, colour, width) next to the tools, one tap to select, long press or context menu to save the current pen into a slot (or reset it). Stored in the plugin settings.
  - **Eraser size**: five presets in the toolbar for both eraser modes, remembered, and a circle that follows the pen (or mouse) while erasing.
  - **Commands for hotkeys**: every tool and action as a command that only applies when a notebook is active (pen, highlighter, eraser and its mode and size, lasso, shapes, text, favourite pens 1-6, pen only, next/previous page, delete selection, pages panel, ruler). No default hotkeys.
- **Pen and input**
  - **Pen only** toggle in the toolbar: fingers pan and zoom but do not draw. It is the inverse of the *Draw with finger* setting and stays in sync with it.
  - **Edge rejection zones** (#151): settings *Edge rejection zones* (off by default), *Edge zone width* (4-80 px) and one switch per edge (left, right, bottom on; top off). A touch that starts within that distance of an enabled edge of the page area is ignored: it neither draws nor scrolls, so a palm resting on the edge does nothing. The pen, the mouse and a touch that started inside the area are never affected; the zone never takes more than a third of the shorter side.
  - **Radial menu at the pen tip** (#152): pressing the pen's side button opens a ring around the nib with pen, highlighter, eraser, lasso, undo and the last three pen colours. Lift the pen (or tap) on a sector to choose it; releasing the button over a sector also chooses it; tapping elsewhere or Escape closes it. No stroke is drawn. New setting *Pen side button*: radial menu (default), erase while held, or nothing. The eraser end and the eraser button (`buttons & 32`, `button 5`) keep erasing as before. Whether the S Pen reports its side button as `button 2` / `buttons & 2` has not been checked on a device.
  - **Instant start** (#271): the page being read is saved with the workspace layout, so reopening Obsidian (or going back/forward) shows the same page; new command **Continue last notebook** opens the notebook that was open last on the page that was in view (stored in the plugin settings, never in the notebook file).
- **Selection and clipboard**
  - **Selection actions bar** (lasso tool): when something is selected, a bar over the bottom of the page offers Copy, Cut, Duplicate and Paste, the colour and width rows, and (for images) Make white transparent; with the lasso active and nothing selected it offers Paste. Every button works; none of this changes the file format.
  - **Restyle selection** (roadmap 5): change the colour and width of the lasso-selected strokes, and the colour of selected text boxes, as one undo step. Pens and shapes use the pen palettes; a highlighter keeps the highlighter palette and widths (in a mixed selection each group has its own row and the other group is left alone).
  - **Copy, cut, paste and duplicate** (roadmap 6): strokes and elements with their images, across pages and across notebooks. The plugin clipboard lives for the whole session, so it survives switching notebooks; pasting into another notebook stores the needed images again in its `_assets/` folder (content-addressed, so nothing is duplicated). Each paste gets new ids, lands in the middle of the view (a little further each time) and is selected so it can be moved at once. Ctrl/Cmd+C, X and V work too. Copying also writes a short marker text such as `[Notebook selection: 3 strokes]` to the system clipboard, which is how a later paste knows whether something newer was copied elsewhere. A selection still belongs to one page; paste goes to the page in view.
  - **Paste from the system clipboard** (roadmap 114): a picture becomes an image element (same sizing and HEIF/HEIC message as Insert image), plain text becomes a text box (line breaks kept, at most 20,000 characters). Uses `navigator.clipboard` when the WebView allows it; where it does not (Android WebViews often refuse), Paste opens a small box in which you press and hold and choose the system Paste. Not verified on Android.
  - **White to transparent** (roadmap 172): the selection bar's *Make white transparent* stores a processed copy of each selected image (a PNG, at most 2048 px on the long side) as a new asset and points the element at it; undo points it back. No new fields in the file. It is a fixed threshold (near-white pixels, with a short ramp at the edges): it suits screenshots and drawings on white, not photos with grey paper. PDF and SVG exports draw images on white, so a transparent image shows a white box there over a coloured page or a PDF background.
  - **Words and lines without OCR (#255).** With the lasso tool, a **double tap** on handwriting selects the word under it and a **triple tap** the whole line; the result is the normal lasso selection (move, scale, rotate, delete). Words and lines are found geometrically from the strokes (`src/ink/words.ts`); highlighter and shape strokes and oversized strokes (frames, long underlines) are ignored. Taps do nothing to the document: no strokes or dots are created. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **PDF**
  - **Expandable margins on PDF pages** (#104): page menu *Page margins…* (or the command) widens a PDF or scanned page to the left, right and bottom to write beside the slide without covering it; optionally for every page of the same PDF, one undo step. The PDF keeps its place and size and the ink moves with it; an infinite page with margins grows below the PDF instead of stretching it. Exports and thumbnails include the margins.
  - **Re-published slides** (#235): *Update PDF from a new version…* (page menu or command) recognises the slides already in the notebook by their content (pdf.js text; a small image hash for slides with little text), not by number. New pages are appended after the last pages of that PDF (with the same margins), notes stay on their slides even when the order changed, changed slides are listed as "updated" and can be switched to the new version keeping the notes or kept as they are. A summary (new, updated, unchanged, missing) is shown first; the whole update is one undo step; old PDFs stay in `_assets/`.
  - **Export PDF annotations to Markdown** (#155): highlights on PDF pages (with the PDF text under them when the PDF has a text layer) and text boxes, page by page, each with a link that opens the notebook at its page, in a new note next to the notebook (never overwrites an existing note).
- **Lecture**
  - **Instant board shot** (toolbar camera button, command): one tap opens the in-app camera; after the shutter the photo is cropped automatically (the scanner's detection; the whole photo if no outline is found), gets the whiteboard filter and is placed as an image on the page in view, under the last ink, with a small "Board HH:MM" text label. On a page with no room it is scaled down, an infinite page grows, and as a last resort it is centred over the content with a notice. One undo step. Not verified on Android hardware.
  - **New lecture** (command, page menu): a small dialog asks for the course or title (default: the notebook name) and the date; a page like the last one is added at the end with the header as a text box, in one undo step. The format has no page titles, so the header is ordinary text.
  - **Loose sheet** (command, ribbon icon, library button): a quick-notes notebook opened from anywhere, created on first use in the notebooks folder or at the path set in the new setting *Loose sheet location*; an existing file is never replaced.
  - **Move to notebook…** (page menu, command): pick pages and a destination notebook. The destination is saved first (an open notebook: one undo step; a closed file: validated, a backup kept, read back), and only then do the pages leave the source (one undo step); pages edited meanwhile stay. Assets are shared by id, so only their records are copied.
- **Obsidian integration** (not verified in Obsidian or on Android hardware, see `docs/testing.md`, step 37)
  - **Page links (O4)**: `[[Physics.notebook#p3]]` opens the notebook at page 3 (the view reads the link subpath through `setEphemeralState`), also when the notebook is already open. Typing `[[Physics.notebook#` lists the pages with their number and first text box (`EditorSuggest`).
  - **Native embed (O6)**: `![[Physics.notebook#p3]]` renders the same page preview as the `notebook` code block, in reading view and live preview, and tapping it opens the page. It uses Obsidian's embed registry (not part of the public typings, so feature-detected); if it is missing, a reading-view post-processor is used and live preview keeps the plain embed (use the code block there).
  - **File explorer menu (O9)**: *New notebook here* on folders and *Annotate in Notebook* on PDFs (a new notebook in the notebooks folder with the PDF as page backgrounds, reusing the PDF import).
  - **Page into a note (O20)**: the page menu has *Copy link to this page*, *Insert link into the active note* and *Insert page preview into the active note* (links follow Obsidian's link-format setting).
- **Companion notes** (O1, setting *Companion notes*, **off by default**): every notebook gets a Markdown note (next to it, or in a folder that mirrors the notebooks' subfolders; name template with `{name}`) with properties — a link to the notebook, its id, page count, created and modified dates, its tags as Obsidian tags and every field of its metadata — and the text of its text boxes per page with a link that opens each page, so Obsidian search, graph, tags, Dataview and Bases see notebooks. The note may be edited: only the marked generated block and the plugin's own properties are rewritten; your text, properties and tags are kept. Written with a debounce, never while the pen is on the screen, and only when something changed; renamed and moved with the notebook, kept when the notebook is deleted. Commands **Update companion notes of all notebooks** and **Open companion note**. Not yet verified on Android hardware.
- **Data safety**
  - **Repair a damaged notebook** (button in the "cannot be opened" view, command *Repair damaged notebook*): recovers every valid page, stroke, text box, image and file reference from a cut-off or damaged file, also after a damaged region in the middle, and from the journal, version-history snapshots and backup copies; shows what each source recovers and loses and writes the chosen result into a new notebook `<name> (repaired)`. The damaged file is never changed.
  - **Merge conflicted copies** made by Google Drive, OneDrive, Dropbox, Syncthing and Android sync apps (notice when a notebook has one, command *Merge conflicted copies*): only files with a conflict-like name **and** the same notebook id are offered; a summary shows what will be added; everything is added and nothing removed (an item changed on both sides is kept twice); one undo step. The copy is kept, or moved to `_backup/conflicts/` only after the merged notebook was saved and checked.
  - **Restore a single page** from a previous version (page menu, command *Restore this page from a previous version*): pick a snapshot or backup, see its pages, insert the old page as a new page or replace the current one; one undo step, the rest of the notebook untouched.
- **Format**: format v6 with optional page margins (see Changed). Files saved by 0.5.0 with margins open read-only in 0.4.x.

### Changed
- **Format v6**: optional page field `margins`. v5 files are migrated in memory (nothing else changes); once saved by this version with margins, 0.4.x opens them read-only with a backup.
- **Compact toolbar**: the toolbar is two rows (tools; undo/redo, current-pen indicator, favourite pens, page, save state, more) that share one line on a wide view and stack on a phone, with no sideways overflow at 360 px. Tool options (colours, widths, eraser mode, eraser filter and its swatches, eraser size, shape kind, text size) moved into a popover that opens when the active tool is tapped again or from the current-pen indicator; favourite pens are a strip that scrolls sideways; *Insert image*, *Board shot*, *Ruler*, *Pen only*, *Add page*, *Page options* and *Fit to width* are in a "…" menu. Touch targets are at least 40 px. A tap outside a popover only closes it (no stray ink); Escape closes it too. The save state is a coloured dot (text in the tooltip, shown in full on wide views). Tools, settings and the document format are unchanged. Not verified on Android hardware.
- `obsidian://notebook` links can leave out `vault=` (the current vault is used); companion notes use that form.
- Opening a notebook no longer parses it a second time in the middle of the first paint: the version-history snapshot reads the file at once (so it keeps the content as it was on open) but parses and writes it once the page is on screen and the browser is idle. The stroke-line warm-up of the save check also starts 400 ms after the load. On the PC benchmark (10,000 strokes, 6.8 MB) the first painted frame went from 414 ms to 233 ms and the page the reader wants (page 7 of 10) from 519 ms to 233 ms; measured on desktop Chrome only, not on Android. No save or validation step was changed.

### Fixed
- **Move to notebook…**: when the destination is open, the pages leave the source only if the destination's file, read back after the flush, contains them (also when the flush reports unsaved changes because the user kept writing in the destination, so the same pages never stay in both); a destination deleted, turned read-only or closed during the move keeps the source pages, with a notice.
- **Companion notes**: a change that arrived by sync (or from another app) waits 30 s even when the notebook is open here, and opening a notebook waits 30 s too; only this device's own saves use the 4 s delay, so two devices no longer write the same note and cause conflicted copies.
- **Companion notes**: frontmatter and generated region are written in one `vault.process` instead of two writes (user properties and text untouched; broken YAML or markers still leave the note alone).
- **Companion notes**: a file copy of a notebook (same document id) no longer takes over the original's note when that note's link does not resolve; it does so only if no other notebook has the id (read from the top of each file, with a full parse when it is not there).
- **Update PDF from a new version**: another PDF joins "that PDF" only with several unchanged pages (3) or more than half of its slides, so one shared "Thank you" slide no longer pulls in an unrelated lecture; the summary names the PDF of each updated slide and only the main PDF's slides start switched on.
- **Update PDF from a new version**: switching an infinite page that grew beyond the slide keeps its height (the extra goes into the bottom margin when the page has margins), so ink below the slide is not cut off.
- **Conflicted copies**: a copy identical to the notebook (e.g. a browser download named `Notes (1)`) is no longer announced; the merge dialog says that a `Notes (1)` copy may be intentional. Merging stays opt-in.
- **Restore a single page** (*Replace current page*) keeps the v6 margins of the restored page, so its PDF is no longer stretched and its ink stays aligned.
- **Merge conflicted copies** keeps the margins of pages that exist only in the copy; when the other device changed a page's left margin, the copy's ink is aligned to this notebook's margins before comparing, so the same ink is no longer added again as shifted duplicates, and when ink or a text box of the copy would fall off this notebook's page (left, right or bottom, also with equal margins) the page grows in the same undo step so it stays visible (wider margins on a PDF or image page, more height on an infinite page, a larger plain page); the summary reports pages with different margins, and the coverage check before moving the copy away compares content in the page's margin frame.
- **Repair a damaged notebook** keeps page margins (validated like the other page fields, reset when invalid) instead of dropping them as unknown, and no longer loses the strokes of pages with a PDF or image background.
- **Insert or remove space** on an infinite page with margins grows the bottom margin instead of stretching the PDF; undo restores the margins exactly.

### Development
- `npm run bench:open`: open-time benchmark of the real view in headless Chrome (10,000 strokes / 10 pages by default), with the phases timed and an emulation of the work the plugin starts on open.


## 0.4.1 — 2026-10-29

Hotfix for 0.4.0. Not yet verified on Android hardware.

### Fixed
- **Black page, nothing could be written** (reported on a Samsung tablet with S Pen, dark theme): the page area stayed empty — black in the dark theme — and no tool drew, while the toolbar and the page panel worked. Cause: two merges in the 0.4.0 cycle damaged `styles.css` (the "view wave 2" and "integration P1" sections lost their closing braces and many declarations). Browsers with CSS nesting read the rest of the file as nested rules that never match, so the page container was no longer a flex box: the drawing area had zero height, the canvases were never sized and no pointer event reached them. The two sections are restored from their original commits (embed previews and the page panel's hidden/narrow states were affected too).
- One bad stroke, image, PDF background or text box can no longer stop the page from being drawn: every part of a frame is drawn in its own guard, a failure is skipped and logged once in the console (`[notebook] render …`). If painting wet ink fails, the stroke being written is shown on the regular live layer instead, so the pen always leaves ink.
